A page reads the folder listing and the manifests of the runs it returns, whatever the folder holds; the folder is listed while the history index is held, so pages requested at once apply their listings in order (only other pages wait). Runs created or deleted between pages never repeat or hide a run; a record whose modification time changes between pages (rewritten, or deleted and put back as another copy) so that it would be listed twice or not at all makes the continuation invalid (`research.historyChanged`, also for a continuation of another project or an earlier start of the app, or one older than 4,096 logged changes or 16,384 deleted records), and the history is read again from its start (the interface does this itself, to the depth it had loaded). The interface keeps every loaded page across refreshes (delete, restore, resync) and events; a run that the loaded pages do not list (a result's run in the inspector, an agent's figure, a selected run) is read by id (`research_run`), never reported as missing because it is not loaded: "no such run" only when the service says so (asked again after any restore), and a read that fails shows "Could not read this run" with the cause and **Retry**. At most 64 runs read by id are remembered, and their outputs are held under the same bound as those of listed runs. Rust callers: `ResearchService::run_history`, `runs()` (the first page), `run_summary(id)`, `run_records()` (every record, for Storage & History).

| Attach (through a reviewed promotion, `research_promotion_prepare` / `research_promotion_approve`, or the agent tool `insert_artifact`) | Typst `.typ` | LaTeX `.tex`/`.ltx` | Markdown `.md`/`.markdown` |
|---|---|---|---|
| Markers | `// exposition:begin|end <key>` | `% exposition:begin|end <key>` | `<!-- exposition:begin|end <key> -->` |
| Figure | `#figure(image(path, width: 80%), caption) <key>` | `figure`, `\includegraphics[width=0.8\linewidth]`, `\caption`, `\label{key}` | `![cap](path)` |
| Table | `#figure(table(..., table.header(..)), caption) <key>` | `table` + `tabular` with booktabs rules + `\label` | pipe table + `Table: cap` |
| Figure types | png, jpeg, gif, svg | png, jpeg, pdf (SVG refused: emit PNG) | any `image/*` |

- **Insert:** `baseRevision` must be current; offset not inside another block (LaTeX: inside the document body); adds a missing `\usepackage{graphicx|booktabs}` before `\begin{document}` or warns. Key given or derived (`fig-`/`tab-` + 8 hex of the hash; on collision a `-<n>` suffix counting from 2). UI default: end of document, or before `\end{document}`.
- **Update:** exactly one begin + one end marker, same kind; keeps the caption unless a new one is given; replaces the whole block. Artifact sha256 must equal the manifest's; CSV ≤ 4 MiB, UTF-8, ≤ 500 × 50. One undoable transaction (origin `Research{run}`). LaTeX image paths are relative to the file with `\documentclass`, else the project target including it, else the first target (warning), else a scanned main file, else its own folder (warning).
- **Interrupt:** Idle/Busy only. Windows: Python `event`; kernelspec declaring `message` (IJulia's does) `message`; other kernels (IRkernel) `unsupported` + notice to use Cancel. Elsewhere `signal`. **Cancel run:** queued → Canceled at once; active → interrupt, then after 5 s the kernel restarts and the run is Canceled. **Restart:** refused when Detached, Stopped, Starting or Restarting; cancels queued and active runs; failure → Crashed. **Stop:** cancels queued runs, `shutdown` (20 s), waits 5 s, kills the tree.
- **Error codes:** `research.` + `noWorkspace`, `invalidWorkspace`, `notTrusted`, `policyRefused`, `unknownEnvironment`, `environmentUnavailable`, `unknownSession`, `unknownRun`, `unknownArtifact`, `ownerMismatch`, `workspaceMismatch`, `tooManySessions`, `sessionNotReady`, `invalidRequest`, `staleSource`, `historyChanged` (a run-history continuation that no longer describes the listing), `launchFailed`, `timeout` (120 s), `helperGone`, `io`, `helper.<code>`. Helper: `protocol.{invalidRequest, handshakeRequired, unknownCommand, versionMismatch}`, `kernel.{alreadyStarted, invalidCwd, notRunning, dead, ipykernelMissing, invalidSpec, noSuchKernel, startFailed}`, `run.{invalidId, invalidCode, invalidOutputDir, duplicate}`, `helper.internal`, `helper.unknown`. Setup codes (`setup.*`): see **Setup** above.
- **Attach codes:** `attach.` + `unsupportedFormat`, `document`, `stale`, `missingMarkers`, `ambiguousMarkers`, `duplicateKey`, `invalidKey`, `invalidPlacement`, `artifact`, `artifactChanged`, `unsupported`, `invalidTable`, `kindMismatch`.

### 4.1 Research page, research space, and the project graph (M7)

- **Research page** (`research`, Mod+2): explorer over the research space (Sources, Data, Notes, Experiments, Results, Workflows read-only, References; selecting a workflow shows an inspector whose **Open in the workflow editor** opens the tab `workflow:<wf-id>` with the workflow's own id, which `ResearchWorkflowItem.workflow` carries next to the graph `entity` id (the entity's key, else its `workflow.id` attribute; `null` when neither is a workflow id: "No saved definition for this workflow", nothing to open; "Workflow not found" when the listing no longer has it)), Monaco editing of research files in the shared document model (the same model the Editor page uses; closing a research tab asks about unsaved changes unless an Editor tab still shows the file), the shared session/run store (one set of sessions with the bottom Research panel), Run file / Run selection, outputs, and an inspector (provenance, relations, evidence with freshness, impact, entities bound in a file). The Files explorer has a Research subsection that opens the page at an item. The explorer supports multi-selection and the Edit commands (§8): research files are deleted to the Recycle Bin, copied, pasted into Sources, Data, or Notes ("Paste into Data") or beside a file, and duplicated (new identities; nothing is attached, verified, or run); experiments copy to the app clipboard for pasting on the Graph page; read-only references are not duplicated; the group folders, the space itself, and saved workflows are not deleted here.
- **Execution labels:** every interactive session is **Trusted native** (runs with the account's privileges; the research folder and sessions organize work but are not a sandbox). Restricted research execution is the workflow batch step in Python, R, or Julia (§7.7): the Research page's "Restricted research" row lists each language with Available, or the reason it is not, as `workflow_operations` reports for its batch runner in the open project, re-read when images, tool locations, trust, or the project change, and on Refresh environments; nothing falls back to or from it. Computational evidence records the run's actual mode (`execution`: "trusted native (user account privileges)" or "restricted container (<backend>, image <image>, network <network>, user <user>, declared inputs read-only)"; manifests without a recorded mode are trusted native).
- **Enable Research** (`research_space_enable`): one `ResearchSpace` entity per project (contained by the project) and `.exposition/research/<rs-id>/{sources,data,notes}`; idempotent; starts nothing; needs no manuscript. Research files are admitted as resources (members of the space) when created, imported, or listed.
- **Import** (`research_space_import`): copy into a group (never overwrites: `name (2).ext`; the copy's sha256 must match; origin recorded as `import.*` attributes) or a read-only `ExternalResource` reference (never copied, crawled, or run). Files inside `.exposition/` are refused.
- **Declared inputs:** `research_execute` accepts `declaredInputs` (at most 32 project files, 1 GiB each); each is pinned by sha256 (open buffer first) in the run's inputs when it is dispatched. `RunManifest.unsavedInputs` lists inputs that were unsaved buffers (their hash is the buffer's; the code itself is kept in the manifest up to 256 KiB).
- **Retention** (`research_retain`, `research_result_state`): Keep / Archive / Discard. Keeping admits the artifact file as one resource (`artifact`; tables `dataset` + `artifact`), verified against the run's recorded sha256 (`research.artifactChanged` otherwise), with service-owned `Computational` evidence (run id, inputs with hashes/revisions/unsaved flag, environment and kernel versions, output hash), `derivedFrom` its run inputs (and the chosen experiment), and membership in the space. Discard changes the lifecycle only (no file, attachment, or evidence is removed); output never kept cannot be discarded into the graph (`research.notRetained`).
- **Experiments** (`research_experiment_save`, `research_experiment`): an `Experiment` entity (objective, procedure) created through the validated change path as the user: `experiment derivedFrom` procedure and declared inputs, `outcome derivedFrom experiment`; attempts are the runs of the procedure (and the runs of its outcomes), at most 50, newest first: the procedure's runs are looked for in the first page of the run history, the runs of the outcomes are read by id. When the project holds more runs than that page, `attemptsIncomplete` is set and the Attempts section says "Only the most recent runs of the project were examined; older runs of the procedure may not be listed." Updates need the entity revision (`research.experimentChanged`).
- **Reviewed promotion** (`research_promotion_prepare`, `research_promotion_approve`, `research_promotion_cancel`): prepare verifies the artifact digest and plans the format adapter's exact edits against one document revision (kept in Rust for 30 min); approval re-verifies the digest (`research.artifactChanged`) and the revision (`promotion.stale`), re-plans and compares (`promotion.planChanged`), writes the intent to the operation journal, applies one undoable transaction with `Reject` conflicts, then retains the artifact and records `manuscript usesAsset artifact`. If the graph write fails, the outcome is `pendingReconciliation` and the journal record is `NeedsReconciliation` (shown on the Research page); `research_promotion_reconcile` records the attachment if its markers are in the document (never re-applying the edit) or closes the record. Undo/redo of the insertion removes/restores the `usesAsset` relation research recorded; on project open, interrupted promotions are marked for reconciliation and followed attachments are re-checked. "Insert or update in document..." uses this review. `research_promotion_preview` returns a still-pending review exactly as prepared (`null` once applied, canceled, or expired), so a review a workflow step prepared can be shown in full before approval.
- **Codes:** `research.notEnabled`, `research.artifactChanged`, `research.notRetained`, `research.notExperiment`, `research.invalidExperiment`, `research.experimentChanged`, `research.runActive` (also: deleting the record or outputs of an active run, §8.4), `research.restoreConflict` and `research.unknownOutputs` (restoring deleted run outputs, §8.4), `promotion.{unknown, expired, projectChanged, stale, planChanged, journal, document, unknownOperation, unreadable}`, plus `graph.*` codes from the graph service. Workflow batch codes: §7.6.

## 5. Lean

- **Project:** folder with `lakefile.lean`/`lakefile.toml`; other files are standalone (root = open folder, pin = nearest `lean-toolchain`). Scan: depth 6, 20,000 entries, skips dot-folders, `.lake`, `lake-packages`, `node_modules`, `target`; runs nothing.
- **Toolchain:** pin = `lean-toolchain`, else elan `default_toolchain` (`ELAN_HOME` or `~/.elan`); channels pick the newest installed match (with a note). Resolved directly to `<ELAN_HOME>/toolchains/<dir>/bin/{lean,lake}`; elan shims never run; nothing downloads implicitly. Version from `lean --version`.
- **Install:** command "Install Project Toolchain" / Infoview banner → confirm (elan downloads from the internet) → needs trust and elan (settings location re-checked at use, `ELAN_HOME/bin`, PATH) → `elan toolchain install <qualified>` trusted batch job (1 h) → waiting files re-attach.
- **Server:** `lake serve` (or `lean --server` for standalone files), one per (project root, toolchain), TrustedNative interactive job; LSP over stdio, Content-Length framed (header ≤ 8 KiB, body ≤ 64 MiB); `hasWidgets:false` (no RPC/widgets). Crash → auto-restart after 1/2/4/8/16 s; Unavailable retried every 10 s and on edit. Stops 120 s after its last file closes, and on folder switch/close or trust revoke; `lean-toolchain`/`lakefile.*`/`lake-manifest.json` changes restart it. **Restart Lean Server:** shutdown (3 s) + re-attach. **Restart File:** `didClose` + `didOpen` (rebuilds out-of-date imports).
- **Package gate:** before a server starts for a file in a Lake package, the package is inspected from files only (`inspect_package`, `crates/exposition-lean/src/setup.rs`: lakefile and its SHA-256, `lean-toolchain`, `lake-manifest.json` and its `packagesDir`, dependency checkouts with the address each is fetched from (credentials in an `http(s)` address left out), Mathlib's `lean-toolchain`, and `.lake/build/lib/lean/Mathlib.olean` in Mathlib's checkout as the sign of its build outputs). *Reading the lakefile* (≤ 1 MiB, never run; a hint only, the manifest decides when there is one): `lakefile.toml` is parsed with the `toml` crate (`[[require]]` tables, an inline array of tables, or one table; `name`, `scope`, `git` (string, `git.url`, or `source.url`), `path` (or `source.path` / `source.dir`), `rev`; a file that is not TOML, or whose `require` has another shape, counts as requiring something when its text contains "require" and as requiring Mathlib when it also contains "mathlib"); `lakefile.lean` is code: comments are removed (`--`, nested `/- … -/`, doc comments; string literals kept) and its `require` commands are read as written (`require [scope /] name [@ "rev"] [from git "address" | from "path"]`, names in quotes or `«»`), and requirements made any other way are not seen. `usesMathlib`: the manifest pins `mathlib`, or without a readable manifest a requirement named `mathlib` (any letter case) was read. `requirementsInCode`: the requirements are code or could not be parsed, something is required, and the text mentions Mathlib outside Lean comments (a lakefile that is not readable text counts). *The decision* (`blocks_checking`) is taken from what the files show, not from the headline readiness, so a missing pin or an unfinished-setup record never hides unresolved or missing dependencies. No server starts, and the file is `Unavailable` with the reason, when a setup is running in that package ("Mathlib is being set up in this package. …"); when the package does not use Mathlib but has no manifest and `requirementsInCode` ("This package's lakefile.lean is Lean code that requires dependencies and mentions Mathlib, and they have not been resolved … If the package requires Mathlib, use Set Up Mathlib; otherwise run `lake update` in the package folder in a terminal."); or when the package uses Mathlib and, in this order, a setup left it unfinished (unless the user chose to build from source and the record allows it, below), there is no manifest, pinned checkouts are absent (named), Mathlib's checkout pins another Lean than the package, or Mathlib's build outputs are absent and the user did not choose to build from source. Nothing else blocks: a package that does not use Mathlib is not held back by an unfinished-setup record (its readiness is not `unfinished` either), and a `lakefile.lean` that requires other libraries without mentioning Mathlib is started as before (Lake resolves them in the server). Readiness (`LeanPackageReadiness`, the headline shown by the Lean view, first match): `notAPackage`, `unfinished{message, canBuildFromSource}`, `toolchainUnpinned`, `toolchainMissing`, `dependenciesUnresolved` (requirements but no manifest), `dependenciesMissing`, `toolchainMismatch`, `cacheMissing`, `ready`. The Lean view shows the reason and, by readiness, **Set Up Mathlib…** (for `cacheMissing`, `dependenciesMissing`, `dependenciesUnresolved`, `unfinished`, and `toolchainUnpinned`, where the setup explains which pin is needed; not for a mismatch) and **Build from source instead…** for `cacheMissing` and for `unfinished` with `canBuildFromSource` (`lean_allow_local_build`: confirmed, per package root, until the app closes). The choice replaces only the missing cache: it never lifts unresolved or missing dependencies or a mismatch. `canBuildFromSource` is true when the unfinished setup recorded that the manifest pinned every dependency and every checkout (Mathlib's included) was present before it began, and the manifest is still there with all checkouts and no mismatch. The gate applies to everything that goes through the Lean service: the editor, `lean_check` (agents' `check_lean`), and the workflow `lean.audit` step, which therefore resolve the same package root, pinned toolchain, and dependencies.
- **Set Up Mathlib** (`lean.setUpMathlib`; `lean_setup_plan` / `lean_setup_start` / `lean_setup_cancel` / `lean_setup_status`, events `lean:setup`): the user's action only; no agent tool reaches it. Requests: `newMathlibPackage{folder, name}` or `prepareMathlibPackage{folder}`. The webview names a folder inside the project ("" = the project folder; ≤ 400 chars; no component starting with `.` or named `lake-packages`, `node_modules`, `target`, `venv`; every existing component a real folder, no links, resolving inside the project) and a library name (a capital letter then letters and digits, ≤ 40; not `Mathlib` or a Windows device name); Rust chooses the toolchain and the Mathlib revision and builds every command.
  - *New package:* refused when the folder already is a Lake package (`packageExists`), is inside one (`insidePackage`) or contains one (`containsPackage`), or already holds `lake-manifest.json`, `.lake`, `<Name>.lean`, or `<Name>` (`wouldOverwrite`). Toolchain: the folder's own `lean-toolchain` when present (kept; installed by the first step when missing), else elan's default when it resolves to an installed Lean release, else the newest installed release that is not a release candidate (`noElan`, `noToolchain` otherwise). Mathlib revision: the tag named like the Lean release (`leanprover/lean4:vX.Y.Z[-rcN]` → `vX.Y.Z[-rcN]`; any other toolchain, a channel such as `stable` included → `noMathlibRelease`). Tested pair: `leanprover/lean4:v4.35.0` with Mathlib `v4.35.0` (`tests/fixtures/lean-mathlib`). Writes (create-new, never replacing) `lakefile.toml` (Lake's `math` template layout with `[[require]] name = "mathlib"`, `scope = "leanprover-community"`, `rev = "<tag>"`), `lean-toolchain` unless kept, `<Name>.lean` (`import <Name>.Basic`), `<Name>/Basic.lean` (a starter theorem using `Mathlib.Tactic.Ring`), `.gitignore` (`/.lake`) unless one exists. Sources: one row, `mathlib` from the registry entry `leanprover-community/mathlib` at the tag, with the note that Lake resolves it and everything it requires. Steps: `lake --keep-toolchain update` with `MATHLIB_NO_CACHE_ON_UPDATE=1` (30 min), `lake exe cache get` (60 min), `lake build <Name>` (30 min).
  - *Existing package:* refused without a lakefile (`notAPackage`), with an unreadable pin or manifest (`invalidToolchain`, `invalidManifest`), without `lean-toolchain` (`toolchainUnpinned`), when it does not use Mathlib (`noMathlib`; an existing lakefile is never edited), when already ready (`alreadyPrepared`), on a toolchain mismatch (`toolchainMismatch`), when the lakefile is not a regular file of at most 1 MiB (`invalidLakefile`), when `mathlib` is not fetched from a git address the plan can show (`mathlibSourceUnsupported`, below), or when `lean-toolchain` names a channel (`stable`, `beta`, `nightly`) instead of one Lean version (`channelPin`: "… Write the exact release in lean-toolchain …, then set up again. Nothing was changed."). Sources: with a manifest, every package it pins (name, type, address, commit, requested revision, whether its checkout is present) and no note; without one, the requirements read from the lakefile (kind `git`, `path`, or `reservoir`; address; requested revision) with the note "Lake resolves these and everything they require once and pins each revision in lake-manifest.json. The setup stops before the cache step unless `mathlib` then comes from <the plan's Mathlib address>.", preceded for a `lakefile.lean` by "<lakefile> is Lean code: the requirements listed are the ones written in it, and it can require more." Steps: `lake --keep-toolchain update` only when there is no manifest; `lake exe cache get`; `lake build --no-build Mathlib` (verifies the outputs, builds nothing). With a manifest no `lake update` runs: Lake materializes the pinned revisions. Nothing is written by the setup itself; the lakefile, `lean-toolchain`, and an existing manifest are kept.
  - *Both:* a pinned toolchain that is not installed adds a first step `elan toolchain install <qualified>` (60 min; `noElan` when elan cannot be located). `lake` is the installed toolchain's own `bin/lake` under `<ELAN_HOME>/toolchains/` (for a toolchain the first step installs, the folder elan will create for it), never a shim; elan's default toolchain is never changed. *Mathlib source* (`mathlibSource: LeanSetupMathlibSource{official, url}`; `MATHLIB_URL` = `https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4`, official also with `.git`, a trailing `/`, in any letter case, or as `git@github.com:…` / `ssh://git@github.com/…`): with a manifest, the `git` address of its `mathlib` package; without one, the `git` address of the lakefile requirement named `mathlib`, else the official repository. Any other address is a fork: `official: false`, `url` exactly as written (without credentials), the summary ends "Mathlib comes from <url>, not the official repository: the setup runs that repository's code, and the prebuilt cache may not cover it.", and the dialog shows a warning Banner in Sources (`lean-setup-fork-warning`: "Mathlib is not from the official repository" / "Mathlib comes from <url>, not the official repository (https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4). This setup downloads and runs that repository's code: its `cache` program runs on this computer with your account's privileges. The prebuilt cache may not cover a fork; then the cache step or the check after it fails, and Mathlib can only be built from source (hours).") and the badge "Not official" on the `mathlib` row (`data-official`); one primary action, no confirmation. Refused at planning (`mathlibSourceUnsupported`: "… takes `mathlib` from <source>. Set Up Mathlib prepares a Mathlib fetched from a git address the plan can show: the official repository (…) or a fork. <why> Nothing was changed."): a manifest `mathlib` that is not a `git` package or has no address, a lakefile requirement with a local path, a registry scope other than `leanprover-community` without a git address, or two different fork addresses. The plan (`LeanSetupPlan`: roots, `createsPackage`, toolchain and whether installed, `mathlibRevision`, `mathlibSource`, `pinnedByManifest`, `lakefileDigest` and `manifestDigest` (SHA-256 of the existing files; `null` for a new package or a missing manifest), `sources` (`LeanSetupSource{name, kind, scope, url, rev, inputRev, present}`; fields ≤ 200 chars without control characters, addresses without credentials), `sourcesNote`, `writes`, `keeps`, `removedOnFailure`, steps with program, args, per-step environment, `network`, timeout, a summary, `planHash` = SHA-256 over the whole plan except the hash itself) is built from files only. Start re-plans and refuses a different plan (`lean.setup.planStale`; an edited lakefile or a manifest whose dependencies changed after the review gives other digests and sources, so the reviewed plan is stale; checked before trust), requires trust (`lean.untrusted`; looked up on the package folder, or while a new package's folder does not exist yet on the nearest existing folder above it; each step is checked again at launch), allows one setup at a time (`busy`), and restarts the package's Lean server, which the gate then holds back while the setup runs. Each step is a TrustedNative job of the execution service in the package folder (label "Mathlib setup: <step>", log ≤ 1 MiB, no stdin); output is forwarded bounded and redacted (events ≤ 16 KiB, status tail 80 lines of ≤ 500 chars, the webview keeps 200 lines). After `lake update` the setup fails if `lean-toolchain` no longer pins the planned toolchain, Mathlib's checkout pins another Lean than the package (reported before any cache is fetched), no manifest was written, or the written manifest does not pin `mathlib` as a git package from the plan's `mathlibSource` (official: any accepted spelling; a fork: the same address, compared exactly apart from surrounding space, a trailing `/`, `.git`, and credentials): "Lake resolved `mathlib` from <address>, but the plan you reviewed takes it from <plan address>[ (the official repository)]. Its cache program was not run." / "The resolved dependencies do not include `mathlib` …". The run then ends as any failed run (cleanup below). With a manifest the same comparison is made on the manifest as it is just before the cache step. When the cache step or the verification fails for a fork, the message adds "Mathlib here comes from <url>, and the prebuilt cache may not cover a fork." and either "To build it from source instead (hours), choose Build from source instead… in the Lean view." (the gate would lift) or "… first download the sources yourself: run `lake --keep-toolchain update` (no manifest) / `lake exe cache get` (manifest) in the package folder in a terminal, then choose Build from source instead… in the Lean view." Fork cache behaviour (read from the `cache` program of Mathlib `v4.35.0`, not run against a fork): the repository is taken from the checkout's `origin`; reads go to the official store by content hash, then to a per-fork, per-commit store filled by Mathlib's CI; files not found are not an error and `cache get` exits 0, so an incomplete cache fails at the verification step. Success requires readiness `ready` on a final inspection.
  - *Failure, cancel, records:* what the run will create (`removedOnFailure` entries that do not exist yet) is recorded in `<data>/lean/setup-records.json` (`exposition.leanSetupRecords/1`; never in the project) before it exists, together with whether the dependency sources were complete (`sourcesIntact`: an existing package whose manifest pinned every dependency, Mathlib among them, with every checkout present before the first step). When the setup fails or is canceled, only that is removed again, innermost first (`clean_up`): `.lake` and `lake-manifest.json` whole (Lake's own outputs); a file the setup wrote only while its SHA-256 is still that of what was written; a folder the setup made, the package folder included, only once it is empty. Anything else found in those folders, and a written file whose content changed, was added or changed meanwhile: it stays, is listed in the status (`kept`, ≤ 20 paths, an empty folder with a trailing `/`), and is named in the message ("The files this setup created were removed. Kept, because they were added or changed while it ran: `<path>`, …. Nothing else in the project changed."). A new package otherwise leaves nothing ("The new package's files were removed; nothing else in the project changed."); an existing package whose `.lake` existed before keeps completed downloads and stays recorded as unfinished (not ready; gate reason "A Mathlib setup did not finish in this package …", with "… building Mathlib from source stays possible." when `canBuildFromSource`) until a setup of it succeeds, as does any run whose cleanup failed; a run in which no command started leaves no record. An elan-installed toolchain is not removed. Revoking trust stops the running step ("Workspace trust was revoked; setup was stopped."). The status (`LeanSetupStatus`: state running/succeeded/failed/canceled, phase preparing/step/verifying/cleaningUp/finished, step index, message, `removed`, `kept`, final inspection, `ready`, job ids, log tail) is kept for the running setup, else the last finished one of the session.
  - *Not covered:* no upgrade (`lake update` on a package that has a manifest never runs), no change to an existing lakefile, no Mathlib from a fork, a mirror, or a local folder, no check that the dependencies other than `mathlib` come from any particular address (they are listed for review), no recognition of a Mathlib requirement a `lakefile.lean` computes without naming it, no sandbox for Lake (`lake update` and `lake exe cache get` run the package's Lake configuration and downloaded programs with the account's privileges), network and several GB of disk in `.lake` required, one tested Lean/Mathlib pair.

| Feature | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| Diagnostics | the current version only. Messages ≤ 20,000 bytes, with hints for `sorry` and unsolved goals. Monaco markers and Problems (source "Lean") only when they match the editor revision |
| Hover | synced revision only. Untrusted Markdown, no HTML, ≤ 50,000 chars. Links are copied to the clipboard, never opened |
| Goals | cursor or focus, 120 ms debounce → `plainGoal` + `plainTermGoal`. Plain text: hypotheses / `⊢`, "Goal i of n", "Expected type". A newer query cancels the older one |
| Definition | Ctrl+Click, F12 or Go to Definition; ≤ 50 targets. Opens the workspace file, or notifies "Defined outside the workspace" |
| `sorry` marks | semantic tokens `leanSorryLike`, decorated and listed in the Infoview |
| Abbreviations | 202 entries. `\` gives completion (≤ 60). Auto-expands on space, tab, `()[]{},` or newline. Undoable; `\\` does not expand |
| Syntax | Monarch grammar; `sorry`, `admit` and `stop` tokenized as invalid |

| Status (shown as) | Meaning |
|---|---|
| NotStarted (Not checked) | not yet sent to Lean |
| Unavailable (Lean unavailable) | no folder open, or a bad `lean-toolchain` file; or the package gate: a Lake package that is being set up, uses Mathlib and was left unfinished by a setup or is not prepared, or has a `lakefile.lean` with unresolved requirements that mentions Mathlib (the reason is shown) |
| ToolchainMissing (Toolchain missing) | the pinned toolchain is not installed, or there is no pin and no default |
| Untrusted (Untrusted) | the folder is not trusted; nothing runs |
| Checking (Starting Lean / Checking / Auditing axioms) | the server is starting, Lean is elaborating (progress line), or the audit is running |
| Checked (Checked) | no errors, no `sorry`, reached the end, the kernel re-checked every dependency, only the toolchain's `propext`/`Classical.choice`/`Quot.sound` |
| CheckedWithSorry (Incomplete (sorry)) | depends on `sorry`, including imported or warning-suppressed uses ("found by the axiom audit") |
| ReliesOnCompiledCode (Relies on compiled code) | native evaluation axioms (`native_decide`) or the toolchain's `ofReduceBool`/`ofReduceNat`/`trustCompiler` |
| CheckedWithAxioms (Checked with axioms) | any other axiom. A non-toolchain axiom with a standard name is shown "(declared outside the toolchain)" |
| PartiallyChecked (Partially checked) | stopped before the end (`#exit` or another terminal command) |
| Unverified (Not verified) | the kernel rejected a declaration on replay (for example `skipKernelTC`); a non-toolchain `prelude` module or a declaration named like a constant the kernel evaluates by name; a declaration the language server indexes for this file is missing from the audit (tripwire); or the audit could not run: unsupported toolchain, modified programs, bad output, timeout |
| Errors (N error(s)) | an error diagnostic or a fatal stop; no audit |
| ServerUnavailable (Lean not started) | the server could not start (for example the job limit); retried |
| ServerCrashed (Server crashed) | the server died; shows attempts and whether it is restarting |

The final status is chosen in this order: Errors, Unverified, Partially checked, Incomplete, Relies on compiled code, Checked with axioms, Checked.

**Audit (two steps):**
1. Runs after Lean reports a revision error-free (or speculatively 800 ms after a stable edit, only if its import lines match a version Lean already finished; a failed speculative audit reruns when Lean is done), with the toolchain's `lean`, its library first on `LEAN_PATH` plus the project path from `lake env`, in `%TEMP%\exposition-lean\audit`; nothing written to the project.
2. **Compile** (runs the file's code): elaborates the exact text as a temporary module, writes its `.olean`, reports from frontend snapshots whether elaboration reached the end; only the one line tagged with a per-run secret (sent on stdin, then closed) counts.
3. **Check** (runs none of the file's code): imports only toolchain modules, reads the module and its non-toolchain imports as `.olean` data, refuses non-toolchain `prelude` modules and kernel-special names, replays every non-toolchain dependency into the kernel (`Kernel.Environment.replay`), lists each declaration's axioms with toolchain origin and prints each checked declaration's statement with the toolchain's notation only.
4. **Tripwire:** names written after declaration keywords that the audit lacks are looked up in the language server's `workspace/symbol` index; any hit → Not verified.
5. Both programs are compiled before the first language server for a toolchain starts, SHA-256-pinned, held open with write/delete denied for the service's lifetime (Windows) and re-hashed around each step (tampering → Not verified, recompiled next time); written for Lean 4.35 APIs; 15 min and 32 MiB log per step.

- **`lean_check`** (the service check behind the agent tool `check_lean`; the IPC command of this name is debug-only, §10): target = open document or path (disk text as a temporary document); `checked` only for Checked of that exact revision; diagnostics, `sorry` locations, axioms and goals (≤ 32 positions) only when finished; timeout 120 s default, 600 s max (agent tool: 300 s). A check records no evidence.
- **Formal evidence (M7):** the service keeps the audit record of each finished revision (`lean_audit_record`: every audited declaration with its statement, axioms, and its own verdict from those axioms (Checked, Checked with axioms, Incomplete, Relies on compiled code) next to the file's status). `lean_record_evidence(document, declarations)` registers service-owned `FormalCheck` evidence per listed declaration (file and declaration verdict, toolchain, sha256 of the audited text, revision, `dependencies: unavailable` "the Lean audit does not report declaration dependencies", no fingerprints, so freshness is never current); names the audit did not list are refused. For a file in a Lake package the audit record carries `package` (`LeanAuditPackage{folder, toolchainFile{path, sha256}?, manifest{path, sha256}?, mathlibRevision?, mathlibSource?, dependencyCount}`, read from files when the audit finishes; `null` for a standalone file), and the evidence records it: environment entries `lake package`, `lake-manifest.json sha256`, `mathlib revision`, `mathlib source` (the git address the manifest fetches Mathlib from, without credentials, so a fork is told apart; not part of the audit id; absent in earlier records) next to the toolchain and `lean --version`, and `lean-toolchain` and `lake-manifest.json` as inputs with their digests after the source. The audit id is `<path>@<revision>:<16 hex of the text digest>`, plus `+<16 hex of the manifest digest>` when there is a manifest, plus `~<16 hex>` of a SHA-256 over the Lean environment (the toolchain name, its `lean --version`, and the digest of the package's `lean-toolchain`, length-prefixed, an absent part distinct from an empty one), so the same text audited against another manifest or under another Lean environment is recorded as new evidence instead of reusing the old record. A record stored under the earlier id (without the `~` part) stays listed and is reused only when its recorded toolchain, `lean --version`, and `lean-toolchain` digest equal the audit's (`same_audit`). Evidence is recorded only by these commands and by `workflow_association_decide` (§7.7), always on the `FormalDeclaration`; a check alone, or a `formalizes` relation, records none. The declaration is a `FormalDeclaration` entity (key = qualified name, bound to the `.lean` file, `anchoredIn` it). `lean_associate_declaration({declaration: {kind: "audited", document, name} | {kind: "entity", entity}, target, note})` records the reviewed `formalizes` relation (accepted: a correspondence, not a proof of equivalence). `lean_declaration_evidence(document)` lists the evidence history with freshness against the current text (older audits are stale history) and against the recorded package files: a `lean-toolchain` or `lake-manifest.json` whose indexed digest differs from the recorded one, or that was deleted, makes the record stale ("… the Lean version or dependency revisions it was checked against are no longer the package's"); a file the index has no answer for is not reported. The graph's own freshness (§7.1) treats the two files as inputs in the same way. This is file-level identity only: no declaration-level dependency fingerprints exist, and a change inside a dependency checkout that leaves the manifest unchanged is not detected. The infoview shows "Mathlib <12 hex> · lake-manifest.json <12 hex>" with the evidence, and treats evidence as recorded for the current audit only when text digest, revision, manifest digest, toolchain, `lean --version`, and the `lean-toolchain` digest all match (`isRecordOfAudit`); otherwise Record evidence is offered again. The infoview offers Record evidence and Associate with... on checked and audited declarations.
- **Error codes** (45; 23 + 22 `lean.setup.`): `lean.setup.` + `invalidFolder`, `invalidName`, `packageExists`, `insidePackage`, `containsPackage`, `wouldOverwrite`, `invalidToolchain`, `invalidManifest`, `invalidLakefile`, `noElan`, `noToolchain`, `noMathlibRelease`, `channelPin`, `notAPackage`, `toolchainUnpinned`, `noMathlib`, `mathlibSourceUnsupported`, `alreadyPrepared`, `toolchainMismatch`, `planStale`, `busy`, `failed` (the setup thread could not start); a setup in an untrusted folder is `lean.untrusted`. `lean.` + `invalidDocument`, `invalidRequest`, `stopped`, `closed`, `cancelled`, `timeout`, `serverStopped`, `server`, `unknownDocument`, `noWorkspace`, `stale`, `notReady`, `invalidOffset`, `toolchainInstalled`, `invalidToolchain`, `untrusted`, `noElan`, `notLean`, `unreadable`; formal evidence: `noAudit` (no revision has a finished audit), `notAudited` (the audit did not list that declaration), `notDeclaration` (only a formal declaration can formalize), `associationRefused` (the graph refused the association; its reason). Graph errors during evidence commands keep their `graph.*` code.

## 6. Agents

### 6.1 Provider and model
| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Providers | `anthropic` (Messages API, streaming); `openai` (Responses API with an API key); `scripted` (deterministic, compiled only in debug builds; a stored `scripted` reads as `anthropic` when not allowed). Every provider runs the same task loop, tool validation, permission gate (Ask/Auto, restricted container, graph controls), budgets, cancellation, review, and artifact rules; a provider's tool call is a request the gate decides, never a grant |
| Connections | One per provider, authenticated by an API key (`AuthMode::apiKey`) in its own OS credential entry (`anthropic-api-key…` / `openai-api-key…`; keys never mix); OpenAI optional context `organization` (`org-…`) and `project` (`proj_…`) sent as `OpenAI-Organization` / `OpenAI-Project`. The ChatGPT-plan sign-in ("Sign in with ChatGPT") is not offered: OpenAI documents it for open-source clients (rechecked 2026-11-17) and Exposition's eligibility is not established; API-key access is never labeled as plan access |
| Settings (`ProviderSettings`) | the default every new task inherits: `provider` (default `anthropic`) · `model` exact provider ID, trimmed, 1–100 chars `[A-Za-z0-9.-_:@/]`, default `claude-opus-5-5` · `effort` `low`/`medium`/`high`/`xhigh`/`max`, default `medium` · `consent` default for new tasks · `openai?` connection context. Settings saved before connections existed read unchanged (same provider, model, effort; no `openai`) |
| Resolution | A task records `{provider, model, effort}` when it starts: the composer's explicit choice or the default. Changing the default affects later tasks only, never a running task or its review subagents. An OpenAI task also records the organization/project context it started with and keeps it for continuations (a model switch resolves it again from the settings). Preflight prefers the connection's fetched catalog entry (e.g. Anthropic's image/PDF flags) over the maintained rules; a model without reasoning gets no reasoning items or parameters, and earlier non-reasoning OpenAI models get their smaller output limit (16 384). A follow-up may switch model explicitly (`ContinueTaskRequest.provider`): the task keeps its identity, instructions, permissions, graph record, and changes; the switch is refused with an explanation when the new model cannot read the history's attachments, and recorded as a notice. Workflow review steps use the default and record it in the run manifest |
| History | Canonical content-block JSON (Anthropic shape) in `agents.sqlite3`. Provider-private state stays with its adapter: Anthropic `thinking`/`redacted_thinking`/`fallback` blocks are never sent to OpenAI; OpenAI reasoning items (`openai_reasoning`, encrypted, replayed with their tool calls) are never sent to Anthropic; nothing is fabricated for the other provider |
| Preflight | Before a task starts or continues: `agents.modelUnsupported` (catalog says the model cannot run the agent), `agents.effortUnsupported` (effort level not accepted), `agents.attachmentUnsupported` (images/PDF on a model not known to read them; unknown support is not treated as support); nothing is dropped |
| Storage | JSON in `agents.sqlite3` table `settings`, key `provider`; never a key; corrupt JSON → defaults |
| Request (Anthropic) | `POST https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages`; `x-api-key`, `anthropic-version: 2026-10-21`, `anthropic-beta: server-side-fallback-2026-07-01` (+ body `fallbacks:"default"`) only for models starting `claude-opus-5`, `claude-fable-5`, `claude-mythos-5`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`; UA `Exposition/0.1.0`; adaptive summarized thinking, `output_config.effort`, strict tools with eager input streaming, `tool_choice:auto`, prompt caching; `max_tokens` = min(remaining output budget, 64 000) |
| Request (OpenAI) | `POST https://api.openai.com/v1/responses`; `Authorization: Bearer`; `stream:true`, `store:false` (full history each request), `instructions` = system prompt, function tools (`strict` only when the schema meets strict-mode rules), `tool_choice:auto`, `parallel_tool_calls:true`, `max_output_tokens` ≤ 64 000; for models with effort: `reasoning:{effort, summary:"auto"}` + `include:["reasoning.encrypted_content"]`. Images → `input_image` data URLs, PDFs → `input_file`, tool results → `function_call_output`. Stream: text and reasoning-summary deltas; final items from `response.completed` / `response.incomplete` (`max_output_tokens` → stopped, `content_filter` → refused with category `content_filter`, any other reason → stopped as an unexpected response naming it); a refusal item → refused with its text (bounded) as the explanation, never stored as the agent's text; a stream ending without a terminal event is a network error, never success; `insufficient_quota` is not retried |
| Status / network | Both adapters: redirects never followed (3xx → "rejected the request"); connect timeout 30 s, idle read 300 s; 4 retries (1 s·2^n + jitter, ≤30 s; 429 `retry-after` ≤120 s) for rate-limit, overloaded, 5xx, network (a retry after streamed output emits a reset; tools run only after a complete response, so a retry never repeats a side effect); never for auth/invalid/protocol/cancel. Webview sees `ProviderStatus {settings, configured (default provider's connection), connections[{provider, auth, configured, credentialError, context}], credentialStore, credentialError, scriptedAvailable}`; `providerChanged` after every settings/key change; each connection carries a `version` that changes with every key save/removal (views refetch its models). Debug builds accept loopback-only endpoint overrides `EXPOSITION_DEV_ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` / `EXPOSITION_DEV_OPENAI_BASE_URL` (`http://127.0.0.1:<port>`) for end-to-end mocks; release builds ignore them |
| Model catalog (`agents_models_list`) | Fetched in Rust with the connection's key: OpenAI `GET /v1/models` (`data[]`: id, created, shutdown_date); Anthropic `GET /v1/models?limit=1000` paginated by `after_id` (display name, lifecycle active/deprecated/retired, `max_input_tokens`, `max_tokens`, nullable `capabilities` with image/PDF input, effort levels, adaptive thinking). `ModelDescriptor {id, displayName, lifecycle, agent: supported / unverified{reason} / unsupported{reason}, capabilities {tools, images, pdf: yes/no/unknown, efforts, contextTokens, maxOutputTokens}, source: catalog/saved}`. A model the provider lists but Exposition has not verified stays *unverified*, never automatically eligible. Cached per connection fingerprint (provider + digest of the key + context) for 24 h; `refresh` fetches now; a failed refresh returns the cached list as `stale` with the reason; no key → `unavailable`; a connection change during a fetch discards the result. The saved default model is always listed as itself (`saved` when not listed); the suggested default is the saved choice when listed and supported, else the newest supported active model (with its reason). Capability rules are maintained in `crates/exposition-agents/src/catalog.rs` (`METADATA_CHECKED`) |
| Attachments | §6.1.1 |

### 6.1.1 Attachments
| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Kinds | UTF-8 text and source files (≤ 1 MiB; sent as a text block `<attachment name=… media_type=…>`), PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP images (≤ 3.75 MiB so the encoded image stays within the providers' 5 MiB; each side ≤ 8000 px; sent as image content; with more than 20 images in an Anthropic conversation each must be ≤ 2000 px on a side, `attachments.tooManyLargeImages`), PDF (≤ 20 MiB, so one always fits a request; sent as a document / `input_file`, only to models known to read PDFs). The kind is decided by the bytes, never by the name or a declared type; anything else is rejected with a reason (`attachments.unsupported`, `…TooLarge`, `…imageDimensions`, `…unsupportedImage`, `…empty`, `…unreadable`) |
| Ingestion | **Attach files**: the backend opens the native multi-file dialog (`agents_attachments_pick`; the webview never names a path) and reads only the chosen files. **Paste**: image bytes from the clipboard (`agents_attachments_add_bytes`, base64), named "Pasted image.png" when nameless. **Drop**: files dropped from the operating system on the chat (`agents_attachments_add_dropped(paths)`, at most 100 per drop): the desktop shell records the paths of each native drop on the window (`DroppedFiles`, `RunEvent::WebviewEvent` drag-drop; at most 4096 paths, each for 60 s) and only recorded paths are read, each once and only within those 60 s (`attachments.notDropped` otherwise, also for a path dropped longer ago or already attached), so the webview alone never names a file; a folder is `attachments.folder`, never read. **Pasted text**: a large text paste as UTF-8 bytes through `agents_attachments_add_bytes` ("pasted-text-N.txt", `text/plain`), an ordinary text attachment. A partial batch keeps the accepted files; duplicates get distinct ids (a drop does not attach content the draft already has) |
| Snapshot | Content is copied into `<data>/agents/attachments/<sha256>` at ingestion (content-addressed, written atomically, verified on read); later edits to the source file never change a queued or sent message. Metadata in `agents.sqlite3` table `attachments` (schema 6). Attaching never imports the file into the project, promotes a research artifact, or gives the agent access to the file's folder |
| Messages | Up to 20 per message; attachment-only messages are allowed. The message stores `exposition_attachment` reference blocks; when a run first sends the conversation they become the provider's content read from the snapshot (read and encoded once per run; a message the run adds is converted as it is stored). The whole request's attachments (replayed history included) are limited to 30 MiB encoded (`attachments.requestTooLarge`); a missing snapshot fails the request with its name, never silently omitted |
| Display and retention | Sent messages show their attachments (name, kind, size; thumbnails for images via `agents_attachment_preview`), also after reopening; a missing snapshot is marked. Drafts removed before sending are forgotten (`agents_attachments_discard`); unsent drafts older than a day and snapshots no stored message refers to are removed at the next start (purging a task record removes its snapshots then) |

### 6.2 Task lifecycle and statuses
- **Create** (`CreateTaskRequest {prompt, workspaceRoot, scope, permission, execution, consent?, budgets?, approvalMode?}`; absent `approvalMode` = `ask`): prompt trimmed, 1–100 000 chars; folder absolute, existing, not a drive root, and the open project; budgets in range; files scope non-empty; selection document open at that revision; an `auto` task needs the project's permissions record (§6.5; else `agents.permissionsRequired`, also on Continue) → `queued` → first user message = `<task_context>` (project folder name, scope, the authorization lines below, consent, up to 40 open in-scope documents with revision and "saved" / "unsaved changes in the editor") + `<selection>` only with `scopeContent` (the selected text as written, except that the `<` of a tag opening or closing `selection` or `task_context`, in any letter case and with spaces or `/` after it, is replaced by `‹`, so the text cannot close its block or forge a context block) + prompt → worker starts.
- **Run loop**: `running` → checkpoint once (hash, revision, unsaved flag of every open document) → per turn: budget check → stream → store assistant content → run each tool call in order → all results in one user message → repeat until end of turn, budget, cancel, error or stop.
- **Continue** (follow-up prompt, optional new budgets; UI sends none): any inactive task; refused while active (`agents.taskRunning`; of two Continues sent at once one starts the run and the other gets this) or while the worker still releases services (`agents.taskFinishing`); a refused Continue changes nothing stored (budgets, recorded connection, transcript); folder re-checked; unanswered `tool_use` blocks get their recorded result or "Not executed: the previous run stopped before this tool ran."; the follow-up is preceded by a `<task_context>` update read from the application at that moment ("It replaces the earlier approval and file state; scope and content consent are unchanged."): the authorization lines, up to 40 files the task changed (edited / created / deleted / copied by the agent; unsaved in the editor, saved, saved on disk and not open, not on disk, or unreadable; not reviewed yet, kept, reverted, or no longer containing the change), and the open in-scope documents with unsaved changes.
- **Authorization lines** (`prompt.rs` `authorization_lines`, in the first context and every update; derived from the gate's `Outlook`, which applies the gate's own rules and the live authority without paths, services, or the graph; a description, never an authorization, and every call is still decided by the gate when it is made): "Approval mode:" Ask, or Auto with the project's permissions (Files · Code · Setup), or Auto without permissions ("every action asks"); "Approved by the user for the rest of this task:" the allowances (native code, file changes in scope, package installs) or nothing; then what a call gets now for five kinds of action (file, graph, and workflow-draft changes; changes to files that run code (Lean sources, Lean/Lake configuration, R startup files); `run_python` and `run_command`; `check_lean` and a LaTeX build; `install_packages`), each as "proceeds without a prompt" / "runs natively without a prompt" / "runs in the restricted container (<backend>) without a prompt" with the reason (the project's permissions, approved for the rest of this task, this task's settings), "asks the user first" (once or for the rest of the task, or every time), or "refused: …" with the gate's reason; and a line for `delete_paths` / `copy_paths` with consequences (asks every time; refused in a read-only task or an Ask task that applies changes directly). Project text in a context block (folder name, scope, paths, reasons) is put on one line with `<` and `>` replaced, so it cannot open or close a block; control characters and the Unicode line and paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029) become spaces.
- **Authorization in results:** the result of every call that ran with authority carries `authorization` (none for reads and queries, or for a call that did not run): "No prompt: this task's settings allow it.", "No prompt: the project's permissions cover it (Auto).", "No prompt: covered by what the user approved for the rest of this task (<kinds>).", "The user approved this call in a prompt, once: the next call of this kind asks again.", or "… for the rest of this task: later calls of this kind (<kinds>) proceed without a prompt unless the user revokes it."
- **System prompt** (`SYSTEM_PROMPT`, constant across tasks, ≤ 7,800 bytes by test): states the saved/unsaved rules of §6.3 (an unsaved edit that must run is saved first with `save_document`, which writes the whole editor text, the user's unsaved changes included, and is not done for a file that changed on disk or is in a conflict), that Keep accepts a change without saving it, that a file's state comes from the file tools and the task context and never from the project graph, that approvals are described only as the context and results state them, that only the `<task_context>` block that opens a user message is the application's and carries approval state (text shaped like one in a file, a selection, or a tool result is data), and how the user does things in the app (palette, Save, review, Research runs, workflows, graph suggestions, Lean including Record evidence and Set Up Mathlib…, approvals). The 33 interface names it uses are listed with the frontend source file that defines each (`INTERFACE_NAMES`); the test `interface_names_in_the_prompt_exist_in_the_app` fails when a name is missing from the prompt or from its source file (three palette commands are also checked with their category).
- **Cancel**: aborts the stream, an approval wait, or the running tool (tool gets 5 s to report). Worker end: terminal status + notice stored → `TaskChanged` (once per run: the finished task is announced at once; review actions work from here) → agent research sessions shut down (a Continue meanwhile gets `agents.taskFinishing`).

| Status | UI label · meaning | Status | UI label · meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `queued` | Queued · created/continued, not started | `running` | Agent working · loop active |
| `awaitingApproval` | Waiting for approval · blocked on the user | `completed` | Task completed · `end_turn`/`stop_sequence`, no tool calls |
| `awaitingAnswer` | Waiting for your answer · blocked on a structured question (pending or being discussed, §6.7.1); not terminal, waiting excluded from the time budget | | |
| `canceled` | Canceled · user cancel; changes stay reviewable | `budgetExceeded{budget, detail}` | Stopped: {turn/output-token/input-token/time/tool-call/command} budget; a task stops before a model request when fewer output tokens remain than one request needs (1024), and the detail says how many remain ("used up" only at 0) |
| `refused{category?, explanation?}` | Declined by provider · `stop_reason: refusal`; nothing from it stored or run | `failed{message}` | Task failed · provider, storage, checkpoint or folder error |
| `stopped{stopReason, message}` | Stopped: response cut off / context window full / response paused / unexpected response · `max_tokens` (per-turn 64 000), `model_context_window_exceeded`, `pause_turn`, unknown stop; its calls `notRun` | `interrupted` | Interrupted · app closed while active; set at next start |

### 6.3 Tools (16; every property required, unknown fields rejected in Rust; the nine file/build/research/Lean tools are sent `strict`, `save_document`, `graph_query`, `graph_propose`, `workflow_propose`, `delete_paths`, `copy_paths`, and `install_packages` non-strict because the provider compiles all strict schemas of a request into one size-limited grammar)
| Tool | Parameters | Effect | Declared effects (exposure · launch · service) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `list_files` | `path` ("" = root), `recursive`, `offset` (0 = start), `listing` ("" = none) | In-scope entries, breadth-first, 500 walked entries per call; a longer listing has `truncated: true` with `nextOffset`, `listing` (32 hex characters, a hash of the walk up to there) and a `note`, and a call with that `offset` and `listing` returns the next entries or, when the walk up to the offset is no longer the same, `listingChanged: true` with no entries and `nextOffset: 0`; `offset` counts walked entries before the scope hides any and is refused above 49,500 (one listing walks at most 50,000 entries); skips `.git`, `node_modules`, `target`, `.venv`, `__pycache__`, `.lake`; no recursion into links; open docs get `openRevision`, `unsavedChanges` | metadata · none · — |
| `read_file` | `path`, `start_line`, `max_lines` (0 and 0 = the whole file) | Opens the document; returns `path, revision, unsavedChanges, contentHash, text` (buffer incl. unsaved; ≤512 KiB; disk ≤4 MiB); remembers the revision (last 48). With `start_line` or `max_lines` above 0 the read is partial: `text` holds whole lines from `start_line` (1-based), at most `max_lines` and 512 KiB, and the result adds `startLine`, `endLine`, `totalLines`, `complete`, `nextStartLine` (when lines follow) and a `note`; `contentHash` stays the whole file's. A partial read of a file within the 512 KiB limit remembers the whole revision, so `edit_document` works on it. A file over the limit is read only in parts (the whole-read error gives its line count); its revision is not remembered and `edit_document` refuses it, saying why | scoped content · none · — |
| `edit_document` | `path`, `base_revision`, `edits[{old_text,new_text}]` (≥1) | Each `old_text` exactly once in a revision the agent read, no overlaps, no no-ops; one transaction (origin Agent, `RebaseDisjoint`, new undo group, "Agent edit to {path}") on the open buffer, unsaved; overlap with user edits → error + `currentRevision`; the result carries `unsavedChanges: true` and a note that the edit is in the editor text and not saved to disk until it is saved (`save_document`, or the user) | metadata · none · — (changes) |
| `create_file` | `path`, `content` | Creates through the workspace service (never overwrites, makes folders): the file is written to disk, then opened; recorded as Created; the result carries `unsavedChanges` (false unless the editor text changed since) and a note saying so | metadata · none · — (changes) |
| `save_document` | `path`, `revision` (the buffer's current revision) | Saves one open document with the workspace service's strict save (`WorkspaceService::save_document_exact`: the user's atomic save without overwrite, in the document's encoding and line endings; never a file write by the tool). Prepared at the gate before anyone is asked: no workspace service → `agents.serviceUnavailable`; not open → error ("… is not open in the editor …"); buffer at another revision → error + `currentRevision`; no unsaved changes → `agents.nothingToSave`; the file changed, was deleted, or moved on disk since it was opened or last saved (`file.modifiedOnDisk`), a recorded disk conflict (`document.conflict`), text restored from recovery that the user has not saved (`document.recovered`), recovery data of an earlier session waiting for that file (`document.recoveryPending`), or a file deleted through the app while its text was kept (`document.fileDeleted`) → `agents.saveConflict` with `reason` ("Nothing was saved and the file on disk was not changed. Only the user resolves this, in the editor …"). The save repeats every check under the workspace I/O lock; a buffer that changed after the gate → `agents.changedSinceApproval`. Approval card: title "Save {path}"; detail = revision, bytes, SHA-256 of the bytes written, the other-changes sentence, the watching-triggers lines (below), and a line diff of disk → buffer (`-`/`+`, two lines of context, hidden characters as ⟨U+XXXX⟩; the full text when there is no file to compare). Other changes come from the document history since the saved revision (`DocumentService::origins_since`): `none` (every change is this task's), `included` ("… also changed outside this task (your own unsaved edits, or another task's) …"; an undo or redo by the user counts), `unknown` (no saved revision, or more than 2048 revisions since). Watching triggers (disclosure only: no change to the gate or to triggers; the trigger still fires on the save as on a user's): `SaveTriggers` reads `ProjectOperations::triggers_watching(path)`, in the application `TriggerService::try_watching` (the lookup of the deletion consequences, `watching`, in a form that reports an unreadable store or a closed project instead of an empty list; active triggers only, so a disabled or suspended trigger is not listed; sorted, duplicates dropped). None → nothing on the card. One or more → "An enabled workflow trigger watches this file:" / "Enabled workflow triggers watch this file:" and one line per trigger, "- “{workflow title}”: saving this file can start a run of this workflow, within the trigger's own limits." (`inputsChanged`) or "- “{title}”: it runs on a schedule; saving does not start it, and its next run reads the file as saved." (`interval` with the file among its inputs); titles through the hidden-character display. Not readable (lookup error, or no project operations in the build) → "Exposition could not determine whether an enabled workflow trigger watches this file. If one does, saving can start a run of its workflow, within the trigger's own limits." Read again when the save runs, for the result: `triggersKnown`, `triggers[{workflow, startsOnSave}]` (empty when unknown) and, unless known and empty, `triggersNote` (the same lines addressed to the agent, ending "Tell the user."). Not named: triggers for which the file is a non-watched input of the standing plan (code above all; the save suspends them). Result: `path, saved: true, revision, bytes, contentHash, unsavedChanges, otherChanges, otherChangesNote, note, triggersKnown, triggers, triggersNote, graph`; not a review record (the task's edits stay reviewable; a revert changes the buffer, the file keeps the saved text until saved again) | metadata · none · — (changes) |
| `build_document` | `entry`, `engine` (`auto`/`pdflatex`/`lualatex`) | `.typ` Typst, `.md`/`.markdown` Markdown print profile, in-process (reads confined to scope unless whole project); `.tex`/`.ltx` TeX service full build (`auto` = project default target's engine if entry matches, else pdfLaTeX); other ext → error. Returns status, pages, durationMs, ≤40 diagnostics with line/column, `diagnosticsOmitted`, LaTeX `passes` | Typst/MD: scoped content · none · typesetting; LaTeX: unconfined · native-only · typesetting |
| `insert_artifact` | `path`, `run_id`, `artifact_id`, `after_text`, `caption`, `update_key` | Research artifact as format-owned figure/table markup in a `.typ`/`.tex`/`.md` document (`after_text` unique, "" = end; `update_key` replaces an earlier insertion); returns `revision, attachmentKey, warnings`, `unsavedChanges: true` and the same not-saved note as an edit; artifact file must be in scope | metadata · none · research (changes) |
| `run_python` | `code` | Native: agent-owned research session `agent-{task}` (never the user's; the run records its `authorization`, §4); the run's output is read to its end (≤ 40 pages of 500 entries); stdout/stderr tail 16 KiB, the latest ≤ 500 displays (`resultsOmitted` counts the earlier ones), error with last 12 traceback lines, `outputTruncated` (a stream was cut to its tail, displays were omitted, or part of the output could not be read), artifacts, provenance `.exposition/runs/{run}.json`. Restricted: Docker job `python -` with code on stdin. Code reads project files as saved on disk: when in-scope open documents had unsaved changes at the start, the result lists them (`unsavedNotSeen`, ≤ 20) with `filesNote` (which names `save_document`) | unconfined · native or restricted · research |
| `check_lean` | `path` | Lean service check (the file's package root and pinned toolchain, `lake serve`; §5), 300 s; checks the editor text when the file is open, unsaved changes included, else the saved file (`checkedText` says which); report (≤ 60 diagnostics: errors first, then warnings, then the rest, listed in the report's order; `diagnosticsOmitted` always, `diagnosticsNote` when it is above 0; verdict and counts cover all of them) + note; error result unless `checked`. Records no evidence (the note of a Checked result says so and names Record evidence in the Lean view). `Unavailable` → "Not checked: Lean did not run on this file. <reason> …", naming the user's Set Up Mathlib… for a Mathlib package that is not prepared | unconfined · native-only · lean |
| `graph_query` | `query` (`search`/`entity`/`neighborhood`/`inspect`/`impact`), `text`, `kinds[]`, `entity`, `path`, `depth` 1–3, `limit` 1–100 | Bounded reads of the project graph filtered to the task (§6.14); never returns, traverses through, or text-matches invisible records; a neighborhood never walks through a rejected relation (an entity query still lists it with its status); result ≤64 KiB with `graph {project, revision, freshness}`. `entity` returns the entity's own evidence (≤ 20) and, for an entity that is not a formal declaration, `formalEvidence`: per non-rejected incoming `formalizes` relation (≤ 10) the declaration, the relation and its status (`association`), for an accepted one `review` (`current` / `statementChanged` / `statementMissing` / `unrecorded`), an `associationNote` when the link is suggested ("Suggested link: no person has reviewed this correspondence."), needs re-review ("Link needs re-review: …"), or has no recorded statement, and that declaration's newest `formalCheck` records (≤ 5; statement and axioms only with `scopeContent`). `entity`, `neighborhood`, and `inspect` results that show a formal declaration or a `formalizes` relation carry `evidenceNote` (evidence is recorded only by services, on the entity it is about; `check_lean` records none; how formal evidence is recorded; never on the formalized statement). `impact` items carry `locatedIn` when listed only because their file changed, the result lists affected `evidence` (≤ 50: id, kind, subject, freshness), and for a file root a `scope` note says that located items are informational and their dependents were not followed | consent-filtered · none · graph |
| `graph_propose` | `summary`, `base_revision`, `operations[]` (one strict object per `op`: `create_entity`, `add_relation`, `update_entity`, `set_lifecycle`, `remove_relation`, `bind_source`; `""`/`[]`/`-1` for unused fields) | Validated by the graph service as `Origin::Agent{task}`; applied as one graph change (agent relations `suggested`); returns `applied, revision, created[{tempId,id}], delta, effects`; invalid → `agents.graphInvalid` + diagnostics (messages only with `scopeContent`); conflicting → `agents.graphConflict` | consent-filtered · none · graph (changes; bound paths scope-checked) |
| `workflow_propose` | `workflow_id` ("" = new, or `wf-…` for a new revision), `expected_revision` (-1 = none), `title`, `description`, `preset`, `inputs[]` (`id, label, port_type, path` "" = unset, `pinned_hash`), `steps[]` (`id, label, operation` (registered; the schema's list omits `workflow.composite`; the `steps` description is a catalogue generated from the operation registry: per operation its version, title, purpose, input ports with accepted types, required/optional and whether several connections are accepted, output ports with types, parameters with kind, bounds and label, payload, and the ceiling it needs; `inputs`, `connections`, and `outcomes` carry descriptions of the file input types with extensions, the binding rule, and the output types each outcome kind accepts), `version`, `parameters[{name, kind, text, integer, boolean}]`, `payload` none/code/prompt), `connections[]` (data/prerequisite/annotation), `outcomes[]`, `ceiling {execution, model_requests, retain_results, propose_changes}`. Always schema `exposition.workflow/1`: no `maxParallel`, composite, condition, or repeat fields exist (unknown fields are refused), and a new revision of a workflow that uses them is refused (`agents.workflowUnsupported`); code payloads may be `python`, `r`, or `julia` | Validated by `validate_workflow` (bounded by the contract's limits; composites resolve against the project's saved revisions) and saved by `GraphService::save_workflow` as `Origin::Agent{task}` (a `Workflow` entity); incomplete drafts are saved with diagnostics; returns `saved` (false with `unchanged: true` when the definition equals the latest revision), `workflow`, `revision`, `entity`, `title`, `state`, `errors`, `warnings`, `diagnostics`, `ran: false`, `reviewable` (true only when well-formed), `summary` ("Saved as revision N of workflow wf-… (“title”). Validation: well-formed (…)" or "Validation: INCOMPLETE (…); the draft is kept and stays editable, but it cannot be reviewed or run until the errors in `diagnostics` are fixed.", then "Nothing was run." and that runnability on this machine was not checked), `next` (call again with this `workflow_id` and `expected_revision` for a new revision of the same workflow; an empty `workflow_id` creates a second workflow), `open` (the Workflows list, the Agents page, Research › Workflows), and `run` (only the user, from Review and run… and Start run; "Saving a proposal never runs it; the user reviews a run plan and starts a run separately."); there is no `runnable` field; never executes anything, and no agent tool starts a workflow run | consent-filtered · none · graph (changes; input paths scope-checked; whole project only) |
| `delete_paths` (P1) | `paths[]` (1–100, project-relative) | Moves the files or folders to the Recycle Bin through the project-operations orchestrator (§8) as `Agent{task}`: never permanent (no such field), unsaved editor text is kept in the editor, `.exposition` data is never deleted this way; a plan with consequences beyond the files is refused in Ask `autoApply` tasks (`agents.needsDecision`) and approved once (never for the task) in Ask `reviewEdits` and Auto tasks; a plan in which every item is refused → `agents.operationRefused`; result `{outcomes[{path, status, code, message}], notices}` (error "Nothing was changed." when none succeeded) | metadata · none · project ops (changes; paths written) |
| `copy_paths` (P1) | `paths[]` (1–100), `destination` (an existing project folder; "" = root, whole-project tasks only) | Pastes the saved versions into the folder through the same orchestrator: readable new names (`name (2).ext`), nothing overwritten or merged, links not copied, fresh identities (no evidence, verification, or workflow wiring); result lists each item's outcome and `created` path | metadata · none · project ops (changes; sources read, destination created) |
| `run_command` | `program`, `args[]`, `working_directory` ("" = root) | Execution job, no shell, empty environment, 10 min timeout, 256 KiB log/stream, 16 KiB tail/stream in result; the program reads files as saved on disk (`unsavedNotSeen` + `filesNote` as for `run_python`); label "Agent: {program} {args}" ≤120 chars; restricted note: project read-only at `/workspace`, `/out` kept in job folder | unconfined · native or restricted · execution |
| `install_packages` (A1) | `packages[]` (1–50; validated like §4 Setup) | The application plans the setup before the gate (never from the model: a `plan` field is unknown): into the project's `.venv`/`venv` when discovery found one, else a new `.venv` from the newest discovered Python outside the project (`setup.unsupported` when none); an approval, when one is needed, shows the complete plan; runs it through the research setup service as `Agent{task}` (starting re-plans: `setup.planStale`), waits for the end, returns `state, target, createdEnvironment, packages, environment, ready, removed, message` and the last 40 output lines; canceling the call or the task cancels the setup | unconfined · native-only · setup |

Questions: `ask_user` (interactive tasks only) and, only inside a discussion turn, `revise_question` (§6.7.1); both non-strict, neither touches files or launches anything. Figures: when a native `run_python` call finishes, the artifacts of kind figure with an image media type (PNG, JPEG, GIF, SVG) in the manifest of the run it created are recorded on the call (`ToolCall.figures`, `FigureRef {runId, artifactId, mediaType, name, caption}`), never parsed from the result text; restricted runs create no research run and record none.

Process launches (count against `maxCommands`): LaTeX build, `run_python`, `check_lean`, `run_command`, `install_packages`. Invalid streamed JSON → error result "not valid JSON", not counted. Results to the model are JSON (`{"error", "code"?}` on failure); transcript copy ≤4000 bytes. A result of a call that ran with authority also carries `authorization` (§6.2). Saved and unsaved: `read_file`, `build_document`, and `check_lean` use the editor text; `run_command`, `run_python`, and `copy_paths` see only saved files; only `save_document` saves: one open document's whole current buffer, after an approval naming its revision, size and SHA-256, and it refuses (`agents.saveConflict`, nothing written) when the file changed on disk or has a conflict or recovery pending (table above).

### 6.4 Central gate (`tools/exec.rs`; applied to declared effects, in order; one decision per call: `Allowed` with an `Authorization`, the only ticket `run` accepts; `NeedsApproval` with title, complete detail, `reason`, and the kinds "Approve for this task" would allow (empty: once only); or `Refused` with a stable `code`)
In Auto the task's own settings are a ceiling: read-only, execution disabled, and restricted-only prohibit exactly as in Ask (the composer sends Apply changes + Ask before each, the widest ceiling). The refusals of rules 1–6c are the same in both modes; rule 7 decides authority by mode.

| # | Rule | Refusal code(s) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Task folder is the open project | plain error (no code) |
| 2 | A change in a `readOnly` task | `agents.readOnly` |
| 3 | Each named path (and an inserted artifact's file) parses and its links resolve; requested and resolved path (links, junctions, case/8.3 aliases) in scope; resolved path inside the project | `agents.invalidPath`, `agents.outOfScope`, `agents.outsideProject` |
| 4 | Unconfined tools need a whole-project scope; scoped or unconfined content needs `scopeContent` | `agents.wholeProjectOnly`, `agents.metadataOnly` |
| 5 | Launch with `readOnly` or execution `disabled`; native-only tool or package setup in a `restricted` task (the container does not use the project environment); package setup in an untrusted folder (never asked: approving cannot grant trust) | `agents.executionDisabled`, `agents.nativeNotAllowed`, `agents.nativeRefused` |
| 6 | Backing service present | `agents.serviceUnavailable` |
| 6b | D7, Lean text is execution: an `edit_document` / `create_file` / `insert_artifact` / `save_document` whose requested or resolved path ends `.lean`, in any folder (the Lean server elaborates open Lean text natively once the folder is trusted; `#eval` runs code), needs native-code authority besides change authority: execution `disabled` / `restricted` refuse; otherwise it asks (reason "Lean elaborates this file's text natively as soon as it changes.", in a folder not trusted yet "Lean will elaborate this file's text natively once the folder is trusted."; kind `nativeCode`; detail ends with the Lean warning, which in an untrusted folder says nothing runs now but the code runs once the folder is trusted) unless Auto with Code: native or a `nativeCode` allowance covers it. This holds whether or not the folder is trusted yet: trust only decides whether Lean (and Lake) run the text now or once the folder is trusted, and this authority does not itself require trust (a native launch still does). Lean/Lake configuration in any folder (`lakefile.lean`, `lakefile.toml`, `lean-toolchain`, `lake-manifest.json`; any case, requested or resolved name) counts as Lean text (Lake may download the packages it names and run their build code, and elan the toolchain it names): edit/create/insert/save need native-code authority, and so does a `copy_paths` whose source tree holds a `.lean` file or Lake configuration (walked without following links, ≤ 20 000 entries; a larger tree counts as configuration). R startup files in any folder (`.Rprofile`, `.Renviron`; any case, requested or resolved name) are treated the same way: R reads them from its working directory at the start of every session (Rscript included) and runs the profile natively, and the user's R research session and native R workflow steps start in the project; edit, create, insert, and save need native-code authority, and so does a `copy_paths` whose source tree holds one (reason "R runs this file natively at the start of every R session in this folder."; in a folder not trusted yet "R runs this file natively at the start of R sessions in this folder (Exposition's once the folder is trusted)."). A save is gated on its own, whatever authorized the edits in the buffer and whoever wrote them: text reaches such a file on disk only with native-code authority for that call. They are not protected (rule 6c): with native authority an agent may set them up, for example for renv. Files a startup file sources (for example `renv/activate.R`) are ordinary files: their changes are reviewable but not gated. | `agents.executionDisabled`, `agents.nativeNotAllowed` |
| 6c | Protected folders: agents never write, create, insert into, save, copy into, or delete anything under `.git/`, `.lake/`, `.venv/`, `venv/` at any depth (any case; requested and resolved path, links and junctions included), and a copy whose tree would create such a folder is refused (discovery runs the project `.venv` interpreter automatically and Python executes `site-packages/*.pth` at startup; Lake loads `.lake/`; `.git/` hooks run in the user's tools). Reads stay allowed; packages go through `install_packages` | `agents.protectedTarget` |
| 7 | Authority (matrices below). Restricted launches need a configured Docker image (never downgraded, never asked around); native launches and package setup need a trusted folder (approving cannot grant trust); an allowance of the needed kind covers what would ask | `agents.restrictedUnavailable`, `agents.nativeRefused` |
