| 3a | Path policy: the graph's own storage is never a file-tool target; run records, generated artifacts, and other `.exposition/` metadata (except `.exposition/research/` and `project.json`) are never written or created, nor used as a `run_command` working folder — compared case-insensitively on both the requested and the resolved path (links, junctions, 8.3 aliases) | `agents.graphStorage`, `agents.serviceOwned` |
| 6a | The project graph is usable (attached or temporary), of the task's folder, with the identity recorded with the task; every target path is not pending reconciliation (`coverage = pending`, binding `missing`) or ambiguous — after a bounded wait (3 s) for reconciliation | `agents.graphUnavailable`, `agents.projectChanged`, `agents.graphPending`, `agents.graphAmbiguous` (calls recorded `notRun`) |
| 7a | `graph_propose`: every referenced entity/relation/bound file visible to the task; narrower scopes bind new entities to a file in scope; no investigations/workflows; the graph validator accepts the operations (approval card = every effect + every operation) | `agents.outOfScope`, `agents.graphReservedKind`, `agents.graphInvalid` |
| 7b | `workflow_propose`: whole-project task; the definition is well formed; its authority ceiling fits the task's (execution: disabled → none, restricted → restricted, ask-each → trusted native; model requests only with `scopeContent`; retain/propose only when not read-only); nothing wired into a service-owned result (rule 6); a new revision of a workflow whose current revision uses a schema /2 feature is refused (a `/2` definition that uses none can be revised and is saved as `/1`) (`agents.workflowUnsupported`: "Workflow <id> (revision N) uses features agents cannot edit (parallel steps, composite steps, conditions, or repeats). Saving an agent revision would remove them, so nothing was saved; ask the user to make this change, or propose a new workflow instead."); approval card = title, "Saving runs nothing: the workflow runs only if you later review its run plan and start it yourself.", validation summary + every diagnostic, the complete definition | `agents.wholeProjectOnly`, `agents.workflowInvalid`, `agents.workflowCeiling`, `agents.workflowServiceOwned`, `agents.workflowUnsupported` |
| 7c | `delete_paths` / `copy_paths` (P1): paths pass rules 3 and 3a (`.exposition/…` → `agents.serviceOwned`); copying into the root needs a whole-project task (`agents.outOfScope`); the orchestrator plans the operation as `Agent{task}` (agents never import external files, delete or copy app records, stop work, or turn triggers off: `ops.agentScope`); a plan without effects → `agents.operationRefused` (with every refusal); a plan that needs a decision in an Ask `autoApply` task → `agents.needsDecision` (with the impacts; nothing changed: "ask the user to do it, or to run the task with review of each change"); Ask `reviewEdits` and every Auto task: one approval card, never "for this task" (reason "It has consequences beyond the files themselves; only you decide those.") — "Move N items to the Recycle Bin" / "Copy N items into <folder>", each effect, "Not changed:" refusals, "Consequences:" impacts with their consumers, notes, each choice with its default, and "Items go to the Recycle Bin, never deleted permanently."; applied with the default choices; no allowance kind of its own and no widened grant | `agents.serviceOwned`, `agents.outOfScope`, `agents.operationRefused`, `agents.needsDecision` |
| 8 | After approval: same project folder still open; gate re-run yields the identical payload under the same basis (Auto: the project permission version; both modes: the task's allowance version); immediately before the call runs the basis is read again | `agents.projectChanged`, `agents.changedSinceApproval`, `agents.permissionsChanged` |

**Ask** (the task's own settings decide; anything they do not cover asks; the allowance kind in parentheses is what "Approve for this task" grants):

| Tool | Execution off | Ask before each | Restricted (Docker) | Scope and consent |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `list_files`, `read_file` | allowed | allowed | allowed | path in scope; read needs `scopeContent` |
| `edit_document`, `create_file`, `insert_artifact`, `save_document` | allowed¹ | allowed¹ | allowed¹ | path (and artifact file) in scope; a save also needs an open document with unsaved changes and no conflict or recovery state (`agents.saveConflict`, never asked) |
| the same into Lean text, Lake configuration, or an R startup file, any folder (rule 6b) | refused (`executionDisabled`) | approval (`nativeCode`; with `reviewEdits` also `documentChanges`) | refused (`nativeNotAllowed`) | as above |
| `delete_paths`, `copy_paths` | allowed¹² | allowed¹² | allowed¹² | paths (and destination) in scope; file names only suffice |
| `build_document` Typst/Markdown | allowed | allowed | allowed | entry in scope; build reads only scope; `scopeContent` |
| `build_document` LaTeX, `check_lean` | refused | trusted folder + approval (`nativeCode`) | refused (`nativeNotAllowed`) | whole project + `scopeContent` |
| `run_python`, `run_command` | refused | trusted folder + approval (`nativeCode`) | unattended in container (`restrictedUnavailable` without an image) | whole project + `scopeContent`; working folder in scope |
| `install_packages` | refused | trusted folder + approval of the complete plan (`packageSetup`) | refused (`nativeNotAllowed`) | whole project + `scopeContent` |

¹ `readOnly`: refused; `reviewEdits`: approval first (`documentChanges`); `autoApply`: applied directly ("Allowed by task settings"). ² `autoApply`: refused with `agents.needsDecision` when the plan needs a decision (rule 7c); `reviewEdits`: such a plan asks once, never for the task. Process tools in a `readOnly` task: `agents.executionDisabled`. Native launches in an untrusted folder: `agents.nativeRefused`, never asked.

**Auto** (the project's permissions, §6.5, within the ceiling; covered calls proceed without a card and are recorded "Auto: project permissions"; anything else legitimate asks once, with "Approve for this task" where the kind is repeatable; the task's allowances cover what would ask):

| Action | Proceeds when | Otherwise | Refused |
|---|---|---|---|
| Changes in scope (edit, create, insert, save, graph and workflow proposals, routine delete/copy) | Files: edit | one approval (`documentChanges`); reason "Changing files is beyond this project's permissions (Files: off)." | read-only ceiling |
| Delete/copy with consequences (rule 7c) | never | one approval, never for the task | — |
| `run_python`, `run_command` | Code: native (natively, trusted folder); Code: restricted (in the container) | Code: off → a native run after one approval (`nativeCode`) | untrusted folder for a native run (`nativeRefused`); Code: restricted without an image (`restrictedUnavailable`; never asked around, never native) |
| `build_document` LaTeX, `check_lean` (native only) | Code: native, trusted folder | one approval (`nativeCode`) | untrusted folder (`nativeRefused`) |
| Lean text, Lake configuration, or R startup files, any folder (rule 6b) | Code: native (and Files: edit); no trust needed (nothing runs until the folder is trusted) | one approval (`nativeCode`; with Files: off also `documentChanges`) | — |
| `install_packages` | Setup on (requires Code: native), trusted folder | one approval of the complete plan (`packageSetup`) | untrusted folder (`nativeRefused`) |

An Auto task without the project's record is refused at create and continue (`agents.permissionsRequired`); with a restricted ceiling (IPC only) a restricted launch proceeds under Code: restricted or native and otherwise asks once, never for the task. Workflow agent steps are always Ask (§7.7). Nothing a model sends changes authority: no tool, field, or result sets permissions, allowances, the approval mode, or trust; unknown tool names and fields are refused before anything runs. Verified by `tests/approval_modes.rs` (every tool × Ask/Auto × task settings or project profile × folder trust × allowances, against an independent statement of the rules, plus the real task loop with the scripted provider), `tests/central_gate.rs` (every tool × 3 modes × 3 profiles × 3 scopes × 2 consents, with the project graph attached) and `tests/graph_agents.rs` (`graph_query`/`graph_propose` × the same matrix; `graph_query` is allowed in every mode, an `inspect` path must be in scope; `graph_propose` follows the document-change column).

### 6.5 Task settings
| Setting | Values (composer label) | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Approval mode (A1) | `ask` (Ask, default; also every task stored before A1) · `auto` (Auto) — composer "Approvals" | Ask: the settings below decide and anything they do not cover asks; Auto: the project's permissions decide within the task's ceiling (the composer sends `autoApply` + `approveEach` and hides Document changes / Commands and code) |
| Permission | `reviewEdits` (Ask before each change, default) · `autoApply` (Apply changes, review afterwards) · `readOnly` (Read only) | how documents may change |
| Execution | `disabled` (No commands, default) · `approveEach` (Ask before each command (runs natively)) · `restricted` (Restricted container only (unattended)) | how processes may run |
| Scope | `workspace` (Whole project) · `document{path}` (One document, defaults to active editor) · `files{paths}` (Selected files and folders; exact path or under a folder) · `selection{path, range, revision}` (IPC only, not in composer) | paths the agent may use |
| Consent | `scopeContent` (File contents in scope) · `metadataOnly` (File names only) | what content may reach the provider |

**Project permissions** (Auto; `crates/exposition-agents/src/permissions.rs`; stored app-locally, §9): `files` `edit` ("Edit files in scope") · `off` ("Ask before each change"); `code` `off` ("Ask before running code") · `restricted` ("Restricted container") · `native` ("Native on this computer"); `setup` (checkbox "Install packages into the project environment (needs network; installers run natively)", only with Code native, else `agents.invalidPermissions`). One record per project, bound to its canonical folder and its project-graph identity: a copy, a move, an import, a generated project, or another project in the same folder has none. Only the user sets one (composer Options › Permissions, Settings › Model providers); without one the composer offers the defaults Files edit, Code restricted when a container image is configured (else off), Setup off — native is never pre-selected. Every change gets a larger `version`. Narrowing or revoking stops the project's active Auto tasks (notice "Project permissions changed (…), so this Auto task was stopped. What it did before stopping was not undone and stays reviewable."; their tool jobs, research runs, agent sessions, and package setup are canceled), waiting at most 10 s in all and reporting what it could not confirm ("“<task>” is still stopping.", "its research session … has not stopped", "its package setup (…) is still running"); Ask tasks and other projects are not affected; a widening stops nothing. Narrowing or revoking also clears the allowances of every Auto task of the project, active or not (notice "Project permissions changed (…), so what you approved for the rest of this Auto task was cleared."; the allowance version changes, so a call they authorized that has not started is refused), so a later Continue under the narrower permissions asks again instead of running on what was approved under the broader grant; Ask tasks keep their allowances; a clearing that fails is reported with the change's problems.

### 6.6 Budgets (`agents.invalidBudgets` when out of range)
| Budget | Default | Min–max | Enforced |
|---|---|---|---|
| `maxTurns` | 40 | 1–500 | before each request |
| `maxOutputTokens` | 400 000 | 1 024–20 000 000 | before each request (<1 024 left); budget-limited `max_tokens` stop → `budgetExceeded` (only when the remaining budget, not the per-turn limit or the model's own smaller output limit, was what the request asked for; otherwise `stopped`, naming the limit that applied) |
| `maxInputTokens` | 4 000 000 | 1 024–200 000 000 | before each request (input + cache read + cache write); not editable in UI |
| `maxWallMs` | 1 800 000 | 1 000–86 400 000 | races stream and tools; approval waits excluded; UI field "Minutes" |
| `maxToolCalls` | 150 | 0–5 000 | per call; rest of the batch `notRun` |
| `maxCommands` | 20 | 0–1 000 | per process launch |

### 6.7 Approvals
- Raised whenever the gate decides `NeedsApproval` (§6.4: Ask — native launches, changes in `reviewEdits`, package setup, Lean text; Auto — anything beyond the project's permissions, consequential deletions and copies): `ApprovalRequest {id "approval-{uuid}", taskId, toolUseId, tool, title, detail, reason?, forTask[]}`; memory only; no timeout. Detail is the complete payload (nothing truncated); invisible and direction-changing characters shown as `⟨U+XXXX⟩` with a note; native launches end "Runs natively with your account's privileges."; Lean text adds "Lean elaborates the text of an open Lean file natively as soon as it changes: code in it (for example #eval) runs on this computer with your account's privileges." (in a folder not trusted yet: "… This folder is not trusted yet, so nothing runs now; once it is trusted, code in this text … runs …"; Lake configuration has the matching Lake texts; R startup files add "R runs this file at the start of every R session in this folder (also Rscript), natively with your account's privileges.", untrusted: "… This folder is not trusted yet, so Exposition starts no R session here now; R started here any other way still runs it."). `reason` says why it asks when the settings do not make it obvious (e.g. "Running code natively is beyond this project's permissions (Code: restricted).", "This project has no Auto permissions.", "Installing packages is beyond this project's permissions (Setup: off)."). `forTask` lists the allowance kinds "Approve for this task" grants (`nativeCode`, `documentChanges`, `packageSetup`); empty for a once-only approval (consequential deletions and copies, restricted launches under a restricted ceiling).
- Titles: "Apply edits to {path}" (each replacement −/+), "Create {path}" (bytes + content), "Insert artifact {a} of run {r} into {path}" / "Update attachment {key} in {path} with artifact {a} of run {r}" (placement + exact text + key), "Build {entry} with LaTeX", "Run Python code", "Check {path} with Lean", "Run {program}" (Program / Arguments / Working folder), "Move {n} items to the Recycle Bin" / "Copy {n} items into {folder}" (effects, refusals, consequences, choice defaults; §6.4 rule 7c), "Create .venv and install packages" / "Install packages into {folder}" (the plan's summary, target, index, packages, base, "Needs network; installs into the project environment. Packages run code when they are imported later.", and every command exactly as it runs).
- Approve (`agents_approval_resolve`, scope `once`) → `confirm` re-runs the gate (rule 8; revoked trust → `nativeRefused`; changed project permissions or allowances → `agents.permissionsChanged`); a refused call is recorded `failed` with its code; an insertion applies exactly the shown text or nothing. **Approve for this task** (scope `task`; only when `forTask` is non-empty, else `agents.notRepeatable` and the request stays pending) also stores those kinds as task allowances once the call is confirmed, bound to the task's folder and graph identity ("Approved once: the approval for the rest of the task could not be saved (…)." if storing fails); later calls of those kinds in the task are allowed without a card ("Allowed for this task"). Allowances are listed under Options › Permissions ("Allowed for this task") and revocable there (`agents_allowance_revoke`, one kind or all); every grant or revocation changes the task's allowance version, so a call authorized under the old one is refused before it runs; revoking folder trust clears the allowances of every task in that folder (notice "Folder trust was revoked, so what you approved for the rest of this task was cleared."; trusting again does not restore them); narrowing or revoking a project's permissions clears the allowances of that project's Auto tasks, active or not, and Ask tasks keep theirs (§6.5). Decline → `declined`, loop continues. Cancel while waiting → `ApprovalResolved(false)`, remaining calls `notRun`, task `canceled`; a graph proposal that was waiting for that approval is recorded `failed` ("The task was canceled before this proposal was answered: it was not approved, and nothing was applied."), as it is for any other end of the run, and at the next start for a task the application closed on ("Exposition restarted before …"), so no proposal stays "Waiting for approval" without an approval to answer. Unknown id → `agents.noApproval`.

### 6.7.1 Structured questions (`questions.rs`)
- Tool `ask_user` (non-strict; offered only to interactive tasks: never to workflow review tasks, which are created with `create_unattended_task`, nor to restricted-only tasks; a call there is an error result): `questions[]` 1–3, each `prompt` (≤ 300 chars), `explanation` ("" = none, ≤ 600), `options[]` 2–5 (`id` `[a-z0-9_-]{1,32}` unique per question, `label` ≤ 80, `consequence` ("" = none, ≤ 200), `recommended`, at most one per question), `allowCustom` (default true). Invalid input is an ordinary error result. Questions get ids `q1`…`q3`.
- The call blocks the worker like an approval wait: `QuestionRequest {id "question-{uuid}", taskId, toolUseId, revision 1, questions, state, createdAtMs}` is stored, `questionRequested` is emitted, the task is `awaitingAnswer`, and the rest of the response's tool calls stay `pending` (held). The waiting time is excluded from the time budget. The result given to the model is `{"q1": {"optionId": …} | {"custom": …}, …}`; when the user discussed the question or its options were revised, the same user message also carries a `<question_discussion …>` text block with the final questions and the discussion (not shown as the user's text).
- `agents_question_answer(task, request, revision, answers[])`: exactly one answer per question, known option ids, custom text 1–2 000 chars only where allowed (`agents.invalidAnswer`); `revision` must be current (`agents.questionStale`); the request must be pending or discussing. A second identical answer is accepted and changes nothing; a different one after resolution, or any answer to a superseded request, → `agents.questionResolved`; unknown request → `agents.noQuestion`. An answer is user content and authorizes nothing: the gate decides every later call. If the task's project is no longer open, the request is superseded instead ("The project was closed or switched") and the held calls meet the gate's own checks.
- `agents_question_discuss(task, request, revision, text)` ("Chat about it", 1–4 000 chars): validated as above; one discussion at a time (`agents.questionBusy`). The state becomes `discussing`, the message is stored as a `discussion` transcript row (shown as `userText` with `question`), and the worker runs a bounded discussion turn (at most 3 model requests) counted in the task's budgets: the request carries the history, held results for the response's calls (the question's own with its id, revision, and current questions), and the discussion so far; the tools are `list_files`, `read_file`, `graph_query`, and `revise_question {request, questions}` (keeps the ids it is given, bumps `revision`, emits `questionChanged`); every other tool is refused there and nothing else in the batch runs. Replies are `assistantText` rows of the discussion (not part of the provider history). Afterwards the request is `pending` again. An answer during a discussion turn ends that turn.
- Cancel, a budget stop, a permission narrowing that stops an Auto task, or any other end of the run supersede open requests ("The task was canceled", "The task stopped: …"); a restart supersedes them with "Exposition restarted". Continue after that is an ordinary follow-up; the model may ask again. `questionResolved` is emitted for answers and supersessions.

### 6.8 Tool-call states
| State | Label · meaning | State | Label · meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pending` | Pending · in this response; an earlier call runs first | `running` | Running |
| `awaitingApproval` | Waiting for approval · has not run | `succeeded` | Done |
| `failed` | Failed (Unavailable if "not available in this build") · error result, incl. gate refusals | `declined` | Declined · user declined ("Do not retry it unchanged.") |
| `canceled` | Canceled · running when the task was canceled or its time ran out; result says how far | `notRun` | Not run · budget reached, response cut off, task stopped first, or app restarted |

How a call that ran was authorized (`AuthorizedBy`, recorded once it started; never for a refused, declined, or not-run call) shows as quiet meta on its row: `approved` "Approved by you" / "Approved by you for this task", `taskAllowance` "Allowed for this task", `projectPermissions {version}` "Auto: project permissions", `taskSettings` "Allowed by task settings"; `notRequired` (reads, queries) shows nothing.

### 6.9 Review, accept, revert
- Per file: checkpoint text, baseline (file without this task's changes; concurrent user edits rebased in), tip; `entangled` when a concurrent edit overlapped. States: `pending` (Pending review), `accepted` (Kept), `reverted` (Reverted), `undone` (Undone in editor: derived from current content, never stored; edited change absent or created file missing; redo makes it pending again; an undone revert shows pending).
- Keep / Keep all: refused while active (`agents.taskRunning`); all = effective-pending files only; reverted → "already reverted"; undone → "nothing to keep"; else "Kept."
- Revert / Revert all: refused while active; task folder must be open (`agents.workspaceNotOpen`); edited file: inverse diff as undoable transaction "Revert agent changes" (rebased over later edits, ≤3 retries), refused on overlap or entangled → "Reverted in the open document (not saved)."; created file: deleted only if buffer and disk hold only agent text → "Deleted the file the agent created."
- Diff: read-only Monaco diff, left = without this task's changes, right = after its latest change; side-by-side, inline below 700 px.
- Deletions and copies (P1, kinds `deleted` / `copied`, recorded only for succeeded outcomes): rows read "deleted by the agent (moved to the Recycle Bin)" / "copied by the agent", with a summary instead of a diff. Keep = "Kept.". A deleted item whose path holds something again, or a copy that no longer exists, reads undone ("Nothing to revert: …"). Revert of a deletion is **Restore**: the recorded restore token through `project_op_restore` (never overwriting; "Exposition cannot identify this item in the Recycle Bin; restore it from there." without a token). Revert of a copy is **Remove Copy**: only while its content digest equals the one recorded when it was made and the user's own Delete of it needs no decision; it moves the copy to the Recycle Bin ("Moved the copy the agent made to the Recycle Bin."), otherwise "The copy changed after the agent made it, so it was not removed; …" / "The copy is in use now (…); delete it yourself if you no longer need it."

### 6.10 Persistence and crash behaviour
- `<data dir>/agents.sqlite3` (WAL, foreign keys, 5 s busy timeout, schema v8; newer schema refused; each migration runs in one transaction; an older database is first copied aside with `VACUUM INTO` as `agents.sqlite3.premigration-v<N>-<ms>`, and is not upgraded when that copy fails). Tables: `tasks`, `transcript` (full provider history incl. file text read), `tool_calls`, `checkpoints`, `task_files`, `changes` (patches + inverse), `settings`; v2 adds `task_graph` (bound project graph id, kept investigation), `task_graph_contexts` (one per run), `task_targets`, `task_proposals`; v3 (P1) `tasks.deleted_at` (recoverable deletion: deleted tasks are left out of every list); v4 (P1) `task_files.op_ref` (a deletion's restore token or a copy's content digest); v5 (A1) `tool_calls.authorized_by` (JSON `AuthorizedBy`; unreadable reads as not recorded), `tasks.allowance_version`, and `task_allowances (task_id, kind, root, project, approved, granted_at)` (one row per kind; a kind this build does not know is not honored); tasks stored before v5 read as Ask (nothing migrates to Auto); v6 `attachments`; v7 `tasks.connection`; v8 (agent UI rework) `questions (task_id, id, tool_use_id, revision, questions, state, created_at, updated_at)`, `tool_calls.figures` (JSON `FigureRef[]`; unreadable reads as none), and `tasks.unattended` (a task created without a user in the loop), with discussion entries stored as `transcript` rows of kind `discussion`. Tasks are kept until the user deletes them (§8.4): an active task is refused (`agents.taskActive`) unless the operation stops it first; permanent deletion removes every row of the task and compacts the database (`VACUUM`); nothing is pruned automatically. Startup: active tasks → `interrupted`; `pending`/`running`/`awaitingApproval` calls → `notRun`; warning notice "Exposition closed while this task was running…". Pending approvals are lost (memory only). Continue resumes.
- Events: `agents:event` (17 kinds: taskChanged, transcriptChanged, streamStarted, textDelta, thinkingDelta, streamReset, toolCallStarted, toolCallFinished, approvalRequested, approvalResolved, questionRequested, questionChanged, questionResolved, fallback, reviewChanged, providerChanged, permissionsChanged); broadcast capacity 4096; lag → `agents:resync`. Consecutive `textDelta` (or `thinkingDelta`) events of one model request are joined: the first delta after a pause is sent at once, text that arrives within 25 ms of the last delta event is held and sent together, and held text is sent at 16 KiB whatever the time; a delta of the other kind, a reset, a fallback, and the end of the stream send the held text first, so nothing is dropped or reordered.

### 6.11 Credentials
- Windows: generic credential target `anthropic-api-key.org.exposition.desktop` (user `anthropic-api-key`), persistence local machine (not roaming). Persistence is never changed in place (Credential Manager would keep the old copy in its other store and bring it back on the next write of that persistence by any program): a roaming copy is deleted before the local one is written, and every save, move, and removal is checked after both stores reload (a throwaway roaming and a throwaway local credential `<target>.settle-roaming` / `.settle-local`, no secret, written and deleted at once), up to 4 attempts. A key saved with roaming persistence by an earlier version is moved to local-machine on first read; one moved in place by an earlier build has its leftover roaming copy removed on first read, keeping the local bytes. macOS Keychain / Linux keyutils via `keyring` (unverified). Debug builds: entry `org.exposition.desktop.development` unless `EXPOSITION_CREDENTIAL_NAMESPACE` (≤64 `[A-Za-z0-9-]`) or `EXPOSITION_USE_REAL_CREDENTIAL=1`; release builds ignore both and use the real entry.
- Key: trimmed, 16–512 ASCII graphic chars (IPC rejects >1 024 bytes); never returned, logged, stored in SQLite or project files; webview clears the field before saving (restored only on failure). Agent jobs start from an empty environment; provider errors replace the key with `<redacted>`; process output and text artifacts pass the execution redactor, which matches the stored key by value (registered when read or saved, removed when cleared) as well as by token shape. Trusted native code runs as the user and could read the stored key.

### 6.12 Agent error codes (22 + graph + project operations + approval modes)
- Provider/storage: `agents.storage` (SQLite/store or serialization), `agents.credentials` (credential store read/write/delete failed), `agents.notConfigured` (no API key for the chosen provider), `agents.provider` (HTTP client creation failed), `agents.providerUnavailable` (no OpenAI connection in this build), `agents.invalidModel`, `agents.invalidKey` (fails validation or > 1 024 bytes), `agents.invalidConnection` (OpenAI organization/project ID), `agents.modelUnsupported`, `agents.effortUnsupported`, `agents.attachmentUnsupported`, `agents.scriptedUnavailable` (scripted provider in release/disabled), `agents.invalidScript` (dev script invalid or > 1 MiB), `agents.noScript` (no dev script queued). Attachments: `attachments.*` (§6.1.1).
- Task requests: `agents.emptyPrompt`, `agents.promptTooLong` (> 100 000 chars), `agents.invalidWorkspace` (not absolute/existing/folder, or a root), `agents.workspaceNotOpen` (task folder not the open project), `agents.invalidBudgets`, `agents.invalidScope` (empty files scope; selection document not open or bad range), `agents.staleSelection`, `agents.invalidTask` (id not `task-[A-Za-z0-9-]` ≤ 64), `agents.invalidApproval` (approval id > 128 chars).
- Lifecycle: `agents.taskRunning` (continue/accept/revert while active), `agents.taskFinishing` (continue while the worker releases services), `agents.noApproval` (approval no longer pending), `agents.notFound` (unknown task, or file not changed by it).
- Project graph: `agents.graphRequired` (service configured with a workspace but no graph), `agents.graphUnavailable`, `agents.graphPending`, `agents.graphAmbiguous`, `agents.graphStorage`, `agents.serviceOwned`, `agents.graphInvalid`, `agents.graphConflict`, `agents.graphReservedKind`, `agents.workflowInvalid`, `agents.workflowCeiling`, `agents.workflowServiceOwned`, `agents.workflowUnsupported`; `agents.projectChanged` also when the folder's graph identity is not the task's.
- Project operations (P1): `agents.needsDecision` and `agents.operationRefused` (gate rule 7c); task records `agents.taskActive` ("The task is still running; stop it first."), `agents.alreadyDeleted`, `agents.notDeleted` (restore of a task that is not deleted).
- Approval modes (A1, gate codes): `agents.permissionsChanged` (the project's permissions or the task's allowances changed between the decision or approval and the run; "Call the tool again"), `agents.permissionsRequired` (an Auto task in a project without permissions, at create and continue), `agents.notRepeatable` ("Approve for this task" on a once-only approval), `agents.invalidPermissions` (Setup without Code native). `save_document`: `agents.saveConflict` (only the user decides what the file holds: changed, deleted, or moved on disk, a disk conflict, recovered text; `reason` carries the workspace code) and `agents.nothingToSave`. The permission commands also use `agents.invalidWorkspace`, `agents.workspaceNotOpen`, `agents.graphUnavailable` (no attached graph to identify the project), `agents.notFound` ("This project has no permissions to revoke."), and `agents.storage` (the permissions file could not be written, or an invalid one could not be set aside). `agents.protectedTarget` (rule 6c).

### 6.13 UI surfaces
Design C of the Agent UI rework ("Options sheet + turn outline"; `src/features/agents/`). Wording of statuses, approvals, execution labels, and plans is unchanged except where noted.

- **Agent view** (right panel, icon Bot): New task; provider chip "Claude · {model}" / "OpenAI · {model}" / "… · no API key configured" / "… · credential store error" / "Scripted provider — development only"; error banner "Agent action failed" (Dismiss). **Task list**: newest first; title, status, "{n} to review", relative time; empty "No agent tasks yet". An open task shows a compact header: back arrow "Back to tasks", title, the one status pill, and the task menu (**Task actions**: New task, Stop task (active; the same action as the composer's Stop), Review changes (disabled with no changed files), Open in Agents page, Show graph context (with a graph context), Delete Task Record).
- **Composer** (`TaskComposer.tsx`, one composer for a new task and a follow-up, anchored at the bottom of the Agent view and the Agents page): context line "{project} · {scope}" (pressing it shows the folder path); "No project folder is open" + Open Folder…; "The model provider is not configured" + Provider settings (opens Settings › Model providers) for a new task without a key; the request field (Request / Follow-up); attachment chips; **Attach files…** (paperclip); one **Options** button whose summary names model · effort · approvals · document changes (+ "· custom budgets"; "Scripted provider" for the scripted model), with a warning badge **Native** when native execution applies (Ask with native commands, Auto with Code: native, or a native-code allowance for the task); **Stop** (`agents-cancel`, active tasks) and **Send** (`agents-start` / `agents-continue`). Keys in the request field (hint "Enter to send · Shift+Enter for a new line" as the field's description): **Enter** sends through Send with Send's rules, once (a held key, a second Enter while a send runs, and the Enter that confirms an IME composition — `isComposing` or keyCode 229 — send nothing; when Send is unavailable Enter does nothing and the draft stays); **Shift+Enter** is a line break; Ctrl/Cmd+Enter also sends. Send is the one primary button unless the task is active. While a task works the field stays editable but nothing is queued: Send is disabled with "You can send when the task stops or asks you something". While a structured question waits (status Waiting for your answer) Send takes only a discussion message (below); otherwise it is disabled with "Answer the question above, or use Chat about it.". A refused send keeps the draft and shows "The task was not started" / "The follow-up was not sent" / "The message about the question was not sent" with the reason. Drafts (text, attachments, rejections, explicit model) are kept per conversation and shared by both views; only the submitted part is cleared after success. Attachments: as §6.1.1 (native multi-file dialog, pasted images "Pasted image N.png", rejected files listed with their reasons while accepted ones stay, "Adding attachments…" blocks sending). **Large paste** (`drafts.ts`: `LARGE_PASTE_CHARS` 8,000 UTF-16 units or `LARGE_PASTE_LINES` 100; at or below either, the field inserts the text itself): a text-only paste above the threshold is attached as "pasted-text-N.txt" (N per conversation) with the exact clipboard text as UTF-8, not inserted; the selection it would have replaced is removed only once the attachment exists and only if the text did not change meanwhile; the row "The pasted text was attached as {name}." offers **Insert as text** (the text goes where it was pasted and the attachment is removed; not offered when the request would exceed 100,000 characters) and **Undo** (the attachment is removed and the replaced text comes back); a failure leaves the request and selection as they were and lists "{name}: {reason} Nothing was pasted and the request is unchanged; the text is still on the clipboard."; with files or images on the clipboard the text is never also attached. **OS drop** (`dropAttach.ts`, a zone of the native drop router `workspace/dropTarget.ts`): files dragged from the operating system over a chat region (`[data-agent-chat]`: transcript and composer, new-task view) highlight that composer (`[data-drop-target]`; cleared on leave, cancel, drop, and when the composer goes away); the drop attaches to that composer's draft through `agents_attachments_add_dropped` and is never also a project import; "Folders cannot be attached. Attach the files inside it instead."; content already in the draft is refused ("Already attached."); results are bound to the draft dropped on and discarded when its project was closed or its task deleted meanwhile; nothing is sent. In a draft at most 20 attachments are kept ("A message can carry at most 20 attachments. …").
- **Options sheet** (one popover above the composer, tabs **Model / Permissions / Budgets**; Escape or an outside press closes it): *Model* — the model menu (§ below) and "The latest turn was served by {model}." when the provider served another model. *Permissions* — for a new task: **Approvals** Ask | Auto with one help line (Ask: "Asks before actions the settings below do not cover."; Auto: "Proceeds within this project's permissions and asks only for anything beyond them."); Ask: Document changes, Commands and code (Read only: No commands, disabled, "Read only also allows no commands or code."); Auto: the project-permissions block ("Files: edit · Code: restricted container · Setup: off", or "This project has no Auto permissions yet. Choose them before starting an Auto task." + "Suggested: …"; the editor (Files, Code, Setup) on Choose… / Change…; the execution statement as a banner — native warning "Runs on this computer with your account's privileges — not a sandbox; Exposition cannot confirm each action inside running code." (+ the untrusted-folder sentence), restricted info "Runs in the restricted container: no network, read-only project, non-root, no host environment; never falls back to native.", or the missing-image warning; Use these permissions / Save permissions, Revoke); Scope (Whole project, One document, Selected files and folders); Content sent to the model provider. For a started task the same tab shows the task's fixed settings ("This task": Approvals, Limited to, Document changes, Commands and code, Scope, Content sent), **Allowed for this task** with each allowance (Native code, File changes in scope, Package installs) and Revoke (the tab title counts them, "N allowed"), the project's Auto permissions, and the defaults for future tasks. *Budgets* — for a new task the fields Model turns, Tool calls, Commands, Output tokens, Minutes (input tokens fixed); for a started task the usage meter (6 bars, "Near limit"). A started task's settings never change from the sheet.
- **Model menu** (`ModelMenu.tsx`, the one menu used by the Options sheet and Settings › Model providers): every configured provider's models in one searchable list grouped under the provider's name (display name; exact ID as secondary text; badges Suggested, Deprecated, Unverified, Unavailable, "Saved, not listed", "Custom ID, not listed"; hints Images · PDF), then the chosen model's **Effort** as a discrete slider with only the model's levels; a provider without a key reads "Configure in Settings"; footer "Manage providers…" opens Settings › Model providers. States: loading, "{provider} did not list its models", "Could not load the list", "Listed {time}; refresh failed" (Refresh), not connected (the chosen model stays chosen). An unverified model needs "Use this unverified model" ticked; an unsupported one cannot be chosen; a saved model the catalog does not list stays selected and is never replaced; results from a previous connection are discarded; an exact model ID can be entered. Choosing changes the draft only (Use default reverts).
- **Conversation as a turn outline** (`TurnOutline.tsx`, `turns.ts`): your messages, then each agent turn under a collapsible header that summarizes what its tool calls and the review records recorded — never the agent's prose — e.g. "Read 3 files · Edited main.tex — unsaved · Ran Python · 1 figure" (only succeeded calls count as done; files edited or created carry "— saved" / "— unsaved" / "— reverted" / "— undone" from the review record, several files "N files · M unsaved"; status text "N failed", "Waiting for approval", "N declined", "Running: {tool}"). Routine tool-call rows (summary, state, authorization (§6.8), input, result) show while the turn is expanded; a turn with a failed call or an error notice starts expanded. The agent's text (rendered as below), reasoning summaries, notices, approval cards, question cards, and figures always stay visible in order. The expansion is shared by the Agent view and the Agents page. The streaming draft ("Thinking" / "Running: {tool}" / "Waiting for approval: {tool}") closes the last turn; the outcome banner of a finished task follows. User messages show their attachments ("Content no longer available" when a snapshot is missing). The log follows new output only while the reader is at its end; otherwise "New activity" jumps there.
- **Needs you** (above the composer; `NeedsYou.tsx`): "Needs you N" with jump links to the inline cards, which stay at their action point in the conversation — "Needs your answer: {first prompt, shortened to whole words}" per open question, "Waiting for approval: {title}" per approval — and, once the task has stopped with changes to review, "Review changes: N files · M unsaved" (opens the review; not repeated while the Agents page shows Changes). A jump scrolls the card into view and moves focus to it; nothing is answered from the list.
- **Approval card**: warning banner with the title, the `reason` (when any) before "Nothing is applied until you approve. Review the complete content below.", "{lines} · {bytes} · complete, nothing omitted", scrollable payload (a Tab stop only when it scrolls), Approve / **Approve for this task** (only when `forTask` is non-empty) / Decline, then a muted line naming what it covers ("Approve for this task also covers later native code in this task; revoke it from the task.").
- **Question card** (`QuestionCard.tsx`, at the `ask_user` call it belongs to; §6.13.1): built like the approval card — an info banner **Needs your answer** ("The agent waits for your answer before it continues. Your answer is passed to it as your reply; it approves nothing.") with the questions beneath it; "{n} of {m} answered" for a group; "Discussing" while a discussion turn runs. Each question is a radio list (its prompt as the label, the explanation, each option's consequence; at most one dashed **Recommended** badge; nothing preselected; options a discussion added are marked **New**) with "Other answer…" when custom answers are allowed (an option and a typed answer replace each other; ≤ 2 000 characters). **Send answer** (primary) is enabled only when every question has an answer and sends them together at the request's revision; **Chat about it** starts a discussion. Refusals say what to do: stale revision "The question changed; review the updated options." (the task is re-read), answered elsewhere or superseded "This question is no longer open." (the task is re-read and the card shows how it ended), busy "The agent is still replying about this question. Send again after its reply.". An answered request stays as a quiet record ("Answered: {label or text}" per question); a superseded one reads "No longer needed — {reason}" (e.g. "the task was canceled", "Exposition restarted"). Both views share one in-flight guard, so a double press or a second view never sends twice. Without a recorded request (older transcripts) the call shows as an ordinary tool row.
- **Chat about it**: marks the task's draft as being about the question — the composer shows the accent chip **About: {first prompt}** (its remove button stops discussing and gives an emptied field its earlier text back), takes focus, and keeps the draft text and attachments. Send then discusses the question (`agents_question_discuss`, at the current revision; a stale revision is re-read and sent once more) instead of continuing the task; attachments are not sent and stay. After success the sent text and the chip are cleared and an unrelated draft that was in the field comes back; the agent's reply appears as an agent turn, and a revision updates the card. A request that was answered or superseded meanwhile clears the chip with "This question was already answered." / "This question is no longer open.".
- **Figures in the conversation** (`FigureCard.tsx`): each figure a native `run_python` call recorded (`ToolCall.figures`) shows beside that call, outside the collapsed activity, frameless like a Research figure (the image's 1 px border only): the image (fetched once visible through `agents_figure`, at most 20 MiB, PNG/JPEG/GIF/SVG rendered only as `<img>` from a blob URL, so an SVG never runs scripts or loads anything), the file name, "Run {short id} · Python", the caption (agent text), **Expand** (a dialog), **Open in Research** (reveals the run), Research's own promotion controls when Research lists the run, and "Showing a figure does not add it to the document.". An unavailable figure reads "Figure no longer available — {reason}"; another media type points to Research; a read error offers Retry. Restricted runs produce no figures.
- **Agent responses** (`ResponseSurface` + `Markdown`, `src/ui/components/Markdown*.tsx`; the "inset block" the user chose): each assistant message in an inset block headed "Agent" with Copy (accessible name "Copy response": the original Markdown; "Copied" / "Could not copy"; absent while streaming), the streaming draft's status as its footer. CommonMark + GFM (tables, strikethrough, task lists as disabled checkboxes, autolinks, footnotes) + math (`$…$`, `\(…\)`, `$$…$$`, `\[…\]`, KaTeX with MathML for screen readers); plain text stays plain. Code fences are highlighted with Monaco's tokenizer and have Copy ("Copy code": the exact fence text) and, for Python, R, and Julia, **Run in Research** (the user's action: it runs in the user's research session of that language when one is ready; otherwise it fills the Research input — never replacing code typed there, offering "Replace input" instead — and shows Research; nothing runs on render). Streaming re-parses at most once per animation frame; an unclosed fence or display formula shows as text until it closes; the transcript (`role="log"`) follows new output only while the reader is within 32 px of its end. Code, tables, and display math scroll inside their block. The user's prompt, reasoning summaries, tool inputs and results, approval payloads, and diffs stay plain text. **Untrusted-content policy**: react-markdown with remark-gfm and remark-math only, no rehype plugins; raw HTML is shown as literal text, never elements (`skipHtml` as a second barrier); element allow-list; links never get `href` or `target` — an absolute http/https address with a host and no credentials, whitespace, control, invisible, or bidi characters (≤ 2048 characters) is a focusable link shown with its full address that opens in the system browser through `app_open_external` on click or Enter, and every other scheme (`javascript:`, `data:`, `file:`, `vbscript:`, `tauri:`, `ipc:`, `mailto:`, relative links, fragments) is inert text with its address; images never load (a placeholder with the alt text, "Image not loaded: <address>", and Open for http/https); KaTeX `trust: false`, `strict: "ignore"`, `throwOnError: false`, `maxSize` 10em, `maxExpand` 500, no shared macros, ≤ 20 000 characters, unparseable TeX shown as code. The renderer has no IPC access besides opening a link the user chose.
- **Attention**: status-bar item (right) — approvals only "Agent: waiting for approval" / "Agent: N waiting for approval"; questions only "Agent: waiting for your answer" / "Agent: N waiting for your answer"; both "Agent: N waiting for you" — a button that opens the oldest task waiting for you (its approval or question card); else "Agent: N running"; else "Agent: N to review". Persistent notifications "Agent needs your approval" ("{title} · Task: {task}", action Open approval) and "Agent needs your answer" ("{prompt} · Task: {task}", action Open question), not shown when the card is visible, retired when answered or when the task ends. Opening focuses the card. Finished notifications: completed (success, files to review), budgetExceeded/stopped/interrupted (warning), failed/refused (danger), canceled (none).
- **Task lists** (panel and Agents page, P1; flat rows since the UI rework): a click opens the task and selects it, Ctrl/Cmd+click adds the row to the selection without opening it, Space selects, Enter opens; the open task's row carries an accent bar at its leading edge, other selected rows only the selection fill; each row has a **More actions** button; Copy copies the title as text; Delete Task Record ("The record only; files it changed stay"; restorable) and Delete Permanently ("The record cannot be restored; files stay") through §8; no Duplicate ("A task is not duplicated; start a new task to reuse its instructions") or Paste.
- **Palette:** commands in category Agent, no keybindings (§2); Open Pending Agent Approval / Open Pending Agent Question are enabled only while a task waits for that and focus the first open card.
- **Review** (the task menu's Review changes, the Needs-you row, or Changes on the Agents page): Keep all, Revert all, Open in editor area (editor `agents-review:{id}`, "Review: {title}"), "The task is still running" banner (actions disabled), per-file rows (state pill, "Overlapping edits", "N changes" / "created by the agent", "· edited since", Keep, Revert), results "Done"/"Refused", diff.
- **Agents page** (top-level page `agents`, order 30, Mod+3): a collapsible task list (filters All / Running / Needs you — a task waiting for an approval or an answer, or with files to review / Kept; collapses to a rail with Show task list and New task) and "Kept in the project graph" (investigations, workflow definitions; a workflow offers "Open workflow"); the selected task's header (title, the one status pill, header toggles **Changes N · M unsaved**, **Graph context**, **Proposals N · M waiting**, Keep as investigation, Show in Agent panel, and the task menu); the conversation centered (max 720 px) with Needs you and the composer below it; the header toggles open one right panel (Changes = the review, Graph context, Proposals; close button or Escape). Same store as the Agent view: one task list, one draft, one approval or question answerable from either; a pending approval or question opens where the user is.
- **Settings › Model providers** (`src/features/settings/ProvidersSection.tsx`; replaces the "Agent provider" dialog and Settings › Agents; the only place keys are entered): one group per provider (Anthropic, OpenAI): connection state (Connected / Not connected / Credential storage error, with the error banner); API key — "Configured — stored in {store}" or "Not configured" ("Exposition never shows a saved key again."), the key field (API key / New API key) saved with Save key or Enter and cleared before saving, Remove (confirm "Remove the API key?" Keep key / Remove key); the billing sentence; Model list state ("Listed once an API key is saved", May be out of date, Could not load the model list, Refresh); OpenAI: Organization ID and Project ID (optional) with **Save organization and project**; "Sign in with ChatGPT is not available" with the reason. **Defaults for new tasks**: Model and effort (the model menu) and Content sent to the model provider, each saved when changed ("Default model saved.", "Default content saved."; a failure shows "Not saved"). **Project permissions N** ("What Auto tasks may do without asking, per project. Stored on this computer, never in the project; a copied or moved project has none."): one row per record (folder path, summary · "Updated <date>", "Folder no longer exists"), Change… (open project only; the dialog of §1), Revoke (any project); "No project has Auto permissions." when empty. The composer's provider banner and the model menu's "Configure in Settings" / "Manage providers…" lead here.

### 6.13.1 Structured questions and figures (UI side; backend contract §6.7.1, landing in the backend lane)
- **Statuses.** `awaitingAnswer` reads **Waiting for your answer** (warning tone, like Waiting for approval) in the status pill, the task lists, and the status bar; it counts as "needs you" (attention filter, status bar, notifications). It is not terminal: Stop and Stop task work and supersede the question.
- **Data.** `TaskDetail.questions` (every request of the task in order, resolved ones included) renders at the `toolCall` item with the same `toolUseId`; open requests (pending or discussing) come first when a call has several. `TranscriptItem::UserText.question` marks a discussion message. `ToolCall.figures` holds the figure references of a native `run_python` call. `FileReview.unsaved` drives "saved" / "unsaved" in the turn headers and the unsaved counts of Needs you and the Changes toggle (absent: the file is not open or its state is unknown, and nothing is claimed).
- **Commands the UI uses.** `agents_question_answer(task, request, revision, answers)` from Send answer (one answer per question, `{kind: option, optionId}` or `{kind: custom, text}`; idempotent; the UI re-reads the task after a stale or resolved refusal); `agents_question_discuss(task, request, revision, text)` from the composer while About is set; `agents_figure(task, runId, artifactId)` from a figure card once it is visible. Events `questionRequested`, `questionChanged`, `questionResolved` (with `transcriptChanged`) re-read the task detail; the card marks options new in a revision by comparing with the revision it saw before (in memory, per session).
- **Errors shown.** `agents.questionStale`, `agents.questionResolved`, `agents.questionBusy` as worded on the card and in the composer above; other refusals show the backend's message. `agents.figureUnavailable` shows "Figure no longer available — {reason}".
- **Grants nothing.** An answer or a discussion message is user content: it authorizes nothing, and every later action is still decided by the central gate (§6.4).

### 6.14 Project graph (M7)
- **Mandatory context**: create and continue attach a scoped graph context (refused with the graph codes when the graph cannot be used): project id + revision, freshness, in-scope pending files, coverage, counts by kind, ≤160 entities (`id, kind, roles, label?, path, lifecycle, revision, origin kind, source version, binding, coverage, evidence kind+freshness`), ≤300 relations among them (`kind, status, origin kind, note?`), authorized operations; ≤48 KiB; `truncated` when summarized. Whole project: project entity, research/mathematical entities first, then files. Narrower scopes: files in scope, entities whose every binding is in scope, relations whose both endpoints are listed; unanchored entities only for whole-project tasks. Metadata-only: labels of anything but files/folders/project, notes, attributes, reasons, diagnostics withheld. Sent as `<graph_context>` JSON (`<`/`>` escaped), hidden from the transcript, recorded with the task; the first context binds the task to that graph identity. A follow-up whose context equals, in everything but its timestamp, the latest whole context block the application put into the stored conversation is sent a short `<graph_context>` statement that the earlier block is still current (with the graph revision) instead of the block. That block is found by its position in a user message the application built (task context, graph context, prompt last), never by scanning text; a prompt at or after it that begins with `<graph_context>`, or any doubt, sends the whole context. The context is recorded with the task either way, and the task context is sent with every follow-up. Its preamble states that the graph follows saved files and can lag behind the editor, so unsaved or reverted state is read from `read_file`, `list_files`, and the task context, never from this block. A `graph_propose` refused with `graph.serviceOwnedEvidence` (an `evidenceFor` relation of kind computational, formalCheck, or build that cites no existing record of that kind) carries `guidance`: such records are written only by services (Keep in Research or a workflow retention; Record evidence in the Lean panel or an accepted workflow association), a run or check the task made is not one, and review or literature evidence or a plain reference can be proposed instead.
- **Targets**: paths of `read_file`, `edit_document`, `create_file`, `save_document` (recorded as an edit), `build_document`, `insert_artifact`, `check_lean` resolve to resource identities (`ensure_resource`; generated outputs and excluded files such as logs and run records stay operational references, never admitted); results carry `graph {resource, admitted?, revision, freshness, note?}`; `create_file` journals a provisional identity (`CrossService` operation) before writing and completes it after admission (failed admission → needs reconciliation). Targets are recorded per task (uses: read, edit, create, build, check, insert). `insert_artifact` also records the attachment like a reviewed promotion (`ResearchProject::record_external_attachment`: artifact retained with computational evidence, `usesAsset`, journaled, followed through undo/redo); `graph.attachment.state` is `recorded`, `pendingReconciliation` (code `agents.graphPending`, plus a `graphNote`), or `notRecorded`.
- **Workflow drafts** (`workflow_propose`): saving is a deliberate admission of a `Workflow` entity, gated like a graph change (read-only refused, review-edits approval, auto-apply direct); saving runs nothing, and only the user runs a workflow, from a reviewed run plan (§7.7); a proposal that names the saved workflow's id and revision adds a revision to that workflow, one with an empty id creates another workflow (the result's `next` says so, §6.3); the context's operation entry says so ("saves a workflow draft …; saving never runs it (only the user runs a workflow, from a reviewed run plan)"); forged service-owned outputs and ceilings above the task's are refused and nothing is saved; listed under "Kept in the project graph" on the Agents page, and on the Workflows tab with an "Agent proposal" badge. Agents have no trigger tool: no tool name, description, or schema mentions triggers, a call naming `workflow_trigger_plan`/`_enable`/`_disable`/`_list` is refused as an unknown tool before anything runs, and a proposal carrying a `trigger`, `triggers`, or `standing_hash` field is refused as an unknown field (§7.8).
- **Research space**: the context lists the project's research space (whole-project tasks; it is not bound to a file) with its `research.path` attribute even under metadata-only consent (a path); other attributes only with `scopeContent`.
- **Keep as investigation** (`agents_task_keep_investigation`, stopped tasks): the user's admission of one `investigation` entity (attributes `agentTask`, `taskStatus`; `references` to the task's admitted targets) plus `review` evidence referencing `agentTask:{id}`; idempotent.
- **Project generation** (`agents_task_create_generation {objective, workspaceRoot, permission, execution?, consent?, budgets?, approvalMode?}`): an ordinary whole-project task (Ask unless `approvalMode` is `auto`, which needs the new project's permissions), execution disabled unless given, whose request is the objective plus a generation brief; TS helper `startProjectGeneration` (`src/features/agents/generation.ts`).

## 7. Project graph, projects, and workflows

Every open project has one typed project graph (`crates/exposition-graph`). It is attached when the folder opens, whether or not a graph view is shown, and a reconciler thread (`graph-reconciler`) keeps it in step with the project's files and open buffers. Rust owns identities, admission, validation, and semantics; the webview reads bounded projections and proposes typed changes (§10 "Project graph"); agents use the same service within their scope (§6.14). Workflow runs (§7.7) are a separate service (`crates/exposition-workflow`) with its own app-local run history; it reads saved workflow revisions from the graph and acts through the research, Lean, graph, and agents services.

### 7.1 Model, storage, and reconciliation

| Item | Values |
|---|---|
| Entity kinds (23) | structure `project`, `folder`, `collection`, `researchSpace`; resources `resource`, `externalResource`; mathematics `definition`, `assumption`, `conjecture`, `lemma`, `theorem`, `proposition`, `corollary`, `claim`, `proof`, `question`, `mathObject`, `formalDeclaration`; citation `reference`; research `experiment`, `finding`, `investigation`; `workflow`. Statements: conjecture, lemma, theorem, proposition, corollary, claim |
| Indexed only | `project`, `folder`, `resource`, `researchSpace`, `formalDeclaration` (people and agents cannot create them: `graph.notCreatable`) |
| Resource roles (10) | `manuscript`, `note`, `script`, `dataset`, `artifact`, `asset`, `formalSource`, `bibliography`, `configuration`, `other` (resources only) |
| Relation kinds (12) | structure `contains`, `memberOf`, `anchoredIn`; resource use `imports`, `usesAsset`; mathematics `dependsOn`, `assumes`, `proofOf`; citation `references`; evidence `evidenceFor`, `derivedFrom`; formal `formalizes`. Endpoint rules per kind (`graph_vocabulary`, `graph.endpointRule`); no self relations; `contains` may not form a cycle (`graph.cycle`) and an entity has one container; every other kind may form cycles (`RelationKind::allows_cycles`: nothing orders work by a relation; impact reports dependency cycles, below); `contains`, `anchoredIn`, `imports` follow the files and are never added by hand (`graph.notManual`) |
| Relation status | `observed` (extraction or a service), `suggested` (agent), `accepted` (person or built-in preset), `rejected`. Only a person accepts or rejects; observed relations cannot be reviewed or removed (`graph.observed`); an agent removes only its own suggestions. A rejected relationship is kept as the person's decision: an agent that proposes the same kind between the same items again is refused (`graph.rejected`, "the user rejected … ; only the user can add it again"); a person (or a preset or service) can assert it after all, which continues the same record (same id and creation time, revision + 1). When the source itself states a relationship whose only record is a suggestion or a rejection, reconciliation turns that record into the observed relation (same id, origin `extracted`, status `observed`); an accepted hand-made relationship is left as it is. **Review of a formal link:** accepting a `formalizes` relation, asserting one as a person, or accepting it again (`setRelationStatus` `accepted` on an accepted relation = confirm) records `reviewedStatement`, a digest of the `to` entity's statement as its bindings identify it (`crates/exposition-graph/src/review.rs`: a region binding's text fingerprint, an extracted declaration's whitespace-normalized body fingerprint, a whole file's content hash, the label of an unbound item; no path or range); rejecting clears it; confirming while the statement cannot be located is refused (`graph.statementNotLocated`, "Nothing was confirmed. The statement cannot be located, … Rebind the statement to its current text, then confirm the link. Until it is rebound the link cannot be confirmed, only rejected."; for an item that is gone: "… reject it, or restore the statement.") and changes nothing; accepting a suggestion in that state records no digest and reads `statementMissing`. Every read of an accepted `formalizes` relation carries the computed `review {state, reason}`: `current`, `statementChanged`, `statementMissing` (a binding not resolved, not indexed, or the item tombstoned; never read as unchanged), or `unrecorded` (no digest: reviewed before schema 5, or accepted while unlocatable). `statementChanged` and `statementMissing` need re-review; nothing but a person's accept/confirm or reject changes the record |
| Origins | `extracted{extractor}` (e.g. `latex/1`), `user`, `agent{taskId}`, `preset{blueprint}`, `service{service}`; set by the backend from the calling surface (IPC = user, agent tool = that task), never by the payload |
| Lifecycle | `active`, `archived`, `discarded`, `tombstoned` (set only by reconciliation when a file or declaration no longer exists; shown "Removed from source"); the project entity cannot be archived (`graph.lifecycle`). Archived or discarded is a person's choice and is kept when the file is indexed again, also for an extracted declaration; indexing only undoes `tombstoned`. The label and attributes of an item its source states (a declaration an extractor admitted: statement, proof, bibliography entry; any formal declaration) are written by indexing and cannot be changed by a person or an agent (`graph.sourceDerived`, "the name and attributes of … come from its source; edit the source to change them"); a file's label is its name (`graph.fileBacked`) |
| Evidence kinds | `computational`, `formalCheck`, `build` (service-owned: people and agents can only cite an existing record of that kind, `graph.serviceOwnedEvidence`), `review`, `literature`. Only an `evidenceFor` relation has an evidence kind and cites records (≤ 64 evidence ids): it needs a kind, and a kind on any other relation is refused (`graph.evidenceKind`); another kind of relation that lists evidence ids is refused (`graph.evidenceNotCarried`). Every cited record must exist (`graph.unknownEvidence`) and be of the relation's kind (`graph.evidenceKind`, "is not … evidence"), and a `formalCheck` record is cited only from the declaration it was recorded for (`graph.evidenceSubject`); the other kinds may be cited from an item that reports them (a finding citing the computational record of a retained result). Reading uses the same rule (`validate::cites`): a stored relation that does not meet it (written earlier, restored, or imported) is kept but not read as support, and a rejected relation supports nothing |
| Evidence freshness | `current` (every recorded input hash equals the file's indexed hash), `stale{reasons}` (an input changed, or every resource with that key was deleted), `unknown{reason}` (hash not recorded, file not indexed, or formal evidence without dependency fingerprints: "changes in imported modules are not detected"). Computed when read, against the last reconciled state of each file; the record is never rewritten |
| Coverage per file | `complete`, `partial` (what was not interpreted is listed), `unsupported` (no extractor: only the resource is known), `pending` (changed, waiting for a pass), `failed`; plus a count of unresolved links and bindings with explanations (≤ 1500 chars) |
| Bindings | selector `wholeFile`, `declaration{syntax, name, ordinal}`, or `region`; state `resolved`, `missing`, `ambiguous`, `unresolved` |
| Ids | `<prefix><32 hex>` with prefixes `prj-`, `ent-`, `rel-`, `evd-`, `view-`, `prop-`, `wf-`, `op-` (and `wrun-`, `wplan-` for workflow runs and plans, which are not graph records, §7.7); ≤ 64 chars; validated when they arrive over IPC or from the database |
| Change limits | ≤ 500 operations; label ≤ 200 bytes, one line, not blank; key ≤ 1024; note ≤ 500; ≤ 32 attributes (keys `[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}`, values ≤ 4096); summary ≤ 500; no control characters except `\n` and `\t`; temp ids `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,32}` |
| Proposals and revisions | Valid proposals are kept in memory (≤ 256, cleared when a project attaches or detaches) and can be applied only by the origin that made them, in the same project; applying re-validates, all or nothing; per-entity `expectedRevision` decides conflicts (`graph.staleEntity`). Each applied change increments the project revision once; a service batch with no effect does not |

**Admission** (which files become `resource` entities; one identity per path, `graph.excluded` otherwise):
- By extension: manuscript `tex`, `typ`, `md`, `markdown`; note `txt`, `rst`, `org`; bibliography `bib`; script `py`, `ipy`, `pyw`, `r`, `jl`, `ipynb`; formalSource `lean`; dataset `csv`, `tsv`, `json`, `jsonl`, `parquet`, `feather`, `xlsx`, `xls`, `h5`, `hdf5`, `nc`, `npz`, `npy`, `mat`, `rds`, `rdata`, `arrow`; asset `png`, `jpg`, `jpeg`, `gif`, `svg`, `pdf`, `eps`, `webp`; configuration `sty`, `cls`, `bst`, `toml`, `yaml`, `yml`, `cfg`, `ini`, `lakefile.lean`, `lakefile.toml`, `lean-toolchain`, `lake-manifest.json`, `.exposition/project.json`. Any other file is excluded.
- Excluded folders (any path component): `.git`, `.hg`, `.svn`, `.lake`, `lake-packages`, `node_modules`, `target`, `.venv`, `venv`, `__pycache__`, `.ipynb_checkpoints`, `.pytest_cache`, `.mypy_cache`, `.tox`, `.cache`, `_minted`, `.claude`, `.agents`, `.exposition-dev`, `.worktrees`, `.vscode`, `.idea`. Excluded files: `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `AGENTS.local.md`, save temps (`.exposition-save-`), and generated extensions `aux`, `log`, `out`, `toc`, `lof`, `lot`, `fls`, `fdb_latexmk`, `synctex`, `gz`, `bbl`, `blg`, `bcf`, `nav`, `snm`, `vrb`, `xdv`, `dvi`, `olean`, `ilean`, `c`, `o`, `obj`, `exe`, `dll`, `pyc`, `tmp`, `swp`.
- Under `.exposition/`: only `research/` (recognised types) and `project.json` are scanned. The graph's own storage (`graph.sqlite3*`, `graph-backups/`, `graph.damaged*`), run records (`runs/`), and other metadata are never entities; `artifacts/` files are admitted only on request (a kept result, an attached figure).

**Scan:** bounds of 32 folder levels, 200,000 folder entries, and 20,000 admitted files per scan; past a bound, `ReconcileReport.truncated` names it and files beyond it stay as they were (unknown, not removed). Links and junctions are never followed, and a path with a link in its folder chain is not read. Files over 256 MiB are admitted but not fingerprinted; files over 8 MiB are not extracted (coverage `failed`). Per file ≤ 5000 declarations, 20,000 links, 20,000 labels; names ≤ 256 characters without whitespace. A file whose size, modification time, and extractor are unchanged is not read; one whose SHA-256 is unchanged is not re-extracted. Each file's checkpoint (schema v2) holds size, modification time, physical file identity, content hash, extractor, and its extracted index.

| Extractor · files | Entities | Relations | Not interpreted (reported as partial coverage) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `latex/1` · `.tex` | Theorem-like environments: standard names (theorem/thm, lemma/lem, proposition/prop, corollary/cor, definition/defn/dfn, conjecture/conj, claim, assumption(s)/hypothesis/hyp, question/problem/openproblem, proof; starred too) and literal `\newtheorem` / `\declaretheorem` earlier in the same file (kind from the display name); `\label` names the innermost declaration; title from `[…]` | `\ref`, `\eqref`, `\cref`, `\Cref`, `\autoref`, `\pageref`, `\vref`, `\nameref`, 20 `\cite` variants → `references`; `\input`, `\include`, `\subfile`, `\import`, `\subimport` → `imports` (`.tex`); `\bibliography`, `\addbibresource` → `imports` (`.bib`); `\includegraphics` → `usesAsset`; `\begin{proof}[…\ref{x}…]` → `proofOf` x, otherwise a proof immediately after a statement → `proofOf` that statement | Macros are never expanded (a definition that could hide tracked constructs is reported); `\newtheorem` in another file; non-literal environment names, labels, or cite keys; remarks and examples (not entities); comments, `\verb`, verbatim-like environments, and text after `\endinput` are masked |
| `typst/1` · `.typ` | Calls with the default theorem-like names (`#theorem[…]`, `#lemma("Title")[…]`, …) and ctheorems `#let NAME = thmbox/thmplain/thmproof(…)` in the same file; a following `<label>` names it | `@x`, `ref(<x>)`, `cite(…)` → `references`; `#import "file"`, `#include "file"`, `bibliography(…)` → `imports` (packages `@…` are not links); `image("…")` → `usesAsset` | Nothing is evaluated; theorem packages other than ctheorems; no `proofOf` |
| `markdown/1` · `.md`, `.markdown` | Pandoc fenced divs with the default class names (`::: {.theorem #id title="…"}`, `::: theorem`) | `[t](path)`, `[id]: dest`, `[@key]`, `@key` → `references` (URLs, anchors, schemes dropped); `![](p)`, `<img src>` → `usesAsset` | Front matter, code, comments, and math are masked; footnotes ignored; headings are context only |
| `lean/1` · `.lean` | `formalDeclaration` per `theorem`, `lemma`, `def`, `abbrev`, `structure`, `inductive`, `class` (incl. `class inductive`), named `instance`, `axiom`, `opaque`, qualified by `namespace` | Header `import M` → `imports` (resolved through folders with a lakefile, else the root) | Commands that generate declarations (`macro`, `syntax`, `elab`, `notation`, …); anonymous instances and `example` (not entities); no use-site references |
| `bibtex/1` · `.bib` | `reference` per `@type{key,…}` (title cleaned of TeX; literal year and DOI) | — | `@string`, `@preamble`, `@comment` skipped; duplicate keys and malformed entries (or > 1 MiB) reported |
| `code/1` · `.py`, `.pyw`, `.ipy`, `.r`, `.jl` | — | Literal imports of project files only → `imports`: Python `import a.b`, `from a.b import c`, relative imports; R `source("x")`, `sys.source(…)`; Julia `include("x")` | Non-literal arguments; files a script reads at run time |
| none · `.ipynb`, datasets, assets, configuration, notes | — | — | Coverage `unsupported`: only the resource is known |

- **Attachment markers** (`// exposition:begin|end <key>` in Typst, `% …` in LaTeX, `<!-- … -->` in Markdown): the first image inside a single begin/end pair → `usesAsset` (a generated artifact is admitted, origin `attachment/1`); unpaired or repeated markers yield nothing and are reported.
- **Resolution:** a label resolves to a declaration in the same file, then to a unique live keyed entity; section and equation labels are not entities; a citation resolves to a `reference` by key. A missing or ambiguous target is counted as unresolved and explained, never guessed.
- **Other files' changes:** a pass over some files leaves what a reindex of the whole project leaves. A label that gains or loses a declaration anywhere (a second file declares it, one of two declarations is removed, the label is renamed, its file is deleted) resolves the references to it again, in whichever files they are; a file that appears earlier on a written path's lookup order (the file's folder, the project root, the folders of the LaTeX main files, each with the extensions tried) takes the link; a file that becomes or stops being a LaTeX main file changes the folders LaTeX paths are looked up in; a lakefile that appears, moves or disappears changes `lean.module` of the Lean files beneath the old and the new package root and how every `import` resolves. The files concerned are found from the relations to the declarations of the name and from the targets each file is recorded as waiting for (`source_index.wanted`: `name:<label or key>`, `path:<written path below a lookup folder, without the extensions tried>`, `set:latex-mains`); they are resolved from their stored index, not read again. A graph whose metadata does not record the current rules (`link_rules`) has the links, awaited targets and Lean module names of every indexed file derived again by its next full pass (the pass that follows opening the project), once.
- **References are not dependencies:** extraction produces only `imports`, `usesAsset`, `references`, and `proofOf` (plus `contains` and `anchoredIn` structure). `dependsOn` and `assumes` come only from people, agents (suggested), and presets.
- **File identity:** a rename or move keeps its identity when, within one reconciliation pass, the gone and new paths match one to one by physical file identity, then by identical content; a copy gets a new identity; a file reappearing with identical content at a tombstoned path revives it. Old and new paths seen in different passes are not matched. A folder keeps its identity when all its renamed files agree on one new folder.
- **Path spelling:** a file has one resource, keyed by its path as the file system spells it. A pass resolves every component of each path it is given to that spelling before it looks anything up (a caller or a watcher event that names `notes/a.md` for `Notes/a.md` reaches the stored resource; `ensure_resource` does the same), stays responsible for the records stored under the spelling it was given, and treats a rename that changes only the case of a file or folder as a rename (identity kept), whether the event names the old path, the new one or both. A stored path that no file has under that spelling is retired by the next pass that covers it, at the latest the full pass after opening the project. A component that no longer exists has no spelling on disk and is matched as written. Any other alias of an existing component (an 8.3 short name) is mapped to the stored name in the same way and never keys a resource of its own; a path through a link or junction is not respelled (its components stay as written, and it is refused where files are read). On a file system that distinguishes case, two paths that differ in case are two files with two resources.
- **Declaration identity:** matched by name and kind, then identical text, then identical context, then the same context with one candidate left on each side; only unique matches, never by position. A name declared twice keeps the existing entity with an `ambiguous` binding ("declared n times … none was chosen"), counted, not merged.
- **Tombstones:** a removed declaration, a changed kind, or a deleted file tombstones the entity (bindings `missing`, observed relations removed). A pass never deletes an entity, alters a curated record (except that a suggested or rejected relationship the source now states becomes the observed one, see Relation status), or reassigns an identity on an uncertain match. Tombstones are revived only by an exact name or exact text match. Declarations admitted from a Lean audit are never tombstoned (binding `missing` when no longer declared literally). Tombstones nothing refers to (no relation, no evidence about them) are removed only by project operations: after a permanent file deletion (under those paths) and when the user purges "Disposable records" in Storage & History (§8.4).
- **Region bindings** (curated) follow their exact text: found once → resolved at the new range; not found → `missing` ("The bound text was changed or removed."); found several times → `ambiguous`.
- **Unsaved overlay:** 150 ms after an edit, an open buffer with unsaved changes is extracted like a file and kept in memory as an overlay labelled with its revision (`freshness.overlay`). Only queries naming that revision (`graph_inspect_at_source`) see it, marked unsaved; declarations that exist only in the buffer get provisional `ent-unsaved-…` ids that cannot be change targets. Nothing is written to the graph; saving, or undoing back to the saved text, drops it. Buffers over 8 MiB get no overlay.
- **Driver:** file events are coalesced (250 ms quiet, at most 2 s after the first event); a rescan or more than 200 changed paths runs a full pass. Changed paths read as `pending` until a pass that started after the change has processed them. Sync states: `synchronized` (with the last checkpoint time), `reconciling`, `pending{n}`, `unavailable`. `graph_reconcile` ("Reindex project", a debug-only command, §10) re-reads every file and rebuilds derived projections without touching curated records.
- **Impact** (`graph_impact`): breadth-first from 1–100 roots, depth 4 by default (≤ 8), ≤ 200 items (≤ 1000), ≤ 20,000 edges examined (every relation of a visited entity is read; reaching the limit sets `workLimitReached`). `truncated` is also set when a subject has more than the 200 evidence records read for it, when more evidence is affected than is listed, or when more than 200 files of unknown coverage exist. Follows incoming `dependsOn`, `assumes`, `proofOf`, `evidenceFor`, `formalizes` (action `needsReview`), `references` (`citationReview`), `usesAsset`, `imports`, `derivedFrom` (`stale`), and `anchoredIn` into a file (`informational`: declarations in a changed file are listed, their dependents are not); outgoing `proofOf`, `evidenceFor`, `formalizes`; never `contains` or `memberOf`.
