A changed file (a root, or a file reached through relations) also lists every other entity bound to it that is not a resource or folder (a binding is not a relation, so curated items bound to a manuscript would otherwise be missing): `informational`, reason "is located in “<file>”, which changed; check whether it changed", their dependents not followed. Both kinds of located item carry `ImpactItem.locatedIn` (the file's entity id; `null` for items reached through relations), and of their evidence only records that list that file as an input (or as the formal source) are reported, as becoming historical when the file changes. Rejected relations are skipped; suggested ones are followed and labelled "(suggested relationship)". Reports ≤ 50 cycles (a cycle is a loop of distinct relations, each followed from the item depended on to its dependent, of any kind except `contains`; coming back over the relation just followed, or closing a loop through one of the links followed both ways, is the same link seen twice and not a cycle; each cycle is listed once, from its smallest id), files with unknown coverage (≤ 200), and the evidence of roots and affected items with freshness.

**Storage and recovery**
- `.exposition/graph.sqlite3`, schema 5 (`PRAGMA user_version`; v5 adds `relations.reviewed_statement`, NULL for existing relations; v2 adds the per-file reconciliation checkpoints: coverage `size`, `modified_ms`, `file_identity`, `pending`, and table `source_index`; v3 adds `evidence_seals`; v4 (P1) adds `recoverable` (id, kind `entity`/`relation`/`view`/`workflow`, label, JSON snapshot ≤ 4 MiB, `deleted_at`) and `retired_workflows` (id, `retired_at`), each ≤ 100,000 rows). Tables `meta`, `entities`, `bindings`, `relations`, `evidence`, `evidence_seals`, `changes` (newest 10,000 kept), `views`, `workflows`, `coverage`, `source_index`, `recoverable`, `retired_workflows`. Writes are refused (`graph.limit`, nothing written) when a commit would exceed any per-table row limit of the validated open (so no write can make the next open refuse the database), and field/record sizes and ≤ 64 bindings per entity are checked on write. Rollback journal (`journal_mode=DELETE`, not WAL), `synchronous=FULL`, 5 s busy timeout. Every statement is parameterized. A read for presentation is bounded (≤ 20,000 rows) and says when it left rows out (the `…Truncated` fields of §10); reads that a decision rests on (impact, validation, reconciliation, removal planning) are complete: an entity's relations, a path's bindings, and the coverage of every file are read whole, bounded only by the table's row limit.
- **Validated open** (the database is untrusted input): ≤ 1 GiB; SQLite defensive mode with triggers, views, and untrusted schema disabled; a newer `user_version` or vocabulary is refused and left unchanged; `quick_check`; every schema object and every table's column list must be exactly the expected ones (planted triggers, views, or tables are refused); `foreign_key_check`; per-table row limits; every row decoded and bounded (ids, paths without traversal, ranges, short fields ≤ 256, text ≤ 256 KiB, JSON ≤ 1 MiB, relation endpoint rules, at most one project entity). An older graph is backed up (`premigration`) before it is upgraded; the upgrade is refused if the backup fails. A refused database is left byte for byte unchanged.
- **Graph states:** `attached`. `temporaryReadOnly{reason}` when `.exposition` cannot be created, the folder is read-only and has no graph yet, or the database cannot be created (an in-memory graph), or an existing graph is on read-only storage (opened read-only and copied with `VACUUM INTO` to `<data>/graph-temporary/`): indexed and readable, but nothing is written to the project or claimed as saved (the Graph page offers no changes or saved layouts). `unavailable{reason, recovery}` when `.exposition` or the database is a link, not a regular file, or leaves the project, or the schema is newer (Retry), or the database is damaged or refused (Restore one of the ≤ 5 newest backups, Start new, Retry). An unavailable graph is never replaced automatically; source editing keeps working and graph-dependent actions report unavailable.
- **Recovery** (`graph_recover`): only an option the unavailable graph currently offers is performed (backups matched by name), anything else is refused with `graph.recoveryNotOffered`, and nothing is performed through a `.exposition` link or junction. `restoreBackup` copies the backup to `restore-<ms>.sqlite3`, validates and migrates it like any open, then sets the current database aside; `startNew` renames the damaged database and its side files to `graph.damaged-<ms>.sqlite3`. Nothing is deleted.
- **Evidence seals:** evidence a service of this installation registers is sealed with an HMAC-SHA256 (key `<data>/graph-evidence.key`, never in a project) over its id and stored body (table `evidence_seals`). Evidence without a valid seal — for example copied in with a project's `graph.sqlite3` from another computer — is *imported*: shown as a historical claim of its recorded origin (`EntityDetail.importedEvidence`, `MathRow.imported`), with freshness `unknown` ("recorded outside this installation (copied with the project); a historical claim, not verification on this computer"), never `current`.
- **Backups:** `.exposition/graph-backups/graph-<ms>-r<revision>-<reason>.sqlite3` via `VACUUM INTO` (written as `.partial`, then renamed); the newest 8 are kept and leftovers older than 1 h removed. Reasons: `auto` (after the first successful reconciliation of each open, when the newest backup is older than 24 h), `premigration`, `manual` (`graph_backup`, a debug-only command, §10). A temporary graph has none (`graph.temporary`).
- **Operation journal** (application data): `<data>/graph-operations/<24 hex of SHA-256 of the lowercased root>/<op>.json`, written via fsync + rename; ≤ 200 records per project (finished ones pruned), ≤ 1 MiB each. Kinds `promotion`, `projectCreation`, `crossService` (P1 project operations write `crossService` records "Project operation: …" with their intent, per-target outcomes, and restore records, §8.1); states `pending`, `completed`, `failed{message}`, `needsReconciliation{message}`, `resolved{resolution}`. When a project attaches, operations an earlier session left pending become `needsReconciliation`; none is re-run (project operations are reconciled against the files actually present, §8.1).
- **Saved views:** table `views`; ≤ 5000 positioned nodes, hidden, or collapsed entries; zoom 0.05–8; ≤ 500 listed; replacing needs the view's revision (`graph.staleView`); a save that names an id this graph does not hold (a deleted view, or a view of another project) is refused (`graph.unknownView`), never created under that id. Saving a view never changes the graph revision or any relation.
- **Events:** `graph:event` (`status{status}`, `changed{delta, origin}` with ≤ 500 ids per delta field then `truncated`, `reconciled{revision, freshness}`; broadcast capacity 1024); `graph:resync` on lag. `EXPOSITION_GRAPH_TRACE` (any value) prints reconciliation timings to stderr.

### 7.2 Graph page (`graph`, Mod+4)
- **Loading:** graph reads start only when the Graph page or the Dependencies view is shown; ordinary editing never mounts the page. Header: "Graph", section control **Project** / **Mathematics** (not persisted), freshness pill, "{n} items · {n} relationships · revision {n}", re-read after every graph change (person, agent, or indexing).
- **States:** No folder open (Open Folder…); "Opening the project graph…"; "Attaching the project graph…"; "The project graph could not be read" (Retry); "The project graph is unavailable" + reason, "Editing files keeps working. Graph-dependent actions are paused until the graph is recovered." with Try again / Start a new project graph (confirm "Start a new project graph?": curated relationships, collections, and saved views of the damaged graph are not carried over; the damaged file is kept aside) / "Restore the backup from <date>"; failure → "Recovery failed". Changes are offered only while `attached`; a temporary graph shows "Temporary read-only graph … Changes and saved layouts cannot be kept for this folder."
- **Sync** (the Graph page header's one status pill; `syncStateLabel` in `src/features/graph/status.tsx` is the one wording, also used by the Agents graph context): Graph synchronized, Graph reconciling, Reconciliation pending (N files), Not yet reconciled, Synchronization unavailable; "N unsaved buffers". While pending: "N changed files are waiting to be reconciled" / "The graph has not reconciled with the project's files yet" / "The graph is catching up with recent file changes", "Answers may not include the latest edits yet; answers from unsaved buffers are labelled as such."
- **Project section:** Display Canvas / Table (localStorage `exposition.graph.mode.project`); "Search items" (non-matches are dimmed, not removed); Filters; New (New collection…, New item…); Arrange (canvas); "Show N hidden"; layout save indicator; Inspector. Loads ≤ 300 items ("Showing X of Y items": Search all, Show up to 1000). When more relationships exist among the shown items than were returned (`relationsTruncated`): info banner "Showing N relationships among these items; more exist" with "Filter by kind or show an item's neighborhood to see the others." Neighbourhood focus "Showing N steps around X" (depth 1–4; Both directions / What it relates to / What relates to it; Show whole project).
- **Canvas** (React Flow): pan by dragging the background, Space+drag, or middle-drag; zoom with the wheel, pinch, the buttons, or `+`/`-` (10–250 %); Fit to view (Shift+1), Fit to selection (Shift+2), Reset view 100 % (0); click, Ctrl/Cmd+click, or Shift+drag box to select; Enter or Space selects the focused node or edge; drag or arrow keys move a node (layout only). Dragging from a node's right handle onto another node opens "Add a relationship"; the canvas never creates a relation itself. The canvases and tables are project-operation surfaces (§8.6–8.7): the Edit commands and a context menu "Graph actions" (Copy, Paste Item(s), Duplicate, Hide from view, Copy Reference, Copy Text, Delete Relationship / Delete Entity / Delete Source File, Delete Permanently) act on the selected nodes, edges, or rows, with unavailable actions disabled and their reasons shown; double-click does nothing. Group nodes (project, folder, collection, research space) collapse and expand ("N inside"); relations into a collapsed group are aggregated (×N). Edge labels (UI rework): mathematical, citation, evidence, and formal-association edges are labelled at rest when the canvas shows 60 edges or fewer; structural, resource, and execution edges (contains, is declared in, imports, …) only when the edge or one of its ends is hovered, keyboard-focused, or selected; any edge touching the selection is labelled at every size. A label reads the relation ("depends on", "×N" when aggregated) with a muted status suffix (" · suggested" / " · observed" / " · rejected"; accepted has none); status is also the dash pattern (accepted solid, suggested dashed, observed dotted), colour is the relation family, citations have an open arrowhead. A **Legend** toggle in the canvas controls opens a non-modal key of families and statuses (remembered per viewer). Edge accessible names keep the full reading and status. Edges are straight lines between node borders (parallel relations bowed 22 px apart), not routed around other nodes.
- **Arrange:** deterministic layered layout, left to right (layers 96 px apart, nodes 28 px, components 72 px). It runs only on Arrange or to place nodes without a saved position (to the right of the existing layout) and never moves positioned nodes on its own; "Arranged N items" with Undo.
- **Saved layout:** one saved view per section (scope Project / Mathematics) in the project graph: positions, viewport, filters, hidden and collapsed items. Saved 600 ms after a change with the view's revision ("Saving layout…", "Layout saved", "Layout not saved" + Retry); when the view changed elsewhere the newer one is shown ("The {name} view changed elsewhere"). The UI has no create, rename, or delete for views.
- **Filters:** "Show archived items and rejected suggestions"; item kinds by family (Structure, Resources, Mathematics, Citations, Research, Workflows); relationships by family (Structure, Resource use, Mathematical dependencies, References, Evidence and provenance, Formal association).
- **Table** (keyboard alternative to the canvas): tabs "Items N" (Name, Kind, Status, Source or key, Relationships) and "Relationships N" (From, Relationship, To, Status).
- **Inspector** (docked at ≥ 1100 px window width, otherwise a drawer): type, title, lifecycle and origin badges (You, Agent task, Extracted (x), Preset x, Service (x)); Open source, Relate to…, Show neighborhood (icon button, depth 2), More actions (Add to collection…, Hide from view / Show in view, Archive / Restore for items not backed by a file, with confirmation; then Duplicate, Delete Entity or Delete Source File, Delete Permanently, Copy Reference, Copy Text as in §8.6). Sections: Source, Relationships (by family; a partial list says "Showing the first N relationships; this item has more." and "Some related items are shown by identifier only."; an accepted formal link whose review does not stand reads "Needs re-review" (warning badge) or "accepted · reviewed statement not recorded" with the reason on the next line, and offers Confirm link / Reject link (`setRelationStatus`; "Link confirmed" / "Link rejected"; Confirm link is disabled while the statement cannot be located, the reason line saying to rebind it first); the same status and reason appear in the relationship inspector, the Evidence pointer, the Mathematics Formal verification list, the Dependencies view, the Research graph inspector, the Agents graph context, the Lean view's association line ("reviewed correspondence needs re-review" / "reviewed correspondence, reviewed statement not recorded"), and as "· needs re-review" on the canvas edge; Accept suggestion / Reject suggestion / Delete relationship (§8; applied at once, restorable, stays deleted across reindexing) for hand-made, non-observed relations, otherwise "Observed in the sources: to change it, change the source." or "This kind of relationship follows the files; it is not edited by hand."), Evidence (the records recorded about the item, with freshness, and "Showing the most recent evidence; older records exist." when the list is partial; then, under **Cited by a relationship**, the records its "is evidence for" relationships cite, with the note "Cited as support by an “is evidence for” relationship of this item. These records are not recorded about it." — the same group in the Dependencies view and the Research graph inspector; outside the Mathematics view, an item with non-rejected incoming `formalizes` relations lists those declarations with the status of each association under "Lean evidence is recorded for a formal declaration, not for the item it formalizes. Associated with this item:" and "Its evidence is on the declaration. …"; a formal declaration without evidence shows "A Lean check alone records nothing here. To record Lean's verdict for this declaration, open its file and choose Record evidence in the Lean panel."; `FormalEvidencePointer`), Change impact ("What needs review if this changes?": Needs review / Stale / Citation review / Informational; when an item is listed through `locatedIn`: "Items in a changed file are listed without what depends on them; ask this of an item to follow it."), Provenance (collapsed: admitted by, key, item and graph revision, id). Labels are not editable.
- **Changes:** dialogs "Add a relationship" (Swap direction), "New item" / "New collection", "Add <item> to a collection" propose a typed change (`graph_propose_change`) and show "Valid. Applying will:" with every effect, or "Not allowed. Nothing will change." with the backend's reasons; applying calls `graph_apply_change` ("The graph changed while you reviewed this change: …" on conflict).
- **Mathematics section:** mathematical kinds only. "Mathematics view" **Dependencies** (canvas) / **Verification** (table); Show All / No proof / No formal association / Stale or unknown; "Extraction coverage: … Unknown coverage is not verified." When more relationships exist among the rows than were returned: info banner "Showing N relationships among these items; more exist" with "Each item's state is computed from all of them." Verification columns: **Statement**, **Informal proof** (No proof linked / Proof linked / Proof suggested / Not applicable), **Formal association** (None / Suggested association / Reviewed association (N) / Association needs re-review (N) / Reviewed association (N) · statement not recorded / Is a formal declaration; with several links the one needing attention wins: needs re-review, then not recorded, then reviewed, then suggested), **Verifier verdict** (Checked, Checked with axioms, Incomplete (sorry), Relies on compiled code, Partially checked, Not verified, Errors, or No formal evidence; the declaration's own verdict, else the file's, from the latest `formalCheck` evidence; when it reaches the row through a link that is suggested or needs re-review (`verdictAssociation`) it is a dashed badge reading "<verdict> (suggested link)" or "<verdict> (link needs re-review)", also on the canvas node and in the inspector's "Verifier verdict:" line with a sentence saying why; a declaration's own row and a reviewed or not-recorded link stay unqualified), **Freshness** (Current / Stale / Unknown / No evidence), **Source** (path or Not bound). Inspector adds Depends on (`dependsOn` and `assumes`, depth 4, ≤ 200; accepted, observed and suggested relations only: a rejected suggestion is never followed, directly or transitively, and stays in Relationships labelled "rejected"; an item reached only through suggestions carries the Suggested badge; assumptions listed apart from results and definitions), Proofs targeting this / Proves, and Formal verification ("Formal dependency edges unavailable: …", "Freshness against formal dependencies is unknown (no dependency fingerprints)").
- **Associate a Lean declaration…** (conjecture, lemma, theorem, proposition, corollary, claim, definition, assumption, mathObject): dialog "Associate a Lean declaration with X", Formal declaration, Note (optional, ≤ 500), "Record reviewed association" → `lean_associate_declaration` (entity form). States that the association is a reviewed correspondence, not a machine proof of equivalence, and does not change the verifier verdict. Disabled without formal declarations ("No formal declarations in this project yet. Check a Lean file and record its audited declarations to associate one.") or while changes are not allowed.

### 7.3 Dependencies view (Editor page, auxiliary `graph.dependencies`)
Follows the Monaco caret (250 ms debounce) through `graph_inspect_at_source`: path and line, "Unsaved buffer (revision N)" when the overlay answered, freshness pill, "Extraction: …" coverage (unknown coverage: "absence of results is not evidence of absence"). "At the caret (N)" lists bound items innermost first ("Showing the first N items bound here; more exist." when the list is partial) with their relationships by family ("Showing the first N relationships; this item has more."), binding badges (Binding missing, Ambiguous binding, Unresolved binding), and the innermost item's evidence (when it has none: "No evidence recorded for this item." followed by the same pointer to its associated formal declarations, or for a declaration the Record evidence hint, as in §7.2); Open in Graph / Open in Mathematics (selects the item, or shows its 1-step neighbourhood when the view does not include it). File section with Open in Graph. Other states: No folder open, Dependencies unavailable, No source file active, Inspecting…, "This file is not a graph resource (not indexed, or excluded).", "No graph item is bound at this position.", "Could not inspect this position". Opened by "Graph: Show Dependencies at Cursor".

### 7.4 New Project and project setup
- **New Project tab** (`new-project:`; "File: New Project…" / "…from an Objective…"): "Start from a preset" or "Describe an objective".

| Preset (`id`) | Creates |
|---|---|
| Mathematical paper (`paper`) | Manuscript (`main.typ`, `main.tex` for pdfLaTeX or LuaLaTeX, or `main.md`) + `figures/`; build target "Paper (<engine>)"; Key definition, Standing assumptions, Main result bound to the manuscript (Main result `dependsOn` the definition, `assumes` the assumptions); collection Manuscript |
| Computational study (`computational`) | Research space (`.exposition/research/rs-<12 hex>/`) with `notes/notes.md`, a Research question, `sources/analysis.{py,R,jl}`, `data/`, a Baseline experiment referencing the script and question; optional Numerical experiment workflow draft (Python only) |
| Lean investigation (`lean`) | `lean-toolchain` (an installed toolchain, else `leanprover/lean4:v4.35.0`), `lakefile.toml`, `Investigation.lean`; Main conjecture bound to it; collection Formal development; optional Formal audit workflow draft |
| Blank research project (`research`) | Research space with notes and a Research question; no manuscript |
| Paper with computation (`paperComputation`) | Paper + computational study |
| Paper with Lean (`paperLean`) | Paper + Lean investigation (collection Formalization holds the main result) |

- **Preset form:** preset, title, manuscript format, research runtime ("installed / not found / checking…"), Lean toolchain (installed ones, default marked "(not installed)"), "Include a … workflow draft (saved; nothing runs until you review and start it)" (disabled and unchecked, with the reason "The Numerical experiment draft is a Python workflow, so a project with the R runtime is created without one.", when the preset's draft exists only for another runtime: `ProjectPresetInfo.workflowRuntime`, `python` for the Numerical experiment, null for the Formal audit). Preview "What will be created": files and folders with roles, the project structure (each workflow draft "well formed" or "incomplete (n)"; "nothing runs until you review its plan and start it"), "Required tools (declared, never installed or run)" with Available / Not available, diagnostics.
- **Objective form:** "What is this project for?" (≤ 4000 chars used as the purpose), title, Document changes (default Ask before each change), Commands and code (default No commands). "Create and Start Agent" creates an empty project with a research space (hidden preset `empty`), then starts an ordinary whole-project agent task with the objective (`agents_task_create_generation`) on the Agents page; if the task does not start: "The project was created, but the agent task did not start".
- **Location:** "Project folder" + Browse…; the folder name follows the title until edited; the parent defaults to that of the most recent project. Checked after 250 ms (`project_check_destination`): "A new folder will be created: …" / "The empty folder … will be used." A folder inside a trusted folder needs "Create the project inside this trusted folder" (`project.trustedLocation` otherwise).
- **Create:** closes open file editors, opens the entry (manuscript in the Editor, or the Research page), and reports "Nothing was trusted or run."
- **Materialization** (`project_create`): validate the blueprint → destination checks → refuse if any path already exists (`project.collision`, nothing created) → journal → write each file create-new + fsync (journaled with its SHA-256) → open the folder as Open Folder does → admit resources, save workflow drafts, apply the graph content in one batch → write build targets only if the project has none → complete. Files and the graph are not one transaction; graph content is keyed `blueprint:<op>/<tempId>` so re-applying never duplicates. Full record (blueprint + per-file SHA-256, ≤ 32 MiB) in `<data>/project-creations/<op>.json`. Built-in presets assert their relations; any other blueprint (agent proposal, import) only suggests. Nothing is trusted, run, installed, or sent to a model.
- **Interrupted creation:** an unfinished creation from an earlier session is shown when the project opens ("Project setup did not finish", Review) in the **Project Setup** tab (`project-setup:<operation>`): each file Created / Missing / Different content, "Workflow drafts saved: x of y"; Finish Setup (re-validates the stored record, creates only missing files, applies the rest) or Keep as Is (admits what exists: "Kept as created; the rest of the setup was not applied."). Never replayed automatically or reported complete. Debug builds: `EXPOSITION_TEST_INTERRUPT_PROJECT_CREATION=<n>` aborts the process after n files (e2e).
- **Blueprint rules** (`exposition.blueprint/1`, ≤ 16 MiB JSON, unknown fields refused, also a key beside a tagged value such as `{"kind":"project","grants":[…]}` or, in a workflow draft, `{"kind":"unset","trust":true}` ("unknown field at /path"), so no trust, run, startup-hook, or grant fields; an optional field written as `null` (`"condition": null`) reads as absent and is accepted; a workflow draft with a composite step cannot be resolved in a blueprint and is previewed as incomplete (`blueprint.workflowIncomplete`), never "well formed"): ≤ 200 files (≤ 1 MiB each, ≤ 8 MiB total), 200 folders, 500 entities, 1000 relations, 50 collections (≤ 500 members), 10 workflow drafts, 16 capabilities, 8 build targets; title ≤ 120, purpose ≤ 4000; `source` `[a-z0-9:._-]{1,64}`; research space id `rs-` + 4–32 lowercase hex. Relations limited to `memberOf`, `usesAsset`, `dependsOn`, `assumes`, `proofOf`, `references`, `derivedFrom`; entity kinds must be creatable (not collection or workflow). Paths: relative, ≤ 240 bytes, components ≤ 120, ≤ 12 levels; no backslashes, drive letters, `:`, `..`, `.` or empty components, 8.3 short names, reserved device names; no version-control or tool folders (`.git`, `.hg`, `.svn`, `.exposition-dev`, `.claude`, `.agents`, `.worktrees`, `.vscode`, `.idea`, and the admission's excluded folders); nothing under `.exposition/` except the project's own research space; no startup-hook files (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `latexmkrc`, `texmf.cnf`, `sitecustomize.py`, `usercustomize.py`, `.Rprofile`, `Rprofile.site`, `.Renviron`, `lakefile.lean`, `kernel.json`, `.envrc`, `.pythonstartup`, `desktop.ini`, `autorun.inf`, `ipykernel_launcher.py`, …); no executable extensions (`exe`, `dll`, `bat`, `cmd`, `ps1`, `vbs`, `msi`, `lnk`, `js`, `jar`, `pth`, …); no bare Git layout; compared case-insensitively. A dry run against an in-memory graph follows the static checks.

### 7.5 Workflow drafts
- **Workflows tab** (`workflows:` "Workflows"): a **New Draft** menu in the page header (New Numerical Experiment Draft, New Formal Audit Draft: saved at once with the active file bound when its type fits (`.py` script, `.typ`/`.tex`/`.md`/`.markdown` manuscript, `.lean` file), then opened), saved drafts (revision, "imported", "can be reviewed and run" / "cannot run yet", an "Agent proposal" badge for drafts an agent task saved, Well-formed / Incomplete draft pill, Open), "Trigger on" (info) / "Trigger suspended" (warning) badges for workflows with an enabled or suspended trigger (read when the list loads, not live), "Recent runs" (all runs of the project, §7.7), Import ("Import a workflow bundle file (a workflow with the workflows it runs), or paste an exposition.workflow/1 or /2 definition …": **Import Bundle…** (native open dialog, `workflow_bundle_import`; the root draft opens, its children are saved but not opened) and the JSON box + Import Draft). Nothing on this tab starts a run. The saved drafts list is a project-operation surface (§8.5): click/Space selects, Ctrl/Cmd+click adds; Copy, Paste (new drafts "… (copy)" with no triggers, runs, or approvals), Duplicate, Delete ("Disables its triggers; restorable from Storage & History"), Delete Permanently.
- **Editor** (`workflow:<id>`; the key holds the workflow's own id `wf-…`, never a graph entity id, and every entry that opens a workflow passes that id: the Workflows list, the Agents page, the Research inspector (§4.1), duplicates, and imports. The editor no longer resolves an entity id through the workflow list. A tab that cannot load shows a state with **Show Workflows** and **Close**: "This tab does not point to a saved workflow" when the key is not shaped like a workflow id (`wf-` + letters, digits, hyphens, ≤ 64; nothing is requested) or the IPC layer rejects the id, and "This workflow is not in the open project" for `workflow.unknown` (deleted: restorable from Settings › Storage & History; or a workflow of another project, for example a tab restored after the project changed); any other error keeps "Could not open this workflow draft" with Retry): header with revision, imported/preset/unsaved notes, draft-state pill, view switch **Form** / **Canvas** (localStorage `exposition.workflows.view`, default Form), Save Revision (with the draft's revision), **Export revision N…** (the saved revision as a bundle, backend save dialog; unsaved edits are not exported; errors in a "Not exported" banner), and **Review and run…** (opens the plan review, §7.7). Status banner: "Revision N can run" when the saved revision is well formed and runnable, otherwise "Unsaved changes" ("Save the draft first: a run uses a saved revision, never unsaved edits.") or "Cannot run this revision" with the reason (its problems, or the backend's `runnableReason`); Review and run… is disabled with that reason. An agent-proposed draft shows "Agent proposal" ("Proposed by agent task <id>. It is a draft like any other: …"). Validation in Rust (`workflow_validate(definition, id)`, so a composite that runs the workflow being edited is reported) 200 ms after each change. Using any schema /2 field switches the draft to `exposition.workflow/2`; removing them all returns it to the schema it was saved with. **Form:** General (title, "Most this workflow may run", description, ceiling checkboxes, and "Steps that may run at the same time": "1 — one at a time" (default; no `execution` field) or 2–4, described as "Independent steps and repeat instances run side by side up to this limit and the application's global limits (4 steps in flight, 2 containers, 2 native, 2 model, 1 Lean)."), Inputs (type, project file, version "at run start" (hashed when the plan is made) or pinned SHA-256), Steps from the registered operations (runner title and containment; port bindings offer only compatible, non-downstream sources and, for an input that takes one source, never a for-each step; parameters; prompt; "Write the code here" opens a Monaco editor in the operation's payload language whose code is data of the definition, never a project file), "Runs after (explicit prerequisites; no data)", Outcomes, "Order of steps" (operation, runner, effects). **Composite steps:** New step → "Run a saved workflow" asks for "Workflow to run" (the project's other saved workflows, "Latest revision N", " · incomplete"); the step pins that latest revision and takes its title. Its card: "Runs the saved workflow “<title>” at revision N (pinned) …", ports derived from the child (one required input per child input, one optional output per child outcome), "Show its N step(s) (read-only)" (nested composites expand, up to 3 levels); when the child has a newer revision, an info banner "Revision M of “<title>” is newer" with **Use latest revision (M)**, which changes only the unsaved draft's pin (bindings untouched); "No workflow chosen" / "Revision N could not be loaded" banners. A composite step offers no repeat ("A step that runs a saved workflow runs once; repeat steps inside that workflow instead.") and no condition ("A step that runs a saved workflow cannot carry a condition; put conditions on the steps inside that workflow.", with **Remove the condition** when one is set). **Condition:** "Run this step only when a condition holds" (disabled when no earlier output can be tested), "Tests the output" (outputs of earlier steps that do not run once per value: formal evidence, datasets, or any optional output; choosing one orders that step first), "Negate: run when this does not hold", "Holds when": "Audit verdict is one of…" (checkboxes), "A JSON value compares…" ("JSON pointer (RFC 6901)", empty = the whole document, "The dataset must be JSON of at most 1 MiB."; Comparison =, ≠, <, ≤, >, ≥ for a number, = or ≠ for text and true/false (changing the value type resets it to =); Value type Number / Text / True or false; Value), or "The output was produced"; the summary ends "otherwise it is skipped (condition not met), and steps that need its outputs are skipped too. An unreadable source fails the step." Deleting the source step removes the condition. **Repeat:** "Run once", "Once for each value of a parameter" (operations with parameters: the parameter, then 1–20 values of its kind; the fixed value then reads "Varies: one instance per value"), or "Again until a condition on its output holds" (operations with outputs: the output, negate, a predicate as above, "At most (iterations)" 1–10, default 3, "If it never holds": Fail the step / Use the last iteration's output), with a summary sentence. **Canvas** (React Flow; the keyboard alternative is the Form, and the Inspector below the canvas edits the selected step or input): input, step, and outcome nodes; one typed handle per port (shape and colour by port type, type in text, required inputs marked); data connections solid, prerequisites dashed, annotations dotted, with a legend; dragging an output onto an input adds a data binding only when the form's compatibility rule accepts it (otherwise "Not connected" with the reason, and nothing changes); a step's bottom handle onto another step's top adds a prerequisite; Delete removes the selected connections, steps, inputs, or outcomes (Remove from Draft); Copy, Paste into Draft, and Duplicate act on the selected steps and inputs with new ids and a 32 px offset (§8.5); diagnostics appear as badges on the nodes and ports they name; zoom, Fit to view, Reset view, and "Arrange by dependency order" (clears moved positions); moved positions are kept per workflow in localStorage (`exposition.workflows.layout.<workflow id>`), never in the definition. Composite nodes ("Workflow step", "Runs “<title>” · revision N (pinned)", badges "Revision M available" / "Child not loaded", "Show its N step(s)"); step badges "If <predicate>", "For each <parameter> ×N", "Until · at most N"; a condition is a dash-dot edge "condition (no data)" from the source output to the step, changed or removed only in the step's inspector; dropping a for-each step's output on an input that takes one source or on an outcome is refused with the reason ("… collect its outputs in a step that takes several first."), and outcome choices in the form omit for-each steps. Below either view: the workflow's runs (§7.7) and its **Triggers** (§7.8). Closing with unsaved changes asks first.
- **Contract** (`exposition.workflow/1`, and `exposition.workflow/2` when a definition uses any of `execution`, `composite`, `condition`, `repeat`; both are read, a `/1` definition using one is refused with `workflow.schema`, and a definition without them serializes exactly as before, keeping its `definitionHash`): inputs (`unset` or a project file at run start or pinned by hash), steps (operation id + version, parameters, optional code (`python`, `r`, or `julia`, as the operation declares) or prompt payload), connections (`data`, `prerequisite`, `annotation`), outcomes (transformedDataset, retainedFigure, retainedTable, investigationReport, auditEvidence, reviewableProposal, manuscriptPromotion), ceiling (execution none / restricted / trustedNative, model requests, retain results, propose changes). Rules: inputs bound, every step's outputs reach an outcome through data connections (a prerequisite orders steps but carries nothing, so a step linked to the result only by one is an orphan; a condition's source counts as used), typed data between steps, effects within the ceiling, outputs validated by the runner, service-owned types produced only by their services; no cycles (a condition's source is ordered before its step). Limits: 50 steps per definition (100 after composites are expanded), 200 connections, 50 inputs, 20 outcomes, 32 parameters per step, code 256 KiB, prompt 32 KiB, title 200, description 4000, definition 1 MiB, ids `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,32}`. Parsing is strict for both schemas: an unknown field anywhere, including one beside a tagged value such as `{"kind":"unset","trust":true}`, is refused ("unknown field at /path"). An optional field written as an explicit `null` (`"condition": null`, `"repeat": null`, `"composite": null`, `"execution": null`) is the field being absent, in definitions, bundles, and blueprints alike (`workflow::dropped_key`).
- **Schema /2 fields** (all optional; `deny_unknown_fields`):
  - `execution: {maxParallel}`: 1–4 (`workflow.maxParallel` otherwise); absent = 1.
  - `steps[].composite: {workflow, revision}` with operation `workflow.composite` v1 (`workflow.compositeOperation` unless both are present or both absent). Resolved through the project's saved revisions (`workflow_validate` with the workflow id, `workflow_save`, imports, agents); without a resolver (only blueprint validation) the step is reported `workflow.compositeUnchecked` (warning) and the draft is not runnable. Checks in order: revision 0 or not in the project → `workflow.compositeMissing` (also checked whenever a saved revision is read: a parent whose pinned revision was later purged reads Incomplete in `workflow_get` and `workflow_list`, "Step “<label>” runs revision N of a saved workflow, and that revision was deleted; choose another revision before this workflow can run."); the workflow (any revision) already on the chain of enclosing workflows, the edited one included → `workflow.compositeRecursive`; a fourth level of nesting → `workflow.compositeTooDeep`; more than 300 composite references → `workflow.tooManySteps`; the child validated as a definition of its own (its inputs treated as bound by the parent) → its recursion, depth, and size errors reported as such, any other error as `workflow.compositeNotWellFormed` (with up to 3 messages); its ceiling not within the parent's (execution none < restricted < trusted native, and no model requests, retention, or proposals the parent does not allow) → `workflow.compositeCeiling`. More than 100 steps after expansion → `workflow.tooManySteps`. Derived ports: an input per child external input (same id, label, type; required; one source) and an output per child outcome (optional); containment = the least contained of the child's runners; effects = the union of the child's; limits = their sums. A composite step takes no payload, parameters, condition (`workflow.conditionInvalid`), or repeat (`workflow.repeatInvalid`). Saving a child never changes a parent; "use latest revision" exists only as a parent edit in the editor.
  - `steps[].condition: {source: {step, port}, test, negate}` with `test` one of `{"kind":"verdictIn","verdicts":[…]}` (source port formal evidence; 1–7 distinct labels from exactly Checked, Checked with nonstandard axioms, Incomplete, Relies on compiled code, Partially checked, Unverified, Errors — the stored words, `KNOWN_VERDICTS`; the editor and the run view show each in the Lean view's words through `storedVerdictLabel` (`src/features/lean/verdicts.ts`): "Checked with axioms", "Incomplete (sorry)", "Not verified", the other four unchanged, any detail kept ("Unverified: reason" → "Not verified: reason"; "Incomplete (2 sorry)" stays); sentences written in Rust, such as a plan's description of a condition ("the verdict is one of “Unverified”"), quote the stored words), `{"kind":"jsonCompare","pointer","op","value"}` (source port dataset; pointer empty or `/…`, RFC 6901 escapes, ≤ 256 bytes, no control characters; `op` `eq`/`ne`/`lt`/`le`/`gt`/`ge`; `value` `{"kind":"number","value":"<decimal ≤ 64 chars, exponent ≤ 6 digits>"}` (any `op`; compared exactly as decimals, 1 = 1.0 = 10e-1; the document's integers within 64 bits exactly, its other numbers as the shortest decimal of their double-precision value), `{"kind":"text","value"}` (≤ 1000 bytes) or `{"kind":"boolean","value"}` (both `eq`/`ne` only)), or `{"kind":"outputPresent"}` (any port; meaningful for optional outputs). The source must be another step's declared output of a fitting type and not a for-each step (`workflow.conditionSource`); a malformed test is `workflow.conditionInvalid`. The source adds an ordering (and use) edge, so a downstream source is a `workflow.cycle`.
  - `steps[].repeat`: `{"kind":"forEach","parameter","values":[…]}` (a parameter the operation declares and the step does not also set; 1–20 values of its kind; for an operation with a code payload, text values follow the batch rule below) or `{"kind":"until","port","test","negate","maxIterations","onExhausted":"fail"|"useLast"}` (an output of the step itself; a test as above that fits it; 1–10 iterations); an association step (`formal.associateEvidence`, whose proposals are decided once per step) cannot repeat; otherwise `workflow.repeatInvalid`. A for-each step's output may feed only a port that takes several sources and never an outcome directly (`workflow.iterationFanIn`). A for-each step counts as one step against the 100-step expansion limit.
- **Code languages and batch parameters** (M9): an operation with a code payload declares its language (`python`, `r`, `julia`; `workflow.payloadLanguage` "this operation runs a different language" otherwise, rechecked at run time); a code-source input's file must have that language's extension (`.py`; `.R`/`.r`; `.jl`, case-insensitive), checked on direct input connections and, on the expanded definition, through composites (`workflow.inputTypeMismatch`, rule 3: "<path> is not an R script (.R), which Research R batch (restricted) runs" + " (inside composite step “<label>”)"); the plan reports it as `workflow.notWellFormed`, and the batch adapter and research staging refuse it again. Text parameters of an operation with a code payload are passed to the code as one line: no line break, CR, or NUL and ≤ 4096 bytes (`workflow.invalidParameter` "“<label>” is passed to the code as one line, so it cannot contain a line break"; for-each values `workflow.repeatInvalid`); the `value` parameter is at most 1000 bytes. A value that looks like a secret is refused only by the batch runner, before anything runs (`research.invalidRequest`).
- **Operations** (registry; each with one runner, its containment, and limits; the bare registry says "Runner availability is checked for the open project.", and `workflow_operations` fills in each runner's availability for the open project):

| Operation · title | Runner (id) · containment | Ports (in → out) | Parameters · payload | Limits (time · output) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `research.pythonBatch` · Research Python batch (restricted) | Scoped restricted Python adapter (`restrictedPython`) · `restrictedContainer` | `script` (code source, optional), `data` (datasets, several) → `dataset`, `figure`, `table` | `timeoutSeconds` 1–3600, `value` (text ≤ 1000 bytes, one line; the code gets it as `EXPOSITION_PARAM_VALUE`) · Python code unless `script` is bound | 3600 s · 256 MiB |
| `research.nativePython` · Trusted native Python | "Trusted native Python process (your account's privileges)" (`nativePython`) · `nativeAccount` | same | same | 3600 s · 256 MiB |
| `research.rBatch` · Research R batch (restricted) | "Scoped restricted R adapter (configured R image)" (`restrictedR`) · `restrictedContainer` | same as the Python batch (`script` an R script) | same; R code unless `script` is bound | 3600 s · 256 MiB |
| `research.nativeR` · Trusted native R | "Trusted native R process (your account's privileges)" (`nativeR`) · `nativeAccount` | same | same | 3600 s · 256 MiB |
| `research.juliaBatch` · Research Julia batch (restricted) | "Scoped restricted Julia adapter (configured Julia image)" (`restrictedJulia`) · `restrictedContainer` | same (`script` a Julia script) | same; Julia code unless `script` is bound | 3600 s · 256 MiB |
| `research.nativeJulia` · Trusted native Julia | "Trusted native Julia process (your account's privileges)" (`nativeJulia`) · `nativeAccount` | same | same | 3600 s · 256 MiB |
| `lean.audit` · Lean audit | Lean verification service (trusted native toolchain) (`leanAudit`) · `nativeAccount` | `source` (Lean file) → `evidence` (formal evidence) | — | 600 s · 16 MiB |
| `formal.associateEvidence` · Associate audited declarations | Project graph (reviewed proposal) (`graphProposal`) · `applicationService` | `evidence` → `proposal` (association proposal) | `declarations` (one per line, ≤ 8192 bytes; empty = all audited) | 60 s · 1 MiB |
| `agent.review` · Read-only agent summary or review | Read-only agent task (`readOnlyAgent`) · `readOnlyModel` | `material` (any file type, formal evidence, review report, retained artifact; several) → `report` | prompt | 900 s · 1 MiB |
| `artifact.retain` · Retain artifact | Research retention service (`researchRetention`) · `applicationService` | `artifact` (figure, table, dataset) → `retained` (retained artifact) | `label` (≤ 200 bytes; the name the retained result is listed under in the research space, stored as its `retained.label` attribute; its file name when empty; the file, its path, and its graph label are unchanged) | 60 s · 0 |
| `manuscript.requestPromotion` · Request reviewed promotion | Reviewed artifact insertion (`reviewedPromotion`) · `applicationService` | `artifact` (retained artifact) → `proposal` (promotion proposal) | `document` (required; `.typ`/`.tex`/`.md`/`.markdown`), `caption` (≤ 1000 bytes) | 60 s · 0 |
| `workflow.composite` · Run a saved workflow | "Saved workflow (each step runs through its own runner)" (`composite`) · `applicationService` in the registry; derived per step from the child (least contained of its runners) | Derived from the referenced child (see schema /2 fields) | — · none | 0 · 0 in the registry; derived: the sums of the child's |


- **State on read:** `workflow_get`, `workflow_list`, and a `workflow_save` that saves nothing (identical content) validate the stored definition again, by the rules of a save (input files in the open project, composites against the saved revisions), and report that state, error count, and diagnostics; the validation stored with a revision is the one from the moment it was saved and is not rewritten (nor is the `workflow.state` attribute). A purged pin keeps the message quoted above.
- **Runnable** = validation state `wellFormed` AND every step's runner available in the open project now; computed by the workflow run service on every `workflow_validate`, `workflow_save`, `workflow_import`, `workflow_get`, and `workflow_list` (each runner checked once per list; a batch availability answer is reused for at most 5 s and never across a change of the execution settings or the folder's trust). Open workflow editors, the Workflows list, and composite parents re-read availability when execution settings, trust, or the open project change, and re-read workflows saved, created, or imported in another tab or by an agent; no save is needed. `runnableReason`: "Ready to run: every step's runner is available. Review the run plan to start it.", "The draft has N error(s); fix them before it can run.", or "Not runnable now: step “<label>” (<runner>): <reason>; …". Runner availability: no adapter → `workflow.runnerUnavailable`; a `nativeAccount` runner in an untrusted folder → `workflow.untrustedFolder` ("… runs natively with your account's privileges (not a sandbox); trust the folder first."); `restrictedPython`: the configured image is valid and present locally and the engine reachable (§12), checked without pulling (`workflow.restrictedUnavailable`, "… it never falls back to native execution."); `restrictedR` / `restrictedJulia`: the same for the configured R image / Julia image (`execution.docker.rImage` / `juliaImage`; the Python image is never used; unset → "Restricted execution is unavailable: no R image is configured: choose a local image that provides Rscript as the R image (Settings → Execution). Exposition never pulls images. It never falls back to native execution."); `nativePython`: a trusted folder and a discovered Python interpreter; `nativeR` / `nativeJulia`: a trusted folder and a discovered `Rscript` / `julia` (an available runtime first, else the first with an executable; the kernel is not needed; "No R installation (Rscript) was found for trusted native batches." otherwise); `leanAudit`: available in a trusted folder (the toolchain is resolved when the audit runs); `readOnlyAgent`: the Anthropic provider with a stored key (or, in debug builds, the scripted provider); `researchRetention`, `reviewedPromotion`: the project graph attached for this project; `graphProposal`: always; `composite`: always ("Available: each of its steps is checked with its own runner."), and runnability checks every expanded child step's runner ("composite step “<label>” has no checked steps" / "step “<label>” was not checked" when a child could not be resolved). Batch availability answers are reused for 5 s per (project, language, mode); a batch re-checks its mode itself when it runs.
- **Presets:** Numerical experiment (Python script, or without one an inline code template (standard library only) that reads the optional declared CSV and writes `table.csv` and `figure.svg` into `EXPOSITION_OUTPUT_DIR`; optional data → Python batch run (restricted) → retain figure → optional manuscript promotion; ceiling restricted) and Formal audit (Lean file → audit → associate evidence; ceiling trusted native). An unset file leaves an incomplete draft.
- **Storage:** rows of table `workflows` in `.exposition/graph.sqlite3` (immutable revisions, removed only by a permanent deletion or a revision purge, §8.5; a deleted workflow is listed in `retired_workflows` and hidden from lists, `workflow_get`, and saving, except that `workflow_get` with a revision number returns, read-only, with only that revision listed and no entity, a revision that another saved workflow still runs as a composite step (the editor's composite card); a stored record this version cannot read (damaged, or written by a newer version) is left out of `workflow_list` and refused when opened, and never fails the list; identical content saves nothing; ≤ 4 MiB per body); each draft is a `Workflow` entity (attributes `workflow.id`, `.revision`, `.hash`, `.state`, `.runnable` (always stored as `false`), `.preset`, `.imported`). Validation state `incomplete` or `wellFormed`; a stored `runnable` is never read (the graph returns `false` with "Runner availability has not been checked for this draft in the open project; it cannot run until it is.", and the IPC commands above replace it). Imports get a fresh id and are marked imported. Schema /2 definitions are stored the same way (the body holds the definition; stored validations list each expanded plan step with its `runnerId` and `compositePath`).
- **Bundles** (`exposition.workflow-bundle/1`, `crates/exposition-graph/src/workflow/interchange.rs`): `{schema, root: {id, revision, definition}, children: [{id, revision, definition}]}`, strict (`deny_unknown_fields`; a key smuggled beside a tagged value is refused too), ≤ 8 MiB, ≤ 32 children, each definition ≤ 1 MiB. **Export** (`workflow_bundle_export`): the root at the given revision (latest when absent) and, recursively, every (workflow, revision) its composites pin, de-duplicated and sorted, pretty JSON; refused with `workflow.exportInvalid` when the root includes itself, nests deeper than 3, names a revision that does not exist ("fix the draft before exporting it"), needs more than 32 children, or exceeds 8 MiB (`workflow.unknown` for an unknown root); definitions only (no trust, approvals, triggers, validation, origin, or run data). Destination: the backend's native save dialog ("Export workflow", filter "Workflow bundle" `.json`, suggested `<title>.workflow.json`; dismissed → `workflow.exportCanceled`, which the UI ignores) or, over IPC, an explicit `path`; either must be an absolute `.json` path whose folder exists and is not inside the project's `.exposition` folder or the app data folder, and not a link or folder (`workflow.file`: "Choose a file by its full location.", "Workflow files are .json files.", "The folder of X does not exist.", "That location holds Exposition's own records; choose another folder.", "X is not a regular file."); an existing file is replaced only when it is a regular file ≤ 8 MiB whose top-level `schema` is `exposition.workflow-bundle/1` (`workflow.invalidDestination`: "Refusing to replace a file that is not an Exposition workflow bundle; choose another name."); written atomically (`workflow.exportFailed`). **Import** (`workflow_bundle_import`, a path from the frontend's open dialog, the same location checks; `workflow.bundleInvalid` for > 8 MiB, non-UTF-8, or any bundle problem): a bundle or a single `/1`/`/2` definition, parsed strictly; every check runs before anything is saved: ≤ 32 children, revisions ≥ 1, each ≤ 1 MiB, no duplicate (id, revision), no child running the root, every pinned reference carried, no recursion or cycle, depth ≤ 3, and no child the root does not run ("a bundle carries only what the root runs"). Then children are saved in dependency order (several revisions of one exported id become successive revisions of one new id), each with its references rewritten to the new ids/revisions, and the root last; every save is an ordinary validated draft with `imported` set (incomplete drafts are saved with their diagnostics). Result `WorkflowBundleImport {root, children[{original, saved, title, state}]}`. Nothing is trusted, approved, triggered, or run. Saves are not one transaction: a storage failure part-way leaves the children already saved as imported drafts. A single definition imports as one draft with its composite references unchanged (they resolve against this project).

### 7.6 Codes
- `graph.` (operations): `empty`, `tooLarge`, `invalidTemp`, `duplicateTemp`, `unknownTemp`, `unknownEntity`, `tombstoned`, `notCreatable`, `invalidLabel`, `invalidKey`, `invalidAttributes`, `invalidRoles`, `invalidBinding`, `staleEntity`, `fileBacked`, `lifecycle`, `unknownRelation`, `reviewOnly`, `invalidStatus`, `observed`, `notManual`, `selfRelation`, `endpointRule`, `invalidNote`, `evidenceKind`, `evidenceNotCarried`, `evidenceSubject`, `serviceOwnedEvidence`, `unknownEvidence`, `duplicateRelation`, `rejected`, `sourceDerived`, `statementNotLocated`, `alreadyContained`, `cycle`.
- `graph.` (service): `unavailable`, `damaged`, `storage`, `noProject`, `temporary`, `noRecovery`, `recovery`, `projectChanged`, `invalidQuery` (text > 200, impact roots not 1–100), `invalidRequest`, `unknownProposal`, `notService`, `invalidBatch`, `excluded`, `unknownView`, `staleView`, `invalidView`, `reconciler` (the reconciler stopped), `reconcileTimeout` (reindexing still running after 5 min); `injectedFailure` only from the debug fault command `research_dev_fail_next_graph_write`.
- `blueprint.`: `tooLarge`, `schema`, `source`, `title`, `purpose`, `research`, `path`, `collision`, `content`, `tempId`, `entityKind`, `label`, `attributes`, `endpointRule`, `reference`, `duplicate`, `relationKind`, `selfRelation`, `note`, `capability`, `buildTarget`, `entry`, `graph`, `invalid`; warning `workflowIncomplete`.
- `project.`: `preset`, `destination`, `existingProject`, `notEmpty`, `trustedLocation`, `link`, `collision`, `io`, `outside`, `alias`, `journal`, `interrupted`, `researchSpace`, `inProgress`, `unknownOperation`, `resolved`, `recordMissing`; plus `workspace.*` destination codes.
- `workflow.` (validation): `schema`, `title`, `description`, `preset`, `tooLarge`, `invalidId`, `duplicateId`, `label`, `unsupportedVersion`, `unknownOperation`, `cycle`, `ambiguousCode`, `empty`, `unknownParameter`, `invalidParameter`, `unexpectedPayload`, `payloadLanguage`, `payload`, `payloadKind`, `inputUnset`, `inputVersion`, `inputMissing`, `unboundInput`, `missingCode`, `missingParameter`, `missingPrompt`, `noOutcome`, `orphanStep`, `inputTypeMismatch`, `unknownStep`, `unknownPort`, `unknownInput`, `incompatibleTypes`, `multipleBindings`, `outcomeType`, `ceilingExceeded`, `inputNotResource`, `effectNotAllowed`, `containment`, `inputType`, `serviceOwnedType`; schema /2 (M9): `maxParallel`, `compositeOperation`, `compositeMissing`, `compositeRecursive`, `compositeTooDeep`, `compositeNotWellFormed`, `compositeCeiling`, `tooManySteps`, `conditionSource`, `conditionInvalid`, `repeatInvalid`, `iterationFanIn`; warnings `unusedInput`, `compositeUnchecked`. Commands: `invalid` (import), `unknown`, `stale`, `unknownPreset`; bundles and exports (M9) `bundleInvalid`, `exportInvalid`, `exportCanceled` (the save dialog was dismissed), `exportFailed`, `invalidDestination`, `file`.
- `workflow.` (runs, §7.7): plan and start `noProject`, `graphUnavailable` (the graph is not attached for this project), `planStale` (plan unknown, already used, expired, approval hash mismatch, other project, or the recomputed plan differs), and the blockers `notWellFormed`, `inputMissing`, `inputChanged`, `codeTooLarge` (a code input over 1 MiB cannot be reviewed in full), `codeNotText` (a code input that is not UTF-8), `runnerUnavailable`, `untrustedFolder`, `restrictedUnavailable` (also: the configured image's identity could not be determined), `activeRun` (that workflow already has an active run, or 3 runs are active), and (M9) `compositeNotWellFormed` (a composite could not be expanded), `conditionSource` (a condition reads a for-each step); runner `canceled`, `conditionUnreadable` (a condition or until predicate could not be decided; the step fails), `untilExhausted` (an until step with `fail` ran its maximum without its predicate holding), `modelChanged` (an agent review step's provider or model is not the approved one; nothing was sent), `projectClosed`, `budgetExhausted`, `prerequisite`, `missingOutput`, `outputChanged`, `invalidOutput`, `forgedOutput`, `internal` (a step's service or the runner thread stopped unexpectedly), `interrupted`; step adapters `invalidInput`, `invalidParameter`, `missingPrompt`, `notRunOutput`, `declarationNotAudited`, `nothingAudited`, `tooLarge`, `timeout`; run records `unknownRun`, `notInterrupted`, `runActive`, `unknownStep`, `noProposal`, `repeatedProposal` (M9: the association step ran as several instances), `decided`, `store` (the history could not be used), `storeNewer` (the history was written by a newer version; P1), `untrusted` (a stored record failed decoding), `tooLarge` (a record over 32 MiB); manifests and reproduction (M9, §7.9) `runActive` (reproduce a run that has ended), `reproduceRevisionChanged`, `invalidDestination`, `exportFailed`, `exportCanceled`, `tooLarge` (a manifest over 16 MiB), `manifestUnavailable`.
- `trigger.` (M9, §7.8): `noInputs`, `invalidInterval`, `tooManyInputs`, `duplicateInput`, `unknownInput`, `codeInputWatched`, `inputPinned`, `invalidSettings`, `nativeNotAllowed`, `modelCapTooLow`, `planStale`, `tooMany`, `unknown`, `storeFailed` (a write failed), `storeUnavailable` (the store cannot be read, so nothing is changed or written); suspension codes `planChanged`, `trustRevoked`, `nativeNotAllowed` (plus the code of a blocker or start refusal that suspended it).
- Step codes from the services a step calls: `research.batchFailed`, `research.batchCanceled` (canceled before dispatch), `research.inputRefused`, `research.inputMissing`, `research.inputChanged`, `research.projectNotCurrent`, `research.environmentUnavailable` (restricted or native mode unavailable; never a fallback), `research.imageChanged` (the configured image is no longer the identity the batch was approved for; nothing runs; R and Julia images too), `research.invalidRequest` (also a script of another language, or a parameter that cannot be given to the batch: "parameter `<name>` cannot be given to the batch: …"), `research.notTrusted`, `research.launchFailed`; `lean.noAudit` (no complete declaration audit of the approved text, or the Lean service no longer holds it), `lean.noDeclarations`; `agent.approvalRequested`, `agent.refused`, `agent.failed`, `agent.stopped`, `agent.noAnswer`; and the calling service's own codes passed through (`agents.*`, `lean.*`, `promotion.*`, `graph.*`, `research.*`).

### 7.7 Workflow runs (M8, M9)

`crates/exposition-workflow` (`WorkflowRunService`, one per application); commands §10 "Workflow runs"; event `workflow-run` (§11). M9 adds bounded parallel steps, composites (expanded into the plan), conditions, for-each and until repeats, application-wide concurrency limits, budget reservation, and the run cause; triggers are §7.8, environment capture, manifests, and reproduction §7.9. No retries and no open-ended loops.

**Plan** (`workflow_run_plan(id, revision?)`; latest revision when absent). Needs the open project and its attached graph. The saved revision is re-validated (a stored validation is not trusted) and resolved into a `WorkflowRunPlan`:
- `inputs`: every external input as the run will read it: id, label, type, path, SHA-256 and size of the file now (it must resolve inside the project; links leading outside are refused), `pinned` when the definition pins a hash.
- `steps` in dependency order (the order they run, a condition's source before its step): operation and title, runner and title, containment, effects, the complete code or prompt payload, parameters, data bindings, prerequisites, time limit (`timeoutSeconds` clamped to 1 s–the operation's maximum, else the maximum; ≤ 4 h; a for-each step shows the largest of its instances'), output limit (the operation's), `maxModelRequests` (25 for an agent review step, else 0), `available` + `availability`, and (M9) `compositePath`, `condition`, `repeat`. Composite steps are expanded in place into the pinned child's steps with ids `<composite step>/<child step>` (nested `a/b/c`), `compositePath` = the enclosing composite steps' labels, outermost first; the child's inputs are bound to what the parent bound to the composite's ports, the composite's outputs map to the child steps that realize them, a prerequisite on a composite applies to each of its steps, and the child's own `maxParallel`, ceiling, annotations, and input sources (a version it pins for an input included) are not used. The pinned child is resolved by the rule validation uses (the stored revision, also of a workflow deleted with its revisions kept); only the workflow the plan is made for must be active (`workflow.unknown` for a deleted one). An expansion that fails although validation passed blocks the plan (`workflow.compositeNotWellFormed`).
- `budgets` (n = 1 for a step, the number of for-each values, or the until `maxIterations`): `maxSteps` = Σ n; `maxTotalSeconds` = Σ time limit × n, clamped to 1 s–4 h; `maxOutputBytes` = Σ output limit × n, ≤ 1 GiB; `maxModelRequests` = Σ 25 × n over agent review steps. Not editable.
- `maxParallel` (the definition's `execution.maxParallel`, 1–4, default 1) and `limits` (`WorkflowConcurrencyLimits`: `maxActiveRuns` 3, `maxInFlightSteps` 4, `restricted` 2, `native` 2, `agent` 2, `lean` 1; fixed in the application).
- `execution`: restricted backend (`docker`), the configured (Python) image and that image's engine identity `restrictedImageId` (`sha256:…`, from `docker image inspect`; only when a step uses that image), `runnerImages` (M9: `[{runner, image, imageId}]`, one per restricted R/Julia runner the plan uses, each with its own configured image and identity), folder trusted, native steps, model steps, and `model` (`{provider, providerTitle, model}`: the provider and model the agent review steps send their material to, from the provider settings when the plan is made; present only when the plan has such a step, and named in the model notice: "… send the declared material to the model provider (<Provider>, model <id>); with another provider or model the run does not start."). A restricted image whose identity Docker does not report blocks the plan (`workflow.restrictedUnavailable`, "The identity of the restricted image <img> could not be determined …; restricted steps run only in an identified image. Nothing runs natively instead.").
- `inputs[].text`: the complete text of each code input (port types code source and, since M9, Lean source, because elaborating Lean can run code), read from exactly the bytes its SHA-256 names (> 1 MiB → `workflow.codeTooLarge`, not UTF-8 → `workflow.codeNotText`); the plan review shows it on the step that runs it ("Lean source <path> (N lines, <hash>) — the audit elaborates exactly this text (elaboration can run code)").
- `notices`: with `maxParallel` 1 "Nothing runs until you start the run. Steps run one at a time in the order shown; a failed step stops the workflow, and nothing is retried or repeated." (with repeats: "…, and nothing is retried, and repeated steps run only their declared instances."), otherwise "Nothing runs until you start the run. Up to N independent steps run at the same time (a step starts once everything it depends on succeeded; across all runs at most 4 steps at once: 2 restricted, 2 native, 2 agent, 1 Lean). Budgets are reserved before each step starts. A failed step stops the workflow: nothing new starts, steps already running finish, and nothing is retried or repeated." (the same ending with repeats); per condition "“<step>” runs only when <test, negated in plain words when negated, e.g. “the output is not produced”, “the verdict is not one of …”, “the value at /x ≥ 3”> (output “<port>” of “<source>”); otherwise it does not run (condition not met), and the steps that need it are skipped."; per for-each "“<step>” runs once for each of N values of <parameter> (each instance counts as a step)."; per until "“<step>” runs again, at most N times, until <test> for its output “<port>”; if that never happens, the step fails / the last iteration's outputs are used."; restricted steps, one notice per image ("“<step>” runs in the restricted container (docker, image <img>): only the declared inputs and code are staged read-only, with no network and no credentials; outputs are validated before anything uses them."); native steps ("… run natively with your account's privileges. This is not a sandbox."); model steps ("… send the declared material to the model provider."); promotions are only prepared; evidence associations are only proposed and "never assert that a formal declaration is equivalent to an informal statement".
- `blockers` (non-empty: cannot start): `workflow.notWellFormed` (first 3 errors), `workflow.inputMissing`, `workflow.inputChanged` (a pinned hash differs), `workflow.runnerUnavailable` / `workflow.untrustedFolder` / `workflow.restrictedUnavailable` per step, `workflow.compositeNotWellFormed`, `workflow.conditionSource` (a condition reads a for-each step; validation also refuses this), `workflow.activeRun` ("A run of this workflow is active; wait for it to finish or cancel it first." or "3 workflow runs are active (at most 3 at once); wait for one to finish or cancel it first.").
- `approvalHash`: SHA-256 over a canonical serialization (`exposition.workflowRunApproval/2`; `/1` before M9) of the project identity (canonical root, case-insensitive on Windows, with or without `\\?\`), workflow id, revision, definition hash, inputs, every step's approved fields (including payload, parameters, bindings, limits, `maxModelRequests`, `available`, `compositePath`, `condition`, and `repeat`), ceiling, budgets, `maxParallel`, `limits`, and execution (backend, image, and image identity, the runner images; folder trust only when the plan has native steps; the review steps' provider id and model only when the plan has model steps, so every other plan hashes as before). Notices, blockers, availability text, times, and the run cause are not covered.
- Plans are kept in memory (≤ 32, shared by manual, reproduction, and trigger plans; the oldest dropped; a trigger's checks use a preview that is not kept and cannot be started, and only the plan of a run it actually starts is kept), expire 30 min after creation, and are single-use: a start request consumes the plan whether or not it starts.
- Plan review additions (M9, `PlanReview.tsx`): under Execution, the R and Julia runner images ("<runner id> runs in `<image>`, exactly the image <hash>", or "(identity not recorded)"); a concurrency row (`plan-concurrency`: "One step at a time." or "Up to N independent steps (or repeat instances) at the same time; a step starts only after everything it reads or runs after has finished.", then "Across all runs: at most 3 active runs (one per workflow) and 4 steps in flight; per kind at once: 2 restricted container, 2 native, 2 model, 1 Lean."); the steps heading "Steps, in the order they run (one at a time)" or "Steps, in dependency order (up to N at a time)"; the expanded steps of each composite grouped under "Inside the workflow step “A” › “B” — N step(s), shown in full", each with its complete code or prompt; per step "Condition" and "Repeats" rows in plain words, and, when steps may run together, limits "reserved from the run's budgets before the step starts, so steps running together cannot exceed them". A reproduction plan adds "Reproduces" ("Run <id>: every input is pinned to the content that run read. The new run records it as its cause.") and a warning banner "The environment changed since the recorded run (N)" listing the drift (recorded and current values) with "Approving runs it in the current environment; the results may differ."

**Start** (`workflow_run_start({plan, approvalHash})`): refused with `workflow.planStale` when the plan is unknown, used, or expired, the hash differs, or another project is open; then the plan is recomputed for the same requested revision and refused with `workflow.planStale` if its hash differs, with what changed ("The plan is out of date: the workflow was saved again (revision a → b); input “x” (path) changed (sha256 … → …); the project folder is no longer trusted; the restricted execution image changed (a → b); the restricted image x is now a different image (sha256:… → sha256:…); the runner of “s” is no longer available; the model provider or model of the agent review steps changed (<Provider>, model a → <Provider>, model b), or, for a plan approved before the provider was recorded, the plan now names the model provider and model its agent review steps send material to (…); for an R or Julia runner image: the restricted image changed (a → b) / the restricted image x is now a different image (… → …) / the restricted image x is no longer configured / the restricted image x (sha256:…) is now configured (R/Julia runner images); the steps that may run at the same time changed (a → b); the open project changed. Review the new plan before starting."); then the first blocker's code; then `workflow.activeRun` (one active run per workflow, at most 3 application-wide; checked again under the lock). The run (state `running`, every step `pending`, a for-each step's instances created `pending` with their values, the plan exactly as approved, and its `cause`) is written to the store before its runner thread (`workflow-run`) starts and is returned. **Cause** (`WorkflowRunCause`, tagged `kind`): `{manual}` for a reviewed plan the user started, `{trigger, trigger: <id>, changed: [{input, before, after}]}` (bare SHA-256 hex or null) for a trigger's run (§7.8), `{reproduce, of: <run id>}` for a reproduction (§7.9); the cause grants nothing, is not in the approval hash, and reads `manual` for records written before M9. Application code plans with options (`PlanOptions {cause, pins, notices}`: input pins override an input's version and a mismatch blocks with `workflow.inputChanged`; the options are reapplied when the plan is recomputed at start).

**Scheduling.** A step is ready once every step it takes data from, every explicit prerequisite, and its condition's source have finished. Ready steps start in plan order (a for-each step's instances lowest index first), at most `maxParallel` of the run at once (1: exactly one step at a time, in plan order, as in M8), and only while an application-wide slot is free (`limits`, shared by every active run): every step takes one of the 4 in-flight slots plus one of its kind: restricted container (2), native (2), agent review (2), or Lean audit (1, which also takes a native slot); application-service steps (association, retention, promotion) take only the in-flight slot. A step that finds a slot or its budget reservation unavailable waits (it stays `pending`, retried every 20 ms) and is never refused for it; while it waits, later ready steps of the same run do not start either. A step one of whose needs did not run (skipped or condition not met) is `skipped` ("“<step>”, whose output it needs, did not run."; a step that only runs after it: "“<step>”, which it runs after, did not run."); a step one of whose needs failed or was canceled is not started (the run is stopping).

**Conditions** are evaluated once, when the step is ready and the run has a free place. If the source did not run, the step is `skipped` ("“<source>”, whose output its condition reads, did not run.") unless the test is `outputPresent`, which is decided on the missing output. Otherwise the source's validated output is read: a verdict test reads only formal evidence's overall verdict label (exact label or its class: "Errors (3)" is Errors); a JSON comparison re-hashes the dataset file against the digest recorded when it was validated (`workflow.outputChanged` if it differs), reads it only if ≤ 1 MiB, parses it, and resolves the pointer (numbers compared exactly as decimals (a document number with a fraction or beyond the 64-bit integer range is read as its shortest double-precision decimal; "the number cannot be compared" otherwise); text and true/false by equality); `outputPresent` asks whether the optional output was produced; `negate` inverts. False → the step is `conditionNotMet`, never dispatched (no attempt, no usage), with the reason "Its condition did not hold: it runs only when <test, negated in plain words when negated>, and <observation>." (e.g. "the value at /fit/r2 of output “dataset” of “Fit” is 0.42"). A source that cannot be read or does not fit the test (not a dataset file, larger than 1 MiB, not JSON, no value at the pointer, a value of another type, no verdict, an unproduced output for a non-presence test) fails the step with `workflow.conditionUnreadable` as a never-dispatched attempt ("Not started: its condition could not be evaluated: …"), and so the run.

**Repeats.** For-each: each instance runs the step with the parameter set to its value (and its own time limit); adapters also receive the instance index and value. The step succeeds when every instance has; consumers then receive all instances' outputs per bound port in index order. A failed instance fails the step and the run (running instances finish; pending ones are skipped, "The run stopped (a step failed) before this instance could start."). Until: iterations run one after another (an instance is created as each starts); after each, the predicate is evaluated on that iteration's validated output ("Its predicate held: …" / "Its predicate did not hold: …"); when it holds, the step succeeds with that iteration's outputs; after `maxIterations` without it, `fail` fails the step ("Its predicate did not hold in any of its N iterations.", run code `workflow.untilExhausted`) and `useLast` succeeds with the last iteration's outputs. An unreadable predicate fails the iteration, its attempt (code `workflow.conditionUnreadable` or `workflow.outputChanged`, "The iteration ran, but its predicate could not be evaluated (…); its outputs are discarded."), the step, and the run. Every instance counts as one step dispatch, reserves budget, holds a slot, and has its own attempts; output provenance names its `instance`. Batches (every language) receive `EXPOSITION_ITERATION` = the 0-based instance index (repeated steps only) and their own `EXPOSITION_SEED`, and every parameter set on the step after the for-each value is applied as `EXPOSITION_PARAM_<NAME>` (§7.9, Research batches step 4); a for-each over a batch's `value` gives each instance its value as `EXPOSITION_PARAM_VALUE`.

**Before each dispatch**, in order: canceled → run `canceled`; the run's project is no longer the open one → `canceled` with `workflow.projectClosed`; budgets → `workflow.budgetExhausted` ("Not started: the run may dispatch at most N steps." / "… the run's time budget of N s is used up." / "… the run's output budget of N bytes is used up." / "… the run's budget of N model requests is used up."): the step's output limit (when it has one) and, for an agent review, 25 model requests are reserved from what is left after the reservations of the run's steps in flight, and a step whose reservation does not fit while others are in flight waits for them; the grant is min(limit, left) and is released when the step ends; the time budget is wall-clock time since the run started, not reserved; a prerequisite did not succeed → `workflow.prerequisite`; every bound external input re-hashed and equal to the approved hash (`workflow.inputChanged`), every upstream output present (`workflow.missingOutput`) and, for files, re-hashed and equal (`workflow.outputChanged`); a native step's folder still trusted (`workflow.untrustedFolder`); the adapter installed (`workflow.runnerUnavailable`); a restricted step's backend and image equal to the approved ones and the image's current identity equal to the approved identity, for an R or Julia step its runner's bound image and identity (`workflow.restrictedUnavailable`, "Not started: the restricted image is not the image you approved (approved <img> <id>, now …); restricted steps run only in the approved image."); the batch carries the approved identity, the research batch runner refuses another one (`research.imageChanged`), and the container is started from that identity, not the tag; the adapter available now (`workflow.restrictedUnavailable` / `workflow.runnerUnavailable`, "it is never replaced by another runner"). A refusal is recorded as a failed attempt that was never dispatched. Then the attempt is persisted as `intent` (and the step counted) before the service is called; without a durable intent nothing is dispatched. The adapter reports the service operation as soon as it exists (`researchRun`, `leanAudit`, `associationProposal`, `agentTask`, `retain`, `promotion`), and the attempt is persisted as `dispatched` with it. The step's time limit is capped by the run's remaining wall-clock time, and its output limit and model requests by its grant. Every change of the run record is made and written through under one lock, so concurrent steps never lose each other's updates. A panic in a step's service fails the step (`workflow.internal`, "nothing was repeated"); a panic of the runner thread cancels the run's steps and fails the run ("The workflow runner stopped unexpectedly. Nothing was repeated; start a new run."), releasing its slots. A failed or refused step fails the run (the first failure is the run's code): nothing new starts, steps already running finish and keep their results, and the remaining steps become `skipped` ("The run stopped (a step failed) before this step could start.").
