Model requests a step made count in the run's usage whether it succeeded, failed, was canceled, or had its outputs refused. After a step, usage beyond the output budget fails the run (`workflow.budgetExhausted`); there is no such check for model requests (a review's task is capped at its grant).

**Model binding.** Before an agent review step is dispatched, the provider and model configured now must be the plan's `execution.model` (`workflow.modelChanged`, "Not started: the model provider or model is not the one you approved (approved …, now …); nothing was sent."), and the review task is created with exactly that provider and model rather than the settings' default. A plan or run record written before `execution.model` existed has none and is not checked.

**Final record.** The record a run ends with is written before the run stops being active: the whole record (tried again after 50 ms and 250 ms when the store fails for another reason than size). Only a record the store refuses for its size (`workflow.tooLarge`) is cut down: it is stored without attempt logs and without the reviewed text of code inputs, and its `message` then ends "The record of this run could not be written in full (…), so it is kept without the steps' logs and without the reviewed text of its code inputs; states, outputs, and digests are complete." When the store does not take the record (another failure that outlasts the retries, or the reduced record refused too), the whole record is kept in memory for this session: `workflow_run_get` and `workflow_runs` return the run as it ended (a reproduction is seeded from it too), and each of them first tries to write it again. An acknowledgement or association decision made on such a run replaces the waiting record when its own write fails and removes it when the write succeeds, so an older copy is never written over a newer stored one. Only a session that ends before the store accepts it leaves the row `running`, which the next start reads as interrupted.

**Output validation** (before any consumer or outcome sees an output; the step's own claim is not trusted): formal evidence only from `lean.audit` and retained artifacts only from `artifact.retain` (`workflow.forgedOutput`); the port is a declared output with its declared type, and the value kind matches the type; provenance names this run, step, attempt, instance (repeated steps), and the operation reported at dispatch (a different operation returned at the end is refused); a file must belong to this attempt's research run, lie under `.exposition/artifacts/<research run>/`, resolve inside the project, and match its recorded SHA-256 and size (`workflow.outputChanged` otherwise); a retained artifact must match its digest; formal evidence must carry a SHA-256 that equals the Lean file's current content (`workflow.inputChanged`) and ≤ 2000 declarations; a report ≤ 65,536 characters; a port returned twice is refused. One refused output discards all of the step's outputs and fails it (`workflow.invalidOutput` / `forgedOutput` / `outputChanged`). Every port that a later step binds or a required outcome names must be present (`workflow.missingOutput`, "output port p is realized by the file p.<ext>"), with two exceptions for the `outputPresent` predicate: a binding of a step whose own condition is a non-negated `outputPresent` on that same port does not make the port required (without it that step reads Condition not met), and for an until step whose predicate is a non-negated `outputPresent`, the tested port is not required of an iteration unless it is the last one and `onExhausted` is `useLast` (earlier iterations run again; an exhausted `fail` step ends with `workflow.untilExhausted`). Accepted outputs realize the run's outcomes.

**States.** Run: `running`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `canceled`, `interrupted` (with `code`, `message`, `acknowledged`, `cause`). Step: `pending` (Not reached; also while waiting for a slot), `running`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `canceled`, `skipped` (an earlier step did not produce what it needs, or the run stopped first), `interrupted`, `conditionNotMet` (its own condition did not hold; not a failure). A step has a `reason` when it did not run or ended early, and a repeated step has `instances` (`index`, `parameter` (for-each value, or null for until), `state`, `attempts`, `reason`; its own `attempts` stay empty). A run whose steps were skipped or not met can still succeed; outcomes carry a `reason` when not produced ("“X” succeeded without producing this optional output.", "“X” failed: …", "“X” was canceled.", "“X” was interrupted when Exposition stopped; its result is unknown.", "“X” did not run. <reason>", "“X” ran N instances; each instance's output is listed with the step."). Attempt: `intent` (Dispatch recorded), `dispatched`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `canceled`, `interrupted` (Outcome unknown); each with the operation, intent/dispatch/finish times, code, message, validated outputs, refused outputs (name + reason), and a bounded, redacted log (≤ 65,536 characters, the tail). Usage: steps, seconds, output bytes (accepted files, including files that realize no port), model requests.

**Cancel, project, trust.** `workflow_run_cancel` ("You canceled the run.", `workflow.canceled`) starts nothing new and stops every running step and instance through its service (a batch's job is canceled and its container removed, a review's agent task is canceled, a Lean audit is no longer waited for); the runner waits for all of them, and the command waits up to 5 s for the run to stop (it may return the run still `running`). Running steps and instances become `canceled`; steps never dispatched `skipped` ("The run was canceled before this step could start."); a repeated step between instances `canceled` ("The run stopped before its remaining instances could start."); already validated outputs stay listed. Closing or switching the project cancels every active run of that project (`workflow.projectClosed`, "The project was closed or switched, so the run was canceled. Nothing resumes when the project is reopened."). Revoking trust for the folder (or a folder containing it) cancels every active run there whose plan has a native step (a Lean audit included), all its steps with it (`workflow.untrustedFolder`, "… its native steps run with your account's privileges and need a trusted folder."); the execution service stops its native jobs.

**Restart.** Each change is written through to the store. When the service starts, every run still `running` becomes `interrupted` (`workflow.interrupted`, "Exposition stopped while this run was active. Nothing was resumed or repeated; …"): running steps and instances `interrupted` ("Exposition stopped while this was running; its result is unknown and it was not repeated."), pending steps and instances `skipped` ("Exposition stopped before this could start; nothing resumes."), every attempt in `intent` or `dispatched`, those of instances included, `interrupted` ("… was dispatched when Exposition stopped; its outcome is unknown and it was not repeated." / "… was about to be dispatched …"). Nothing is re-dispatched. `workflow_run_acknowledge` marks an interrupted run reviewed (`acknowledged`; other states `workflow.notInterrupted`); a new run must be planned and started. On app exit the active run's jobs are killed with the execution service. A research batch manifest left `running` reads as failed ("Exposition stopped before this run finished."), and batch staging folders are removed at the next start.

**Adapters.**
- `restrictedPython` / `nativePython`, and (M9) `restrictedR` / `nativeR` / `restrictedJulia` / `nativeJulia` (research batch runner, one adapter per language and mode; the payload must be in the step's language, `workflow.payloadLanguage` "“<label>” runs R code; its code is not R.", and a bound script of another language is refused, `workflow.invalidInput` "Not started: <path> is not an R script (.R), which … runs."): code = the inline payload, or the bound `script` input (exactly one); declared inputs = the files bound to `data`; each output port is offered the extensions its type accepts and the batch exports (dataset: csv, tsv, json, txt; figure: svg, png, jpg, jpeg, pdf; table: csv, tsv, html, json). See **Research batches** below. Batch `succeeded` is required; a non-zero exit, time limit, or flood fails the step with `research.batchFailed` ("The Python batch failed (exit code N): …"); canceled → `workflow.canceled`. Accepted files that realize no port are kept as run artifacts and counted against the output budget. Execution label: restricted container, or trusted native. The batch receives `EXPOSITION_SEED` (§7.9) and reports its environment.
- `leanAudit` (trusted native): exactly one bound Lean file; the operation id (`<path>@<sha256>`) is persisted before Lean runs; the Lean service's check of that file's document (opened if needed; at most 600 s, the operation's limit and the Lean service's maximum) and then its audit record of the same revision and text (`lean.noAudit` otherwise). The audited text's SHA-256 must equal the plan's (`workflow.inputChanged`: an unsaved or changed buffer is not audited on the plan's behalf); no declarations → `lean.noDeclarations`. Output `FormalEvidence {file, fileSha256, verdict, declarations[{name, verdict, statement?, axioms}], evidence: []}`: the file status label (Checked, Checked with nonstandard axioms, Incomplete (n sorry), Relies on compiled code, Partially checked: …, Unverified: …, Errors (n), …; a truncated audit says "audit list truncated: x of y declarations listed; unlisted declarations have no evidence of their own") and each audited declaration's own verdict (Checked, Checked with nonstandard axioms, Incomplete, Relies on compiled code). Records no evidence itself. These are the stored labels; the run view shows them in the Lean view's words (§7.5 Contract, `storedVerdictLabel`).
- `graphProposal` (`formal.associateEvidence`): exactly one formal-evidence input; the `declarations` parameter (one name per line) must all be in the audit's list (`workflow.declarationNotAudited`; empty = every audited declaration; none → `workflow.nothingAudited`; > 200 → `workflow.tooLarge`); output `AssociationProposal {proposal "assoc-…", declarations, status pending, accepted [], evidence []}`. Records nothing. **Decide** (`workflow_association_decide({run, step, accept[]})`, run not active (`workflow.runActive`), ≤ 200 names): the step's upstream formal evidence is required (`workflow.noProposal`), the proposal must be pending (`workflow.decided`), every accepted name must be in the proposal and the audit (`workflow.declarationNotAudited`); accepted names get service-owned `FormalCheck` evidence through the Lean evidence path, which requires the Lean service's current audit record of the file with the same SHA-256 (`lean.noAudit`, `workflow.inputChanged`); status `accepted` (with `accepted` and `evidence`), or `rejected` when none is accepted. Decide takes the audit step's last successful attempt, of its instances too (an until audit: the iteration whose evidence the association consumed); an association step that itself repeats is refused (`workflow.repeatedProposal`, "This association step ran as several instances; association proposals are decided only for a step that runs once."), although validation and the plan accept it.
- `readOnlyAgent` (`agent.review`): needs the prompt payload and at least one file among the material (files, retained artifacts, and the Lean file of formal evidence; formal-evidence verdicts and earlier reports are added as text, ≤ 32,768 characters); starts an ordinary agent task: approval mode Ask (never Auto), scope = those files, permission read-only, execution disabled, consent file contents in scope, budgets `maxTurns` = the step's model-request grant (min(25, what the run has left); a grant of 0 fails the step with `workflow.budgetExhausted`, "The run has no model requests left."), `maxWallMs` = the step's time limit, `maxCommands` 0, others default; the prompt states the step's label, that it is read-only, the material, and the instructions. The task is polled; a task that asks for approval is stopped (`agent.approvalRequested`); budget exceeded → `workflow.budgetExhausted`; refused, failed, stopped → `agent.refused` / `agent.failed` / `agent.stopped`; no answer → `agent.noAnswer`; the step's limit + 30 s → stopped, `workflow.timeout`. Output `Report {text (the last non-empty assistant text, cut after 65,536 characters with "…"), task}`; the task's turns count as the step's model requests. A report is prose, never evidence. Several reviews may run at once (at most 2 across the application), each its own read-only task over its own material; `material` takes several sources, received in connection order (a for-each producer's in index order).
- `researchRetention` (`artifact.retain`): exactly one bound output of a research run (`workflow.notRunOutput` for a plain project file); `ResearchProject::retain` (state active; re-verifies the artifact digest) → `Retained {entity, path, sha256, evidence, researchRun, artifact}` with computational evidence naming the run's actual execution mode.
- `reviewedPromotion` (`manuscript.requestPromotion`): exactly one retained artifact and the `document` parameter; prepares the existing reviewed promotion (§4.1) of that artifact at the end of the manuscript's open document (current revision; optional caption) → `PromotionProposal {promotion, document, status, attachmentKey, expiresAtMs}`. Nothing is inserted until the user approves it in the run view (research promotion commands). When a run is read, a pending proposal reads `accepted` once research follows the attachment in that document or its markers are in the saved file, `rejected` once the user discarded the prepared review, and `expired` when the research service no longer holds it (approved elsewhere without the insertion surviving, or lost when Exposition restarted) or after the 30-min review window.

**Research batches** (`crates/exposition-research/src/sandbox`, `BatchRunner`; restricted = Docker, trusted native = a new process):
1. The project must be research's open workspace (`research.projectNotCurrent`), the request valid (time limit > 0, owner run + step, port names `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}` not starting with `.`, only exported extensions), and the mode available now, else refused before anything is staged (`research.environmentUnavailable`, "Nothing was run, and nothing runs natively instead."). Restricted: an image configured, a valid reference, Docker found, the engine reachable, the image present locally (`docker image inspect`; never pulled); its image id is recorded, and (M9) the image's environment is read from the per-identity cache or, the first time, probed in its own container before staging (§7.9). Native: a trusted folder and a discovered interpreter of the step's language (Python, `Rscript`, `julia`; the first available one, else the first with an executable); its discovery report is the environment recorded. Restricted R/Julia: their own configured image (never the Python image); no environment probe.
2. **Staging** into `<data>/research/batches/<research run>/work`: only the declared inputs (≤ 32, each ≤ 1 GiB, ≤ 2 GiB in total) and the code (inline ≤ 4 MiB written as `__exposition_main__.py` / `__exposition_main__.R` / `__exposition_main__.jl` by language, a reserved name (a declared input with it is refused); or a declared script of the step's language (`.py`; `.R`/`.r`; `.jl`) at its project path (another extension: `research.invalidRequest` "<path> is not an R script (.R)")), each copied create-new and verified against its SHA-256 while copying (`research.inputChanged`). Every declared path is checked first: no link, junction, or name-surrogate reparse point anywhere on the path, a regular file, not the graph's storage or a path the graph's admission excludes for any reason other than an unrecognised or generated file type (run records, `.exposition/` metadata, version-control and tool folders, agent instruction files: `research.inputRefused`), and the name the file system resolves must be the declared name (no 8.3 or other alias); missing → `research.inputMissing`. A cancel during staging dispatches nothing (`research.batchCanceled`).
3. The manifest (`.exposition/runs/<run>.json`, status running, owner `workflow{run, step}`, the pinned inputs, the code provenance, `execution` = the actual mode) is written before launch; restricted settings that changed since the check refuse the batch. The job is launched through the execution service and the research run id reported.
4. **Batch variables** (M9, every language and mode, checked before anything is staged): `EXPOSITION_SEED=<decimal>`; for a repeated step `EXPOSITION_ITERATION=<0-based instance>`; and each parameter set on the step (for-each value applied) as `EXPOSITION_PARAM_<NAME>`, the name an ASCII identifier ≤ 64 characters converted from camelCase to UPPER_SNAKE (`timeoutSeconds` → `EXPOSITION_PARAM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, `value` → `EXPOSITION_PARAM_VALUE`), the value as text (integers in decimal, booleans `true`/`false`); refused before anything runs (`research.invalidRequest`, "parameter `<name>` cannot be given to the batch: …") when the name is not an identifier or looks like a credential, the value is longer than 4096 bytes, is not one line, or contains a secret, or two parameters map to one variable. **Restricted:** a Docker job (flags §12) started from the approved image identity, running `python3 <entry>` (R: `Rscript --vanilla <entry>`; Julia: `julia --startup-file=no --history-file=no <entry>`, in the configured R / Julia image) with the working copy as the registered root, mounted read-only at `/workspace` (the project folder is never mounted), `/out` as the writable output area, and a constructed environment: `PATH`, `HOME=/tmp`, `TMPDIR=/tmp`, `LANG=C.UTF-8`, `EXPOSITION_OUTPUT_DIR=/out`, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1`, `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8`, `MPLBACKEND=Agg`, `MPLCONFIGDIR=/tmp/matplotlib` (Python only; Julia's `PATH` starts with `/usr/local/julia/bin`, its depot is `/tmp/.julia` on the tmpfs, then the image's), and the batch variables. Docker's own failure after the check (exit 125 with a `docker:` line) is reported as the backend unavailable, not as the code's failure. **Trusted native:** the interpreter runs `<work>/<entry>` as a TrustedNative job in the working copy (registered as an application folder bound to the project, so the trust gate applies), with the research environment plus `EXPOSITION_OUTPUT_DIR=<data>/research/batches/<run>/out`, `MPLBACKEND=Agg` (Python only), and the batch variables; R runs the discovered `Rscript --vanilla <script>`, Julia `julia --startup-file=no --history-file=no <script>` (plain absolute paths), with discovery's `R_LIBS_USER` / `JULIA_DEPOT_PATH`; never a user's or an agent's kernel. R and Julia errors are taken from the last `Error in … :` / `Error:` line or the first `ERROR:` (≤ 500 characters).
5. Waiting: cancellation cancels the job (the backend removes the container); the time limit stops it; about every 0.5 s the output area is measured and the job is stopped when it exceeds min(output budget, 512 MiB) + 64 MiB ("wrote more than N MiB to its output area"). Job logs ≤ 1 MiB per stream; the batch log keeps the last 8 KiB of each stream (redacted).
6. **Output registration** (also after a failure): at most 256 entries of the output folder are inspected (more are reported, unread); accepted only regular files directly in it (folders, links, junctions, reparse points, special files, and files with more than one name are refused without being followed), names `[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,100}` without a leading or trailing dot, not a Windows device name, not `transcript.json`, with an exported extension (png, jpg, jpeg, gif, svg, pdf, csv, tsv, json, txt, html), ≤ 32 files, each ≤ 64 MiB, together ≤ min(output budget, 512 MiB), content matching the type (PNG/JPEG/GIF/PDF magic bytes; SVG: UTF-8 containing `<svg`, no NUL; JSON parses; CSV/TSV/TXT/HTML: UTF-8 without NUL). Text types are redacted, then each file is copied create-new to `.exposition/artifacts/<run>/<name>` with its SHA-256 (figure: png/jpg/jpeg/gif/svg/pdf; table: csv/tsv; data: others). Port `p` is realized by `p.<ext>` (the port's first matching extension); other accepted files are extra artifacts. Every refusal is listed (name + reason) and noted in the run transcript ("Output x was not exported: …").
7. The manifest is finalized (status, exit code, Python exception name and message from stderr, outputs) and the staging folder removed.

**Run monitor** (`workflow-run:<id>` tab "Run: <title>", kept current by `workflow-run` events: the run view and the run lists re-read once per burst of events, at once when idle and once more after a read in flight; while its project is not open it shows "This run's project is not open" with Close and no Cancel run, and re-reads the run when the project opens again; a failed re-read shows "Could not refresh this run" with Retry; Recent runs and run history follow the open project): state pill; Cancel run (running), or after the run ended (not interrupted) **Run again** (A1: plans the run's revision again; when the new plan's approval hash equals the plan that run was approved with and nothing blocks it, the press starts it directly through `workflow_run_start` as the user's explicit action, cause `manual`; otherwise, or when the start is refused as stale, the plan review opens with the reason in an info banner (`plan-review-notice`, e.g. "The plan differs from the one this run was approved with (inputs, steps, limits, or execution changed); review it before starting."); any other failure shows a "Run not started" banner); for an interrupted run the banner "The application stopped during this run" ("Nothing was repeated automatically. A step marked “Outcome unknown” may have partly run; check its effects before starting a new run.") with Mark as reviewed and Review a new run; failure banner with the run's code and message; usage bars against the budgets (steps, time, output, model requests); each step with operation, runner, containment badge (Restricted container / Runs natively with your account's privileges / Sends material to the model provider / Application service), state, and its attempts (state, operation, times, error, log, validated outputs with port type and "validated · from <operation> (attempt n)", refused outputs); file outputs show path, media type, size, and SHA-256, images previewed only through `<img>` (SVG included) and CSV/TSV as plain cells (≤ 200 rows), anything else listed only; retained results; formal evidence with the file verdict and each declaration's verdict and axioms; reports as plain text; a promotion proposal ("Review the change…": the prepared markup in full; Discard proposal / Approve insertion or Approve update; while pending, when the prepared change can be reviewed until; once accepted, the inserted attachment key); an association proposal (checklist of the proposed declarations with the audit's verdict, axioms, and statement; Reject all / Accept selected (n); once decided, each declaration marked Associated or Not associated); outcomes (Produced / Not produced, Optional, and the reason when not produced); "The plan exactly as approved" (collapsed). M9 additions: the cause under the header ("Started by you from a reviewed plan."; "Started by trigger <id> under its approved standing plan after N watched input(s) changed." with "<label> (<path>): <old> → <new>" short digests, full in the tooltip, "absent" for none; "Reproduction of run <id>, from a reviewed plan pinned to that run's recorded inputs." with **Open the reproduced run**); while running, **Running now (N)** ("of at most M at once" when M > 1; each step or instance with since-time; "Nothing is in flight: the next step waits for its inputs or a free slot."); a repeated step's sentence and its instances ("<parameter> = <value>" or "Iteration k", state, reason, attempts; "N instances: 2 succeeded, 1 running"), outputs labelled "(attempt n, instance k)"; **Condition not met** ("Not run: its condition was not met. …", info-tinted) distinct from **Skipped** ("Not run: an earlier step did not produce what it needs. …", neutral, dashed); per attempt a collapsed **Environment** (runner and containment, system, image name + identity, interpreter, toolchain + manifest digest, package count + digest with "Show the N package(s)", model, seed, notes; "No environment was recorded for this attempt." for a dispatched attempt without one); after the run, **Export manifest…** (backend save dialog; dismissal quiet; success banner with path, size, SHA-256; errors in a "Not done" banner), a collapsed "Run manifest" section (**Show the manifest**: pretty JSON), and **Reproduce…** (a plan review "Reproduce “<title>”", §7.9). Runs lists: the Workflows tab ("Recent runs", every workflow) and the editor (that workflow): title, revision, start time, "n of m steps" (expanded steps; succeeded and condition-not-met ones count as done), state, "Needs review" for an unacknowledged interrupted run, "Trigger" / "Reproduction" badges, Open. Run lists are project-operation surfaces (§8): Delete Run Record (the record only; restorable; a running run is stopped first only if chosen), Delete Permanently, Delete Run and Its Outputs (outputs held for restore) and Delete Run and Its Outputs Permanently… (the record and its `runOutputs` in one request); a run is never copied or duplicated.

**Store:** `<data>/workflow-runs.sqlite3` (WAL, `synchronous=FULL`, `trusted_schema=OFF`, 5 s busy timeout, `user_version` 1 since P1), table `runs(id, project, workflow, state, started_at, body, deleted_at)` (`deleted_at` added in P1: a recoverably deleted run is left out of lists and reads as unknown; permanent deletion removes the row and compacts the database; an older history is first copied aside with `VACUUM INTO` as `workflow-runs.sqlite3.premigration-v<N>-<ms>` and is not upgraded when that copy fails; a history written by a newer version is refused (`workflow.storeNewer`), left byte for byte unchanged, and the session keeps its runs in memory), keyed by the project identity above, so runs are listed only for their own project; the body is the whole run as JSON (≤ 32 MiB), rewritten on every change. Rows are read as untrusted data: oversized, undecodable, or inconsistent rows (id or state differs from the indexed columns, > 100 steps, > 20 instances per step, > 8 attempts per step or instance, an oversized log) are skipped in lists and refused by id (`workflow.untrusted`). `workflow_runs` returns ≤ 100 runs, newest first; summaries carry the `cause`. A listing reads the index `runs_listing(project, started_at DESC, id, workflow, state, deleted_at)` and decodes a record only when its summary is not already held: summaries are kept per row for the session (≤ 2,048, then all dropped) while the row's `id` and `state` read the same, dropped for a row this store rewrites or removes, and all dropped on a commit by another connection or a compaction. The index is optional: a history that cannot take it opens and lists from the table. A history that cannot be opened is set aside as `workflow-runs.sqlite3.bad-<ms>` (with its `-wal`/`-shm`) and a new one started (in memory if that also fails), so the application always starts. Run ids `wrun-<32 hex>`, plan ids `wplan-<32 hex>`.

### 7.8 Workflow triggers (M9)

`crates/exposition-workflow/src/triggers/` (`TriggerService`, one per application, its own driver thread), `src-tauri/src/workflow_triggers.rs`; commands §10 "Workflow triggers"; event `workflow-trigger` (§11). A trigger is an explicit, user-only standing approval: only the four `workflow_trigger_*` commands (webview only) create or change one; there is no agent tool, and definitions, bundles, blueprints, and the graph have no trigger fields (unknown fields are refused). No record exists until the user enables one.

- **Kinds** (`TriggerKind`, tagged `kind`): `inputsChanged {inputs}`: saved content of these external inputs fires it, and they are the only inputs allowed to change; `interval {minutes, inputs}`: fires every 15–10080 minutes (one week) while the project is open, whether or not anything changed; `inputs` (default none) may change between runs without asking.
- **Standing plan** (`workflow_trigger_plan({workflow, kind})`, latest saved revision; computed as a preview that is not kept as a startable plan): the complete `WorkflowRunPlan` (§7.7, code and Lean texts included), `kind`, `watched` inputs (`{input, label, path, portType}`), `standingHash` = the plan's approval hash computed with each watched input's SHA-256, size, and text blanked (so only the watched content may differ), `mayChange` ("The saved content of “<label>” (<path>, <type>)." per watched input, or "Nothing: every input, step, and setting must stay exactly as reviewed."), `suspendsOn` ("A new revision of “<title>” (this trigger runs revision N only)."; "Any change to the code in “…” (…)." per unwatched code or Lean input, "Any change to “…” (…)." per other unwatched input; "The restricted image <name> becoming a different image (approved: <id>)." per restricted image, R and Julia runner images included; "The project folder's trust: native steps run with your account's privileges and need a trusted folder." when native; always "Trust being revoked for the project folder: every trigger of the folder is suspended." and "Any step's runner becoming unavailable (or available), and any step, budget, or limit changing."), `nativeSteps` (labels), `modelRequestsPerRun` (the plan's `maxModelRequests`), `defaults` (quiet period 10 s, 4 runs/hour, 24 runs/day, native not allowed, model requests min(4 × per run, 1000)), `limits`, `notices`, `blockers` (the plan's, except another run being active), `expiresAtMs` (30 min). Refused: no inputs for `inputsChanged` (`trigger.noInputs`), interval outside 15–10080 (`trigger.invalidInterval`), > 16 inputs (`trigger.tooManyInputs`), an input listed twice (`trigger.duplicateInput`), not a resolved external input of the revision, a missing file included (`trigger.unknownInput`), a code-source or Lean-source input ("“<label>” (<path>) is code[ (Lean elaborates it natively, and `#eval` runs code)]. Code never runs without your review: a change to it suspends the trigger instead of starting a run, so it cannot be watched.", `trigger.codeInputWatched`), a pinned input (`trigger.inputPinned`). Reviews are held in memory (≤ 16).
- **Enable** (`workflow_trigger_enable({plan, standingHash, settings})`): an unknown or used review, or another project → `trigger.planStale`; an expired review or a different hash → `trigger.planStale` (the review is used up); refused settings (`debounceSeconds` 2–3600 for `inputsChanged`, `maxRunsPerHour` 1–12, `maxRunsPerDay` 1–100 and ≥ runs per hour, `maxModelRequestsPerDay` ≤ 1000 → `trigger.invalidSettings`; native steps without `allowNative: true` → `trigger.nativeNotAllowed`; a model-request cap below one run's budget → `trigger.modelCapTooLow`) and an unreadable store (`trigger.storeUnavailable`) leave the review usable for another attempt; no project → `workflow.noProject`; a freshly previewed standing hash that differs → `trigger.planStale` ("The standing plan no longer matches: …"); a non-busy blocker → its code. One trigger per (project, workflow): enabling replaces the existing one's kind, settings, and standing plan, keeping its id and history. ≤ 32 triggers per project (the oldest disabled one is forgotten to make room; else `trigger.tooMany`). The watched inputs' current digests become its baseline. If the store cannot be written (`trigger.storeFailed`, "The triggers could not be saved (…); changes since the last save may not survive a restart."), nothing changes and the review stays usable. Disable (`workflow_trigger_disable`, `trigger.unknown` for an unknown id) drops pending work and is undone if it cannot be saved; resuming is a new review + enable.
- **Listening.** Only for the open project and only after it finished opening and indexing (the graph's first synchronization); then the watched inputs' digests are read again as the new baseline, so nothing fires for the open itself and changes made while the project was closed are never caught up. Input: the workspace watcher's `filesChanged` (any program's change on disk, not only editor saves); a changed path matches an input when equal to it or a folder above it (ASCII case-insensitive on Windows); a lost-track rescan matches everything. A watched change sets the fire time to now + quiet period (restarting it on every change); a change of another plan input (code and Lean sources included, or any input of an interval trigger) schedules a check that can only suspend. Closing or switching the project stops listening and drops pending checks and the queued follow-up; the trigger stays `active`.
- **Firing** (fire time): preview the workflow's plan (not kept, cannot be started; a missing project or graph waits); a standing hash that differs → suspended (`trigger.planChanged`, "The standing plan no longer matches: <what changed>. The trigger is suspended until you review and enable it again."); a non-busy blocker or another planning error → suspended with its code; for an `inputsChanged` trigger, watched content equal to the baseline (the content the last triggered run read, or what was found when listening started) → nothing, and a queued follow-up is dropped (an event with `queued: false`; its "queued" history entry stays); a run of the workflow active, or 3 runs active → `queued` (at most one follow-up; it previews again with whatever is saved then, so the latest change wins; polled after 1 s, then 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 60, 60 … s while still blocked, and at once when any run ends); over the runs-per-hour, runs-per-day (rolling windows), or model-requests-per-day limit (each fire counts the run's full model-request budget) → deferred until allowed; otherwise a startable plan is made, checked again against the standing plan and the baseline, and started through the run service's normal start check (the full approval hash is recomputed) with cause `trigger` and each changed watched input's before/after SHA-256; the new digests become the baseline. A start refused as stale is retried after the quiet period and suspends the trigger after 3 consecutive refusals; another start error suspends it. After each scheduled fire of an interval trigger (whatever its outcome, unless it was queued or deferred), the next is one interval later; when listening starts (after enabling, reopening, or a restart), the first is one interval later and not before the last fire + one interval.
- **Suspension** (`suspended {code, message}`): `trigger.planChanged` (a new revision, a changed code, Lean, or other unwatched input, an input gone, folder trust for a plan with native steps, a restricted image name naming a different image, the provider or model of agent review steps (a trigger enabled before plans recorded them is suspended once, at its next check: "… the plan now names the model provider and model its agent review steps send material to (…)"), a runner becoming unavailable or available, `maxParallel` or any step, budget, or limit), `trigger.trustRevoked` ("Trust was revoked for the project folder, so the trigger is suspended until you review and enable it again.", every active trigger of the revoked folder or below it, with or without native steps), `trigger.nativeNotAllowed` ("The plan has native steps, which this trigger was not allowed to start. The trigger is suspended until you review and enable it again."), and pass-through codes. A suspended trigger does nothing until enabled again. Rate limits and queuing never suspend.
- **Records** (`WorkflowTrigger`): `id` (`wtrg-<32 hex>`), `workflow`, `revision`, `title`, `project` (folder name), `kind`, `watched`, the settings, `modelRequestsPerRun`, `standingHash`, `state` (`active` / `suspended` / `disabled`), `createdAtMs`, `enabledAtMs`, `lastFiredMs`, and the live view `queued`, `nextCheckMs`, `listening`, `runsLastHour`, `runsLastDay`, `modelRequestsLastDay`, `storeProblem` (the store's current read or write problem, on every trigger, else null); `history` (≤ 50, oldest dropped: `enabled {revision}`, `fired {run, changed}`, `queued`, `rateLimited {untilMs}`, `startRefused {code, message}`, `suspended {code, message}`, `disabled`).
- **Store:** `<data>/workflow-triggers.json` (`{"schema":"exposition.workflowTriggers/1","triggers":[…]}`, every project's triggers keyed by the canonical project root, lowercased on Windows; each with its standing plan (payloads included, input text removed), baseline, and fire times); written through a temporary file and a rename. Missing → no triggers. Unparsable or a wrong schema → set aside as `workflow-triggers.json.bad-<ms>` and an empty store started. Unreadable (any other read error, or the set-aside failed) → never overwritten: every change is refused with `trigger.storeUnavailable` ("The trigger store (…) cannot be read (…); triggers cannot be changed until it can be, and it is never overwritten."), and the file is read again on the next enable, disable, or list. A record that cannot be decoded is kept as it is, never fires, and is written back unchanged. Changes the engine makes by itself (a suspension, a fire, a queued follow-up, a new baseline) apply even when they cannot be saved and are reported in `storeProblem`. Never in the project, a definition, or a bundle. On restart active triggers load active and not listening, rate-limit windows persist, nothing catches up.
- **UI** (workflow editor, **Triggers** section below the runs, `TriggersPanel.tsx`): "Off by default. A trigger starts runs by itself only under a standing plan you approve here; agents and imported workflows never enable one."; **New trigger…** (**Replace trigger…** when the workflow has one; disabled, with the reason, like Review and run…) opens the dialog of §1: what to watch (code and Lean inputs left out: "Code and Lean sources cannot be watched (…): changed code always needs a new review.") → the standing plan review with "What may change without asking" and "Anything else suspends it until you review it again" → settings, checked against the limits (runs per hour ≤ runs per day included); every review starts with native steps not allowed → **Enable trigger**, or, when the workflow already has a trigger, a warning "This replaces the current trigger" and **Replace trigger**; a stale review shows "The plan changed since you reviewed it" with **Review the new plan**; an unreadable store "Triggers cannot be saved right now". Card: what it watches or its interval, revision, quiet period, limits, "at most one queued follow-up (the latest change wins)", native permission; last fired, runs this hour/today against the limits, model requests today, next check, enabled time; badges "1 run queued", "Not listening yet" ("It listens once its project is open and has finished indexing."), state Active / Suspended / Off; **Turn off**, **Review and turn on…**; a suspended card shows the backend's reason under "Suspended: the approved plan no longer matches" / "Suspended: folder trust was revoked" / "Suspended: native steps are not allowed" / "Suspended", with **Review again…** (pre-filled, native permission off); a store problem shows "The trigger store could not be saved or read"; "Recent history (N)" (newest 20, with **Open run** for fired runs). The section re-reads on `workflow-trigger` events; the suspension notification is §1.

### 7.9 Environment capture, run manifests, and reproduction (M9)

`crates/exposition-workflow/src/{environment,manifest,reproduce}.rs`, `crates/exposition-research/src/sandbox/image_env.rs`, `crates/exposition-lean/src/provenance.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workflow_repro.rs`; commands §10 "Workflow manifests and reproduction".

- **Environment** (`WorkflowStepAttempt.environment`, recorded when the adapter returns, for attempts that were dispatched; none for attempts refused before dispatch, interrupted by an app stop, or from runs before M9): `runner`, `containment`, `os`, `arch`, `appVersion` (the crates' version, 0.1.0), and as applicable `image {name, id}`, `interpreter {name, version}`, `packages [{name, version}]` (≤ 1000 listed), `packagesDigest` (SHA-256 of the sorted, de-duplicated `name==version\n` lines of every captured package), `toolchain {name, version, manifestDigest}`, `model {provider, model, servedBy?}`, `seed`, `notes` (≤ 16 of ≤ 300 characters; absolute paths replaced by `<path>`). Steps of Exposition's own services record only the base fields; a composite's child steps record their own.
  - *Restricted R/Julia batch*: `image {name, id}` and `interpreter` R / Julia without a version; `os`/`arch` `unknown`; no packages; note "The R image's interpreter version and installed packages are not listed (only Python images are probed); its identity names its exact content." No probe runs.
  - *Restricted Python batch*: `os`/`arch` from inside the image, `interpreter` python + version, every installed distribution (`importlib.metadata`; ≤ 5000 captured, noted when truncated), note "Packages: every installed distribution, listed inside the image by identity (no network, no inputs)." The list comes from `<data>/research/image-environments/<64 hex>.json` (`exposition.imageEnvironment/1`; read only when a regular file ≤ 2 MiB naming the same identity; re-validated; written via a temporary file) or, the first time a batch uses that identity, from a probe: a separate container job ("Restricted image environment probe (…)") started from the identity with the restricted settings (`--network=none`, read-only, non-root, limits; an empty working folder; no host environment) running `python3 -c <probe>`, 30 s limit, output validated as untrusted. A failed probe is noted ("The image's package list could not be captured: …") and retried by the next batch; it never fails the batch.
  - *Native batch*: host `os`/`arch`, the discovered interpreter's name and version, and the packages research discovery checks (Python: jupyter_client, ipykernel, pyzmq, numpy, pandas, matplotlib, scipy, sympy, polars; R: `r` and IRkernel; Julia: `julia`), noted "the research environment report (the packages it checks, not every installed package)". No command runs for this.
  - *Lean audit*: `toolchain` = the audit's resolved toolchain (else the `lean-toolchain` pin) with its version, `manifestDigest` = SHA-256 of `lake-manifest.json` (≤ 4 MiB, inside the project; Lake projects), its pinned dependencies as packages (version = revision), notes (e.g. "mathlib revision …"). Files only.
  - *Agent review*: `model` = the provider and the configured model, `servedBy` = the model the provider reported serving (when it did), notes "Configured model …; the provider served …." when they differ, "Effort …".
  - *Seed*: `EXPOSITION_SEED` = the first 8 hex digits of SHA-256(`exposition.workflowSeed/1\n<basis>\n<step>\n<instance>`) as a decimal number (0–4294967295), basis = the run id (for a reproduction, the original's, following up to 32 reproductions), instance = the 0-based instance index (0 for a step that runs once), so instances get different seeds; given to every restricted and native batch (Python, R, Julia) and recorded (also in the research run record).
- **Manifest** (`workflow_run_manifest(run)` → string; `exposition.workflowRunManifest/2`): `schema`, `generator` ("Exposition <version>"), `run`, `cause`, `workflow`, `title`, `project` (folder name), `revision`, `definitionHash`, `approvalHash`, `state`, `code`, `message`, start/finish times, `inputs` (id, label, type, project-relative path, SHA-256, bytes, pinned), `execution` (restricted backend, image, image id, `runnerImages`, native and model steps), `budgets`, `usage`, `steps` (step, label, operation, runner, containment, state, `payloadSha256` (SHA-256 of the exact UTF-8 code or prompt text; null without a payload), parameters, time limit, attempts with times, code, message, environment, outputs, refused outputs, and for a repeated step `instances [{index (0-based), parameter, state, reason, attempts}]`), `outputs` (each step's, and each instance's, last succeeded attempt: port, type, kind, path, SHA-256, bytes, media type, status, provenance, `instance` (null for a step that runs once)), `outcomes` (realized, SHA-256). Left out: logs, report text (digest only), payload text, notices, blockers. Canonical: keys sorted at every level, 2-space indentation, LF, final newline. Scrubbed (every string value): the project root → `<project>`, the app data folder → `<app data>`, the home folder → `~`, other absolute paths → `<path>`, then the execution service's secret redaction.
- **Export** (`workflow_run_manifest_export(run, destination?)`): without `destination` the backend's native save dialog ("Export run manifest", JSON, suggested `<title>-run-manifest.json`; dismissed → `workflow.exportCanceled`, which the UI ignores); with one (IPC callers such as e2e), that path. Either goes through the bundle export's location rules (§7.5, `workflow.file`: absolute, `.json`, an existing folder outside the project's `.exposition` folder and the app data folder, not a link or folder); an existing file must be a regular file of at most 16 MiB whose JSON `schema` is `exposition.workflowRunManifest/1` or `/2` (an earlier export) (`workflow.invalidDestination`: "The existing destination is too large to be a run manifest; refusing to replace it." / "Refusing to replace a file that is not an Exposition run manifest."); a manifest > 16 MiB → `workflow.tooLarge`; atomic write (`workflow.exportFailed`). Returns `{path (as chosen), sha256, bytes}`.
- **Reproduce** (`workflow_run_reproduce_plan(run)` → `WorkflowReproducePlan {plan, reproduces, drift}`): a run of the open project (`workflow.unknownRun`) that is not running (`workflow.runActive`; interrupted, failed, and canceled runs can be reproduced); its revision must still exist (`workflow.unknown`) with the same definition hash (`workflow.reproduceRevisionChanged`). The plan is the normal plan of that revision with cause `reproduce` and every input pinned to the SHA-256 the run read; a differing file blocks it (`workflow.inputChanged`, "<path> changed since run <id> read it (recorded sha256 …, now …); restore that version to reproduce the run."), a missing one `workflow.inputMissing`. `drift` (per step: `aspect` `image` / `platform` / `interpreter` / `toolchain` / `lakeManifest` / `packages` / `model` / `appVersion` / `unrecorded` / `unknown`, recorded, current, message) compares the recorded environment with what is known now without running anything: the configured image's identity (`docker image inspect`) and its cached package list, native discovery's cached report, the agents' provider settings (the configured model is compared, not the served one), the Lean project's files. A repeated step's instances are compared one by one: an item that holds for every instance is reported once, one that holds for some names them ("(instances 1, 3)", numbered from 1); a step or instance that ran without recording an environment gives `unrecorded` saying why ("no environment was recorded [for instance 2] (the application stopped while it ran (interrupted) / it was never dispatched / the run predates environment capture)"). Notices: "This plan reproduces run <id> (revision N): every input is pinned to the SHA-256 that run read, and a changed input blocks the plan. Environment differences are shown so you can judge them; they do not block the run.", one "Environment drift in “<step>”: …." per step or "No environment drift found in what can be checked without running anything (image identity, interpreter and toolchain versions, package lists, model).", and "Batch steps receive the same EXPOSITION_SEED values as in the original run (…)." (instances as "“<step>” #k <seed>"). Started with the normal `workflow_run_start`; the new run's cause is `{reproduce, of}`.

## 8. Project operations (P1)

Delete, duplicate, copy and paste, and restore across project files and folders, research files, workflows and their revisions, workflow and research run records, agent task records, and graph items — including items declared in a source, which are deleted by removing their text from that source as an unsaved edit (§8.6, `src-tauri/src/source_removal.rs` over `crates/exposition-graph/src/source_removal.rs`). One contract (`crates/exposition-core/src/ops.rs`), one orchestrator (`src-tauri/src/project_ops.rs`) over the file engine (`crates/exposition-workspace/src/ops/`) and the data side (`src-tauri/src/project_data.rs` → `crates/exposition-workflow/src/project_data.rs`, which acts through the graph (`crates/exposition-graph/src/project_ops.rs`), the run history, the trigger service, research records (`crates/exposition-research/src/records.rs`), and the agents store). The webview only names targets and shows the plan; filesystem authority, impacts, recovery, and identities are decided in Rust. Commands §10 "Project operations"; event `project-op` (§11). Cut/move, rename, and whole-project cloning do not exist.

### 8.1 Contract and flow
- **Request** `OpRequest {kind, targets (1–1000, else `ops.invalidRequest`), actor}`. Kinds: `delete {permanent}`, `duplicate` (same parent or container, fresh name and identity), `paste {destination: folder {folder?} | project}`. Targets (13, tagged `kind`): `path` (project file or folder), `external` (an absolute path outside the project, only as a paste source), `workflow`, `workflowRevision`, `workflowRun`, `researchRun`, `runOutputs` (the generated output files of a workflow run — those of the research batch runs it produced — or of a research run: a scope separate from the record, §8.4; delete only), `agentTask`, `graphEntity`, `graphRelation`, `graphView`, `recoverable` (a recoverable deletion, by its token). Actor `user` or `agent {task}`; the webview commands refuse an agent actor (`ops.invalidRequest`, "Agent operations come only from agent tasks, never from the interface."); agents reach the same orchestrator only through their tools (§6.3).
- **Plan** (`project_op_plan`; nothing changes): the file side plans `path`/`external` targets, the data side every other target plus the consequences of the paths for project data; the parts are merged into one `OpPlan {plan, request, effects, refusals, impacts, needsDecision, choices, planHash, expiresAtMs}`. A target refused by either side has no effect or impact. `needsDecision` = any impact other than `savedVersionCopied`. `planHash` = SHA-256 over the request, the project root, a fingerprint (each entry's path, kind, length, modification time, read-only flag; folder tree digests; open documents' revisions; retained-artifact marks; the data side's fingerprint, which also names each consumer source that could not be read: the project graph, the record of a running workflow, the research run records or the record of an active research run (a workflow batch), agent tasks or a task's files, triggers), refusals, impacts, choices, and effects with their recovery. A source that could not be read adds a `consumersUnknown` impact for each deleted path (§8.2), so the plan needs the decision, and a plan made while it failed goes stale once the source reads again. Plans live 15 min and are single-use.
- **Apply** (`project_op_apply({plan, planHash, choices})`): an unknown or used plan → `ops.planUnknown` ("This operation is no longer available; select the items again."); a different hash, an expired plan, another open project, or a fresh re-plan whose hash differs → `ops.planStale` ("The selection or its consequences changed; review the operation again."); nothing is applied (so any keystroke in an affected unsaved buffer makes the plan stale). Choices: missing ones take their default, an unknown key or value → `ops.invalidRequest`. Then, in order: the intent is written to the operation journal (if that fails, `ops.failed` "The operation could not be recorded, so nothing was changed: …"); the data side stops or disables what the user chose (`prepare_paths`; a path whose work could not be stopped is skipped); each path target is applied independently (outcomes journaled in batches of 16); the data targets are applied; items declared in a source are removed from it (one unsaved transaction per file, §8.6); the project data follows the ACTUAL file outcomes (`after_paths`); the record completes. `OpResult {plan, outcomes, notices}`: one `OpOutcome {target, status (succeeded / failed / skipped), code, message, created, recovery (osTrash / appManaged / none / sourceEdit), restore}` per target in request order (refused targets `skipped` with their code); partial success is reported as it is. Notices: non-decision impacts (saved version copied) and "The operation's progress could not be recorded (…); if Exposition stops now, check the project afterwards." when a later journal write failed.
- **Restore** (`project_op_restore({restore, onCollision?})`): a data token `<kind>:<id>` (§8.4) is tried first, then a file token `trash-<hex>-<16 hex>` (§8.3); anything else → `ops.restoreUnknown` ("Exposition can no longer restore this item; look for it in the Recycle Bin." — "the Trash" off Windows). A restore never overwrites.
- **Event:** `project-op` (payload `OpResult`) after every apply and restore, from the webview, agents, and the restart reconciliation (whether the project's graph becoming ready or the session's first plan triggered it); views re-read (Files folders, Research listing, Workflows list, run histories, agent tasks, Storage & History).
- **Interruption:** each operation is a `crossService` record ("Project operation: …") in the operation journal (§7.1) with steps `intent`, `paths`, `data`, `graph`, then `restorable` (restore tokens) and `restoreSpent`. A record an earlier session left pending is reconciled once per project and session (when the project's graph is ready, or at the next plan): every path to delete without a recorded outcome becomes `ops.interrupted` ("Exposition stopped during this deletion; <path> is no longer in the project." / "Exposition stopped before <path> was deleted; it is still in the project."), unfinished copies (staging entries of a copy operation) are removed, an interrupted restore whose item had already left the Recycle Bin is completed (published under its original name or a fresh one; "Exposition stopped while restoring it; the restore was completed now."), else put back in the Recycle Bin or kept under its hidden name and reported (`ops.restoreIncomplete`) — never deleted, the data side receives what actually happened, and a notice says "An operation was interrupted when Exposition stopped (…). The project now reflects what was actually done: …". Nothing is replayed.

### 8.2 When an operation asks: impacts, choices, the one decision
Routine operations apply at once. The plan carries an impact only for a concrete consequence in the open project, or when what uses a deleted path could not be checked (`consumersUnknown`); graph membership (`memberOf`), folder containment, a script importing another, citations and references, `derivedFrom` or copy provenance, rejected relations, tombstoned resources, deleted workflows, build/review/literature evidence, evidence nothing surviving uses, and deleting a manuscript itself produce none, and a copy never needs a decision (at most the saved-version note). All impacts of one request form ONE decision with all its choices; a refusal (outside the project, protected, read-only, invalid) is never overridden by it.

| Impact (`ImpactKind`) | From | When | Message (shortened) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `manuscriptDependency` | data | a live manuscript resource, not itself deleted, `imports` or `usesAsset` (observed or curated, not rejected) a deleted file (for a `runOutputs` target: one of the output files it removes; the same holds for the next two rows) | "“main.tex” uses “refs.bib”. After deletion the manuscript shows it missing until you restore or replace it." |
| `workflowInput` | data | the latest revision of a non-deleted workflow binds an input to a deleted file | "“Fit” takes “Data (data.csv)” as an input; it cannot run until you choose another file." |
| `retainedEvidence` | data | computational or formal evidence records a deleted file and a surviving item uses it (its subject, an `evidenceFor` endpoint, an accepted `formalizes` target); deleting a curated graph item that provides recorded evidence (`evidenceFor` with evidence) for a surviving item ("“x” provides recorded evidence for “y”; deleting it removes that support. …"; being the subject of evidence, or deleting an `evidenceFor` link itself, is no consequence); permanently deleting a research run that live evidence came from | "Retained evidence records … The evidence becomes unavailable for reproduction; its recorded verdicts stay as history." |
| `pinnedRevision` | data | permanently deleting a workflow, or purging a revision, that other workflows' composite steps pin | "Other workflows run revision 2 of “Fit”. Those revisions are kept unless you choose to delete them too; then those workflows show the step missing and cannot run." |
| `unsavedBuffer` | file | deleting a file (or folder) with open documents that have unsaved changes | "“a.md” has unsaved changes in the editor." |
| `activeWork` | data | an active workflow run whose plan reads a deleted file, an active research run with it as an input (a workflow batch is named, and stopped, as its workflow run), an active agent task of the open project whose paths overlap it; deleting an active run, task, a workflow with an active run, or the outputs of an active run ("… is active and may still write outputs; it is stopped before its outputs are deleted."); TeX/Typst builds are not checked | "“Fit” is running and uses “data.csv”. Stop it before deleting, or keep the file." |
| `enabledTrigger` | data | an active trigger watches a deleted path or a path under a deleted folder; deleting a workflow with an active trigger | "The trigger of “Fit” watches “data.csv”; deleting it would make the trigger run or stop. Turn the trigger off first." |
| `sourceDependents` | source | deleting an item declared in a source (§8.6) when surviving items use it: its proofs (`proofOf`; removed with it by default, `proofs` choice), a `references` (`\ref`, `\cite`), `dependsOn`, `assumes`, `formalizes`, `evidenceFor`, or `usesAsset` relationship from an active item not selected too, a proof that cannot be located exactly, and — for a Lean declaration — literal mentions of its name in the project's other `.lean` text (§8.6) | "“Theorem thm:main” has a proof in the source (“Proof (paper.tex)”). It is removed with it unless you keep it." / "“Proof (paper.tex)” references it. After removal that reference shows as undefined until you edit it." / "“LeanBasic.basel” is mentioned by name in “Other.uses” (lean/Use.lean). Those uses no longer compile until you edit them." |
| `recoveryLoss` | both | every permanent deletion or purge, except links, disposable caches (a path through `__pycache__`, `.pytest_cache`, `.mypy_cache`, `.ruff_cache`, `.ipynb_checkpoints`, `node_modules`, `.lake`, or a `.pyc` file), and the unreferenced-tombstone records | "plot.png (12.0 KiB) will be deleted permanently: it cannot be restored from the Recycle Bin or by Exposition." / "The run record of “Fit” is deleted permanently: it cannot be restored afterwards." |
| `consumersUnknown` | data | deleting a path while a source of its consumers cannot be read: the project graph (unavailable or not attached yet), the record of a running workflow, the research run records or the record of an active research run, the agent task records or a running task's files, the trigger store; one impact per path and source, no choice (Cancel or proceed); an agent's deletion of such a path is refused (`ops.agentScope`) | "Running research runs that use “data.csv” could not be checked: the research run records could not be read. They may be affected if you delete it." / "Manuscripts, workflows and retained evidence that use “data.csv” could not be checked: the project graph could not be read. …" |
| `savedVersionCopied` | file | a copied source has unsaved edits (no decision; a notice) | "“a.md” has unsaved changes; the saved version on disk was copied, not the text in the editor." |

| Choice key | Label | Options (default first) | Offered |
|---|---|---|---|
| `unsavedBuffers` | Unsaved changes in the editor | `preserve` "Keep the unsaved text open in the editor (it is not saved to the deleted file)", `discard`, `cancel` "Don't delete files that have unsaved changes" | an `unsavedBuffer` impact |
| `activeWork` | Work that is running now | `skip` "Leave it running and keep these items", `stop` "Stop it, then delete (stopping cannot be undone, even if a deletion then fails)" | an `activeWork` impact |
| `triggers` | Enabled triggers | `disable` "Turn the triggers off, then delete", `skip` "Keep the triggers and these items" | an `enabledTrigger` impact |
| `pinnedRevisions` | Revisions other workflows run | `keep`, `purge` "Delete them too (those workflows show the step missing and cannot run)" | permanent deletion or purge of a workflow whose revisions are pinned |
| `proofs` | Proofs of the removed statements | `remove` "Remove the proof too", `keep` "Keep the proof in the source" | a statement removed from its source has a proof that was located exactly (§8.6) |

- **Stop and disable** happen before any file changes. Stopping cannot be undone; triggers turned off for a path whose deletion then does not happen (refused, failed, or skipped), or for a workflow whose deletion fails, are turned back on with their approved standing plan. `stop` cancels each consumer through its own service (workflow run cancel — also for a workflow's batch research run —, research run cancel, agent task stop) and waits ≤ 15 s; a consumer that does not stop keeps its items (`ops.activeWork` "“x” could not be stopped, so this item was kept."); `skip` keeps them (`ops.activeWork` / `ops.triggerEnabled`, "… It was left running, so this item was kept." / "… The trigger was kept, so this item was kept."); `disable` turns the whole trigger off. An agent can neither stop work nor turn triggers off (`ops.agentScope`).
- **Agents:** in an Ask `autoApply` task a plan that needs a decision is refused before anything changes (`agents.needsDecision`, with the impacts); in an Ask `reviewEdits` task and in every Auto task the one approval card (never "for this task") shows effects, refusals, consequences, and the default of each choice (§6.4). Agents apply with the defaults only. A path whose consumers could not be checked (`consumersUnknown`) is refused for an agent even after the approval (`ops.agentScope` "What uses this item could not be checked; an agent cannot delete it. Ask the user."), like a path that running work uses.

### 8.3 Files and folders
- **Targets:** `path` targets resolve inside the project (parent folders may be links that stay inside; the final component is never followed) under their real on-disk name, so case variants are one entry; a target listed twice counts once, and a target inside a selected folder is covered by it (its outcome: "Included in <folder>."). No open project → `ops.noProject`.
- **Protected (`ops.protected`; only the top-level `.exposition/` is special, compared case-insensitively):** `.exposition` itself, the graph's storage (`graph.sqlite3*`, `graph-backups`, `graph.damaged*`), run records (`runs/`), the research folder and each research space with its `sources`/`data`/`notes` folders (their contents are ordinary files), other application metadata, and generated artifacts (`artifacts/`), except that a single artifact FILE the project graph keeps as a resource (retained or attached) can be deleted (the run's folder stays protected). `.exposition/project.json` is an ordinary file. Messages name the owner, e.g. "… is a generated research artifact kept by the research service; delete it from Storage & History or the run that produced it." Copy sources may be research groups and artifacts; nothing is pasted into the graph's storage, artifacts, or metadata.
- **Refusals:** `ops.outsideProject` ("… resolves outside the project."), `ops.notFound`, `ops.readOnly` (delete only: a read-only file, or a folder containing read-only files, "… they are not deleted. Clear the read-only setting first."; links are exempt), `ops.intoItself` (a folder into itself or a descendant, junction aliases included; an external folder containing the project), `ops.linkNotCopied` (a selected link or junction is never copied: "copying it would copy whatever it points to"), `ops.notSupported` (an external target to delete or duplicate, a destination that is not a folder, `paste {project}` for files, a name Exposition cannot handle, a too-broad external folder), `ops.agentScope` (an agent naming an external source), `ops.nameUnavailable` (no free name in 10,000 tries).
- **Delete → Recycle Bin.** Ordinary Delete moves each target with the `trash` crate (5.3) on a fresh thread per call (the crate's COM apartment). Effect "Move <path> (<size>) to the Recycle Bin" ("the Trash" off Windows), recovery `osTrash`. A failure fails that target only, and nothing falls back to permanent removal: `ops.inUse` (open in another program; also Windows errors 32/33: "… is open in another program (…), so it was not moved to the Recycle Bin. Close it there and try again; nothing was deleted."), `ops.permissionDenied` ("… access was denied (…). Nothing was deleted."), `ops.trashUnavailable` (the bin cannot take it: too big, path too long, no bin on that drive: "The Recycle Bin cannot take … Nothing was deleted permanently; use Delete Permanently to remove it without the Recycle Bin." — the only failure for which the UI offers **Delete Permanently…**, a new plan with its own decision), `ops.failed`. When the bin reported an error but the entry is gone, the outcome is succeeded with the code and "…; look for it in the Recycle Bin." HRESULT classification is Windows-only; elsewhere every bin error is `ops.failed`.
- **Delete Permanently:** effect "Delete <path> permanently", recovery `none`, always with a `recoveryLoss` impact except for links and disposable caches; recursive; links are removed as links (a junction's target is never touched); leftovers → `ops.partial` ("… was only partly deleted: N items could not be removed.").
- **Undo / restore of a trashed item:** at delete time the bin is listed before and after the move; a token is issued only when exactly one new item has the deleted path as its original location and a deletion time within the operation (±2 s) — otherwise the result says "Exposition cannot identify it there reliably; restore it from the Recycle Bin if needed." and no Undo is offered. Restore re-identifies the item (same id and deletion time; gone → `ops.restoreUnavailable` "… is no longer in the Recycle Bin (it may have been restored or emptied)."), recreates a missing parent folder inside the project (a protected parent → `ops.protected`; a retained research output goes back into its run's artifact folder, files only), restores into a staging name, and publishes it under the original name only if that is free, else (`onCollision` `rename`, the default) as `name (2).ext` …, "… exists, so the restored item is …; both are kept."; `cancel` → `skipped` `ops.restoreCollision` ("… exists again, so nothing was restored; the item is still in the Recycle Bin."). Success code `ops.restored`; open documents of a file restored at its original path resume. An item taken out of the bin that cannot be put in place (no free name, an I/O error) goes back to the bin ("… was taken out of the Recycle Bin but …; it was put back in the Recycle Bin."), or, if that fails too, stays under its hidden staging name next to where it was and is reported (`ops.restoreIncomplete`); it is never deleted. Other codes: `ops.restoreFailed`, `ops.invalidRequest` (an unknown collision choice), `ops.noProject` (a token of a project that is not open). Tokens are kept in memory (≤ 2000 per session) and in the operation journal (≤ 500 live restore records per project; items no longer in the bin are dropped when the project's records are reconciled; the journal itself keeps ≤ 200 records per project, ≤ 1 MiB each, so an older token may expire sooner); a token is spent when its restore succeeds, the item is gone or back in the bin under a new identity, or the restore is refused for a reason that will not change (`ops.protected`, `ops.outsideProject`). The restored item is reindexed: the same path with the same content keeps its graph identity; an unrelated new file at the path gets a new one.
- **Copies (duplicate, paste):** saved bytes on disk only (an open buffer is never saved or copied; `savedVersionCopied` notice). Names are readable and collision-free: paste keeps the name when free, then `report (2).pdf`, `report (3).pdf`; duplicate starts at `(2)`; folders `data (2)`; `.gitignore (2)`; `archive.tar (2).gz`; names planned in the same request are avoided. Each copy is written to a hidden staging entry (`.exposition-save-op…tmp`, hidden from the tree and watcher) and published by a no-replace move (`fsutil::move_no_replace`: on Windows `MoveFileExW` without `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING`; elsewhere a hard link and unlink for a file, a checked rename for a folder), so an existing name is never replaced and a folder is never merged; concurrent pastes take distinct names; the outcome's `created` is the actual name. Links and junctions inside a copied folder are skipped, never followed (outcome succeeded with `ops.linkNotCopied`: "Copied to … without N links (links are not copied): …"); hidden files and tool folders inside are copied; read-only files stay read-only. Duplicate uses the same parent; paste into a folder (for a selected file, the UI passes its parent and says so). Copies get fresh graph identities with `copy.from` (source path) and `copy.fromEntity` (the original resource) attributes; no evidence, verification, relations, or workflow wiring carry over. No size limit; the plan's walk of a folder reads ≤ 200,000 entries.
- **External sources** (paste only, user only): an absolute path whose final component is not a link (`ops.linkNotCopied`); a path inside the project counts as a project source; never Exposition's data folder (`ops.protected`) or a too-broad folder (drive root, system or personal folder); the source is only read. External pastes get no copied-from provenance.
- **Unsaved buffers** (delete): with `preserve` every dirty document under the path is suspended before the deletion — its recovery journal is removed and no longer written, and a save is refused with `document.fileDeleted` ("… was deleted, and its unsaved text is kept in the editor only. Saving would bring the deleted file back, so it was not saved; copy the text elsewhere to keep it."); **Save a Copy…** (`document_save_copy`) is how the text reaches disk. `discard` closes those documents (journal removed); `cancel` skips those targets (`ops.cancelled`). Clean documents of deleted files close; a failed deletion resumes journaling.
- **Save a Copy** (`document_save_copy(document, destination?)` → `SavedCopy {location, path?, bytes}`): the document's current text (unsaved edits included, in its encoding) to a NEW file; the document is unchanged. Without a destination the backend's save dialog ("Save a copy", suggested `<stem> (2).<ext>` beside the document; dismissed → `document.saveCanceled`, quiet). A project path follows the protected-area rules (`document.protected`), never an open document's path; an absolute path outside the project may be used, never the app data folder (`document.protected`); an existing file is never replaced (`document.exists` "… already exists; a copy never replaces anything."); `file.tooLarge` (> 10 MiB), `file.binary`, `file.writeFailed`, `path.notDirectory`, `path.invalid`.
- **Clipboard** (Windows): Copy puts the selection on the OS clipboard as a file list (`CF_HDROP` + "Preferred DropEffect" copy) through `clipboard_files_write` (paths re-checked by planning a paste: protected, link, outside, missing → refused), so it pastes in Explorer; `clipboard_files_read` returns the clipboard's file list (empty when it holds none; ≤ 10,000 entries). On Paste the OS list wins: absolute paths inside the project become `path` targets, others `external`; the app's own record is used only when the OS clipboard cannot be read or the copy never reached it. Clipboard text is never interpreted as a command or path. Other platforms: both commands fail `ops.clipboardUnavailable` ("Copying files to and from the system clipboard is supported on Windows only in this version."), and copies paste within Exposition only.
- **Graph after file operations:** succeeded deletions are reindexed (tombstones; surviving consumers show the file missing); after a permanent deletion the unreferenced tombstones under those paths are removed; copies become fresh resources; restores are reconciled. Failed outcomes change nothing.

### 8.4 App records and Storage & History
- **Recoverable deletion** (ordinary Delete; recovery `appManaged`, effect "… (restorable in Settings → Storage & History)"; restore token = `<kind>:<id>`): a workflow is retired (row in `retired_workflows`, its entity and relations kept as a snapshot in `recoverable`; its revisions stay so pins still resolve; it disappears from lists and editing, `workflow.unknown`); a workflow run gets `deleted_at` in the run history; a research run's manifest moves to `.exposition/runs/deleted/<id>.json` with its deletion time in `<id>.deleted-at` (transcript and outputs stay; moves never replace a file); an agent task gets `deleted_at`; a curated graph entity, relation, or saved view is snapshotted in `recoverable` and removed. Deleting again → `ops.alreadyDeleted`. Deleting a run record (recoverably or permanently) never deletes its outputs ("…; its outputs stay").
- **Run outputs** (`runOutputs {run}`, Storage & History "Generated outputs", or together with the record in one request — one plan, one decision; outputs are applied first): the files directly in `.exposition/artifacts/<research run>/` of the run (a workflow run: of the research batch runs it produced), except `transcript.json` and every file the project graph keeps as a resource (retained or attached to a manuscript: "…; k output(s) the project keeps stay (delete those files themselves to remove them)"); source inputs and manuscripts are never touched. Impacts are those of the removable files (manuscript dependency, workflow input, retained evidence), plus `activeWork` (with the `activeWork` choice; a workflow batch's research run is named and stopped as its workflow run) when the run is active and `recoveryLoss` when permanent. Moving and restoring outputs never replaces a file (a no-replace move; a taken name gets `name (n).ext`, up to 64 tries). Ordinary Delete moves the files aside to `.exposition/artifacts/deleted/<research run>/` (deletion time in its `.deleted-at`; "Moved N output file(s) (X) aside; they stay on disk until purged in Settings → Storage & History."; their bytes stay used until purged), restore token `runOutputs:<run>[,<run>…]`; restoring puts them back in the run's folder, a name taken meanwhile getting a fresh `name (n).ext` (`onCollision: cancel` → `research.restoreConflict`, "An output with the same name exists again; nothing was restored."; none held → `research.unknownOutputs`). Delete Permanently removes them, also outputs of that run held from an earlier deletion, and reports the bytes ("Removed N output file(s); X given back to the file system."). Nothing removable → "Nothing to delete among the outputs of …", recovery none. Copying outputs as a scope is refused (`ops.notSupported`, "Outputs are copied as files: copy the output files themselves."). A research run that is still active refuses deletion of its records or outputs (`research.runActive`, "The run is still active; cancel it first.").
- **Restore** (Storage & History **Restore**, or Undo): a workflow comes back with its triggers off ("Restored. Its triggers stay off until you turn them on again."; a workflow whose revisions were purged cannot be restored, `ops.notFound`); a run or task comes back as it was (nothing restarts); a graph entity or relation is refused when the same identity or relationship exists again or an endpoint is gone (`ops.restoreConflict`; skipped relations are noted; a relationship whose other endpoint is itself a deleted item that can still be restored is not skipped: it is handed to that item's record (while it has room) and returns when that item is restored, in either order; a relationship the vocabulary no longer admits between its endpoints is skipped); a view whose id is taken comes back under a new id; a research record whose manifest exists again is refused. Nothing is overwritten.
- **Permanent deletion / purge** (recovery `none`, `recoveryLoss`): workflows (every revision other workflows do not pin, or all with `pinnedRevisions: purge`; triggers removed; "Deleted. Revision(s) 2 are kept because other workflows run them."); a single revision (only permanently; the current revision of a live workflow → `ops.latestRevision`); workflow run records (the row); research run records (manifest and transcript removed; a run of this app session leaves the history at once and output it still sends is dropped); held outputs (`runOutputs:<run>` in Recently deleted: "Purge N deleted output file(s) (X)", "Purged; X given back to the file system."); agent tasks (every table row of the task; a task with changes awaiting review says they can no longer be reverted); graph records; `tombstones:unreferenced` (records of deleted files and declarations nothing refers to). A finished run or task is never duplicated (`ops.notSupported`: "A finished run or task is history: copy its details or start a new one (it is never duplicated as a new result).").
- **Disk space, honestly:** after a permanent deletion the run history (`workflow-runs.sqlite3`) and the task database (`agents.sqlite3`) are compacted (`VACUUM`, WAL checkpoint) and the result says "The history database gave back X of disk space.", "The record was removed; the database did not shrink now (its free space is reused by later records).", or "The record was removed."; the project graph is not compacted ("Purged. The project graph file does not shrink; the freed space is reused by later changes.").
- **Storage & History** (`storage_overview`; Settings section "Storage & History", palette "Preferences: Storage & History"): "Retained records, generated outputs, workflow revisions, and recoverable deletions of this project. Nothing is removed automatically; referenced or shared items are kept unless you include them."

| Category (`id`) | Lists | Size | Referenced / active | Note (shortened) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Workflow run records (`workflowRuns`) | runs of the project ("<title> — <State>, <date>") | record size | — / running | "Deleting removes the run record only (restorable); its outputs stay (select them under Generated outputs to delete them too)." |
| Research run records (`researchRuns`) | research runs | manifest + transcript | retained evidence came from it / running | "… its outputs stay (they are listed under Generated outputs). Records retained evidence came from are marked." |
