### Project graph (13)
Every change from these commands is validated in Rust and attributed to the user (`Origin::User`, set by the backend, never by the payload). No SQL, no service-owned records (evidence, observed relations), and no execution are reachable. Saved views are presentation only.

| Command | Args | Returns | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| graph_status | — | GraphStatus | `state` (`detached` / `attached` / `temporaryReadOnly{reason}` / `unavailable{reason, recovery[]}`), project id, root, schema version, revision, entity/relation counts, freshness, coverage |
| graph_vocabulary | — | Vocabulary | The closed entity/relation vocabulary with endpoint rules (display only) |
| graph_snapshot | query: GraphQuery{kinds?, text? (≤200 chars, case-insensitive on label or key), around?, includeInactive, limit? (default 300, ≤2000)} | GraphSnapshot | Bounded slice of the graph (`truncated` when cut; `relationsTruncated` when more than 20,000 relations exist among the returned entities) |
| graph_entity | entity: EntityId | EntityDetail | One entity with its bindings, ≤ 20,000 relations in each direction (`relationsTruncated` when it has more), ≤ 500 related items (`neighborsTruncated`), `evidenceTruncated` when more own or cited evidence exists than is listed, `evidence` (≤ 200 records recorded about it) and `citedEvidence` (≤ 200 records its non-rejected `evidenceFor` relations cite, by the citation rule of §7.1; never merged into `evidence`), each with freshness |
| graph_inspect_at_source | request: InspectAtSourceRequest{path, offset? (UTF-16; whole file when absent), revision?} | SourceInspection | Entities bound at a source position (≤ 200, then `entitiesTruncated`; ≤ 200 relations per entity, then that entity's `relationsTruncated`); unsaved overlays labelled |
| graph_impact | request: ImpactRequest{entities, maxDepth? (default 4, ≤8), maxItems? (default 200, ≤1000)} | ImpactReport | What a change to the entities may affect, with the path that explains each item |
| graph_math_overview | — | MathOverview | Mathematics view: statements, dependencies, proof targets, formal evidence; ≤ 20,000 relations among the rows are returned (`relationsTruncated` when more exist), and each row's proof and formal-association state is computed from all of them |
| graph_propose_change | request: ChangeRequest{baseRevision, operations, summary? (≤500)} | ChangeProposal | Validate a typed change as the user; nothing is applied |
| graph_apply_change | request: ApplyChangeRequest{proposal, expectedRevision} | ApplyOutcome | Apply a stored proposal, re-validated, all or nothing: `applied{revision, rebased, created, delta}` / `conflict{currentRevision, diagnostics}` / `invalid{…}` |
| graph_views | — | GraphViewSummary[] | Saved views |
| graph_view_get | view: ViewId | GraphView | One saved view |
| graph_view_save | view: GraphViewInput{id?, name, scope, nodes, viewport, filters, hidden, collapsed, expectedRevision?} | GraphView | Create (no `id`; the id is minted in Rust), or replace (`expectedRevision` must match, else `graph.staleView`; an `id` the graph does not hold is `graph.unknownView`); never changes relations |
| graph_recover | option: RecoveryOption | GraphStatus | Recover an unavailable graph with the user's choice: `restoreBackup{backup, takenAtMs, revision}` / `startNew` / `retry` (never automatic) |
### Project creation (8)
| Command | Args | Returns | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| project_presets | — | PresetCatalog | Built-in presets (answered at once; tool availability separately) |
| project_capabilities | — | CapabilityReport | What this computer has for the capabilities presets declare (existing toolchain and research-environment discovery; installs nothing, runs no project code; may wait for first-time discovery) |
| project_preset_blueprint | request: ProjectPresetRequest{preset, title?, format?, runtime?, leanToolchain?, includeWorkflow, purpose?} | ProjectBlueprint | A preset's blueprint with the chosen options (fresh ids each time) |
| project_blueprint_validate | blueprint: String (JSON) | BlueprintValidation | Strict validation of a blueprint (presets, agent proposals, imports alike) |
| project_check_destination | destination: String | DestinationCheck | Check a destination without changing anything (`ok`, `message`, `createsFolder`, `trustedVia`) |
| project_create | request: CreateProjectRequest{destination, blueprint (JSON), acceptTrustedLocation} | ProjectCreated | Create from a blueprint in a new or empty folder, open it, apply its graph content; an interruption leaves a recoverable record |
| project_creation_status | — | CreationStatus[] | Unfinished creations of the open project (interrupted ones need reconciliation; never re-run) |
| project_creation_resolve | operation: OperationId, action: `finish` \| `keep` | CreationResolution | Resolve an interrupted creation with the user's explicit choice |
### Workflow drafts and bundles (10)
Nothing here runs a workflow. `validation.runnable` (and `WorkflowSummary.runnable` in `workflow_list`) is computed for the open project on every call: well formed AND every step's runner available now (§7.5); a stored claim is never used.

| Command | Args | Returns | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| workflow_operations | — | OperationContract[] | The registered operation contracts (the only operations a step can name), each runner's `available`/`availability` filled in for the open project |
| workflow_presets | — | WorkflowPresetInfo[] | Workflow presets |
| workflow_from_preset | request: WorkflowPresetRequest{preset, script?, data?, manuscript?, leanFile?} | WorkflowDefinition | A new, unsaved draft from a preset |
| workflow_validate | definition: WorkflowDefinition, id: WorkflowId? | WorkflowValidation | Validate against the strict contract (files checked in the open project; composites resolved against the saved revisions; `id` = the workflow being edited, so a composite that runs it is `workflow.compositeRecursive`) |
| workflow_save | request: SaveWorkflowRequest{id?, expectedRevision?, definition} | SavedWorkflow | Save as a new immutable revision (admitted as a `Workflow` entity, attributed to the user); incomplete drafts saved with their diagnostics |
| workflow_import | text: String (JSON) | SavedWorkflow | Import a definition as a new draft (strict schema, size limits, fresh id) |
| workflow_list | — | WorkflowSummary[] | Saved workflow drafts of the open project, each with its state as it validates now (§7.5) |
| workflow_get | id: WorkflowId, revision: u64? | SavedWorkflow | One draft (latest, or the given revision), validated now; for a deleted workflow only a given revision that a composite still pins (read-only, §7.5), else `workflow.unknown` |
| workflow_bundle_export | id: WorkflowId, revision: u64?, path: String? | WorkflowBundleExport{path, bytes, children} | Export the revision and every workflow its composites run as `exposition.workflow-bundle/1`; `path` absent: the backend's save dialog (`workflow.exportCanceled` when dismissed); destination rules §7.5 |
| workflow_bundle_import | path: String | WorkflowBundleImport{root, children[]} | Import a bundle or single definition file as untrusted drafts (children first, references rewritten); nothing trusted, approved, triggered, or run (§7.5) |
### Workflow runs (7)
Only these commands run a workflow, and only `workflow_run_start` with a reviewed plan's id and approval hash starts one (§7.7). Runs belong to the open project; another project's run reads as `workflow.unknownRun`.

| Command | Args | Returns | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| workflow_run_plan | id: WorkflowId, revision: u64? | WorkflowRunPlan | Reviewable plan of a saved revision (latest when absent): inputs with SHA-256, steps (composites expanded) with full payloads, runners, containment, effects, limits, conditions, repeats, budgets, `maxParallel`, `limits`, execution, notices, blockers, `approvalHash`; kept 30 min, single use |
| workflow_run_start | request: StartWorkflowRunRequest{plan, approvalHash} | WorkflowRun | Start the approved plan after recomputing it; `workflow.planStale` if anything approved changed, else the first blocker's code, else `workflow.activeRun`; the run's cause is the plan's (manual, or reproduce for a reproduction plan) |
| workflow_run_cancel | run: WorkflowRunId | WorkflowRun | Cancel every running step and skip the rest; waits ≤ 5 s for the run to stop |
| workflow_run_get | run: WorkflowRunId | WorkflowRun | One run: plan as approved, steps, attempts, validated outputs, outcomes, usage (promotion proposals reflect their review) |
| workflow_runs | workflow: WorkflowId? | WorkflowRunSummary[] | Runs of the open project (one workflow, or all), newest first, ≤ 100 |
| workflow_run_acknowledge | run: WorkflowRunId | WorkflowRun | Mark an interrupted run reviewed (`workflow.notInterrupted` otherwise); nothing resumes |
| workflow_association_decide | decision: WorkflowAssociationDecision{run, step, accept[] ≤ 200} | WorkflowRun | Record Lean evidence for the accepted audited declarations of a run's association proposal and reject the rest |
### Workflow manifests and reproduction (3)
Runs of the open project only (`workflow.noProject`, `workflow.unknownRun`); nothing runs (§7.9).

| Command | Args | Returns | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| workflow_run_manifest | run: WorkflowRunId | String | The run's manifest as canonical, scrubbed JSON (`exposition.workflowRunManifest/2`) |
| workflow_run_manifest_export | run: WorkflowRunId, destination: String? | WorkflowManifestExport{path, sha256, bytes} | Write the manifest; `destination` absent: the backend's save dialog (`workflow.exportCanceled` when dismissed); an existing file is replaced only when it is an earlier run manifest |
| workflow_run_reproduce_plan | run: WorkflowRunId | WorkflowReproducePlan{plan, reproduces, drift[]} | A plan of the same revision with every input pinned to the recorded SHA-256 and cause `reproduce`, plus environment drift; start `plan` with `workflow_run_start` |
### Workflow triggers (4)
User-only: no agent tool reaches these, and they are the only way a trigger is created or changed (§7.8).

| Command | Args | Returns | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| workflow_trigger_plan | request: WorkflowTriggerPlanRequest{workflow, kind} | WorkflowTriggerPlan | The standing plan of the latest revision for review: the full run plan, `standingHash`, what may change, what suspends it, native steps, model requests per run, defaults, limits, notices, blockers; held 30 min, single use |
| workflow_trigger_enable | request: EnableWorkflowTriggerRequest{plan, standingHash, settings} | WorkflowTrigger | Enable (or replace) the workflow's trigger from a reviewed standing plan; `trigger.planStale` if it changed |
| workflow_trigger_disable | trigger: WorkflowTriggerId | WorkflowTrigger | Turn a trigger off (`trigger.unknown` for an unknown id) |
| workflow_trigger_list | workflow: WorkflowId? | WorkflowTrigger[] | Triggers of the open project (one workflow, or all); `workflow.noProject` without one |
### Project operations and file clipboard (6)
Rust decides authority, impacts, recovery, and identities (§8). The webview sends only the user's actor; agents reach the orchestrator through their tools.

| Command | Args | Returns | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| project_op_plan | request: OpRequest{kind, targets (1–1000), actor} | OpPlan | Normalize the targets, refuse invalid ones, list effects, recovery, concrete impacts, and choices; `needsDecision`; `planHash`; nothing changes; kept 15 min, single use |
| project_op_apply | apply: OpApply{plan, planHash, choices} | OpResult | Re-plan and compare (`ops.planStale` / `ops.planUnknown`, nothing applied), then apply each target and report every outcome; emits `project-op` |
| project_op_restore | restore: OpRestore{restore, onCollision?: `rename` \| `cancel`} | OpOutcome | Restore by a data token `<kind>:<id>` or a file token `trash-…`; never overwrites; emits `project-op` |
| clipboard_files_read | — | String[] | Absolute paths of the OS clipboard's file list (`CF_HDROP`; empty when none); Windows only, else `ops.clipboardUnavailable` |
| clipboard_files_write | paths: WorkspacePath[] | () | Put project files on the OS clipboard as a file list (each path re-checked: protected, links, outside, missing refused); Windows only |
| storage_overview | — | StorageOverview{categories, excluded} | Settings › Storage & History: 7 categories with items, sizes where measurable, referenced/active flags, notes, and what is not included (§8.4) |
### Debug-only (16; compiled, listed, and granted only in debug builds, by the runtime capabilities `dev-execution-probe`, `dev-agents-scripted`, `dev-research-faults`, `dev-files-faults`, `dev-tex-probes`, `dev-lean-probes`, `dev-graph-probes`)
No interface calls these: five support tests (fixed jobs, the scripted provider, fault injection) and eleven are probes of live services that the end-to-end scenarios drive. A release build has none of them, so it has no command for "Reindex project", a manual graph backup, the backup list, or the operation list (§16).

| Command | Args | Returns | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| dev_start_probe_job (dev) | — | JobId | Fixed Python process tree for cancellation evidence |
| dev_start_workspace_job (dev) | root: String, holdSeconds: u32? (≤600) | JobId | Register root; trust-gated job writes `exposition-dev-ran.txt` |
| agents_dev_load_script (dev) | script: JSON (≤1 MiB) | () | Queue a script for the scripted provider |
| research_dev_fail_next_graph_write (dev) | — | () | Make the next promotion's graph write fail (pending-reconciliation e2e) |
| files_dev_fail_next_graph_record (dev) | — | () | Make the next rename's graph record fail (pending-and-retry e2e, §3.7) |
| tex_build (dev) | target: BuildTarget?, mode: TexBuildMode | TexTargetRef | Queue build (default project target); coalesces; newer cancels running |
| tex_status (dev) | target: BuildTarget? | TexTargetStatus \| null | Running, pending, last, lastGood, stale of a target |
| tex_page_image (dev) | build: BuildId (`tex-…`), page: u32 (<10000), scale: f32 | PNG bytes | Page raster, capped at 32 Mi pixels; open-folder builds only |
| tex_page_map (dev) | build: BuildId, page: u32 | TexPageMapping | Glyph-level mapping of one page |
| lean_check (dev) | request: LeanCheckRequest | LeanCheckReport | Check exact text, audited verdict; ≤32 goal positions; timeout ≤600 s (default 120 s) |
| lean_package_inspect (dev) | folder: String (≤ 400 chars, no control characters; "" = project folder) | LeanPackageInspection | What a folder of the open project holds as a Lake package (lakefile and its digest, `requirementsInCode`, pin, manifest digest, dependencies with their addresses, Mathlib revision and toolchain, cache present, readiness); reads files only |
| graph_reconcile (dev) | force: bool? (default true) | ReconcileReport | "Reindex project": rebuild derived projections (resources, extracted declarations, observed links, coverage) from the files; curated records never touched; waits ≤ 5 min, then continues reporting through `graph:event` |
| graph_backup (dev) | — | BackupInfo | Consistent backup now (`VACUUM INTO`, reason `manual`) |
| graph_backups (dev) | — | BackupInfo[] | Backups newest first (`name`, `takenAtMs`, `revision`, `reason` `premigration`/`auto`/`manual`, `sizeBytes`) |
| graph_operations (dev) | — | OperationRecord[] | Unresolved cross-service operations of the open project (interrupted promotions, project creation) |
| research_space (dev) | — | ResearchSpaceInfo \| null | The project's research space, if Research is enabled (no side effects) |

Typesetting rules: in-process targets by extension only (`.typ` Typst, `.md`/`.markdown` Markdown print profile; anything else `typesetting.unsupported`). Builds, pages, maps and exports are served only for the folder open now. Export destination: an absolute `.pdf` path in an existing folder; an existing file there must be a regular file starting `%PDF-`, not a build input, not an open document (`typesetting.export`).

## 11. Events (core → webview)

24 channels; forwarders read broadcast channels and emit `*:resync` on lag (`research:setup` emits `research:setup-resync`, `lean:setup` emits `lean:setup-resync`; workspace instead re-emits `filesChanged{changes:[], rescan:true}`; trust emits `null`; `workflow-trigger` skips what it missed, and the next event makes the views re-read; `workflow-run` then emits one event per listed run of the open project with its current state (no step), so a run view whose final event was among the missed ones re-reads too; `project-op` is emitted directly by the command, not from a broadcast channel).

| Channel | Payload variants | When |
|---|---|---|
| `documents:event` | `DocumentEvent`: `changed` (`DocumentChanged{document, transaction?, origin, change: edit\|undo\|redo, previousRevision, revision, patches, selection?, canUndo, canRedo}`), `opened{info}`, `closed{document}`, `infoChanged{info}` | Every document-service change, in revision order |
| `documents:resync` | `()` | Lag; editor re-reads attached documents |
| `workspace:event` | `WorkspaceEvent`: `opened{info}`, `closed`, `filesChanged{changes: {path, kind: created\|removed\|modified}[], rescan}`, `documentReloaded{document, path}`, `conflict{conflict: {document, path, kind: modified\|deleted\|unreadable, message}}`, `conflictResolved{document}`, `recoveryChanged{entries}`, `journalFailed{path, message}`, `projectSettingsInvalid{message}` | Folder open/close, watcher batches, auto-reload, disk conflicts, recovery changes, journal failure, invalid `project.json` set aside |
| `jobs:event` | `JobEvent`: `queued{id, label}`, `started{id, pid?}`, `output{id, stream: stdout\|stderr, text}`, `cancelRequested{id}`, `finished{result}` | Every job lifecycle step, in order (`finished` last, also for refusals). On this channel an `output` event is a notification with an empty `text`: the webview reads output by sequence number with `job_output`, and re-reads the job list only on the other events |
| `jobs:resync` | `()` | Lag; re-read the job list and pull the selected job's output |
| `trust:changed` | `TrustChanged{root, granted: bool}` or `null` | Any trust grant/revoke; before emitting, watched LaTeX entries rebuild if the change covers the open folder |
| `lean:event` | `LeanEvent`: `document{state: LeanDocumentState}`, `removed{document}` | Lean document status/diagnostics change; document no longer tracked |
| `lean:resync` | `()` | Lag |
| `lean:setup` | `LeanSetupEvent`: `changed{status: LeanSetupStatus}`, `output{setupId, step, text}` | Every change of a Mathlib setup (phase, step, job, end) and its step output (bounded, redacted, ≤ 16 KiB per event), in order; broadcast capacity 1024 |
| `lean:setup-resync` | `()` | Lag; re-read `lean_setup_status` |
| `typesetting:build` | `BuildEvent{result, timings, lastGood?, refusal?}` | Finished build of a watched Typst/Markdown entry; finished non-canceled TeX build |
| `typesetting:status` | `BuildStatusEvent{entry, activity: queued\|building\|idle}` | Entry queued, build started, build ended (TeX: `queued` if more pending) |
| `agents:event` | `AgentEvent`: `taskChanged{task}`, `transcriptChanged{taskId}`, `streamStarted{taskId, turn}`, `textDelta{taskId, turn, text}`, `thinkingDelta{taskId, turn, text}`, `streamReset{taskId, turn, reason}`, `toolCallStarted{taskId, toolUseId, name}`, `toolCallFinished{taskId, toolUseId, isError}`, `approvalRequested{approval}`, `approvalResolved{taskId, approvalId, approved}`, `questionRequested{request}`, `questionChanged{taskId, requestId}`, `questionResolved{taskId, requestId}`, `fallback{taskId, from, to}`, `reviewChanged{taskId}`, `providerChanged{status}`, `permissionsChanged{change: PermissionsChange}` | Task status/usage, transcript, streaming, tool calls, approvals, structured questions (§6.7.1), model fallback, review, provider settings or key changed (never the key), a project's permissions set, changed, or revoked |
| `agents:resync` | `()` | Lag |
| `research:event` | `ResearchEvent`: `sessionChanged{session}`, `sessionRemoved{sessionId}`, `runQueued{run}`, `runStarted{runId, sessionId, executionCount?}`, `output{runId, entry}`, `runFinished{run}` | Session and run lifecycle, bounded run output |
| `research:resync` | `()` | Lag; also after workspace `opened`/`closed` |
| `research:setup` | `SetupEvent`: `changed{status: SetupStatus}`, `output{setupId, step, text}` | Every change of a setup run (phase, step, end) and its step output (bounded, redacted, ≤ 16 KiB per event), in order; broadcast capacity 1024 |
| `research:setup-resync` | `()` | Lag; re-read the setup status |
| `graph:event` | `GraphEvent`: `status{status: GraphStatus}`, `changed{delta: GraphDelta, origin}`, `reconciled{revision, freshness}` | Graph attached/detached or its sync state changed; every applied change, non-empty service batch, new resource admission, workflow save, and reconciliation pass (origin `extracted{reconcile/1}`); synchronized again; unsaved overlay set or cleared |
| `graph:resync` | `()` | Lag (broadcast capacity 1024); re-read graph state |
| `workflow-run` | `WorkflowRunEvent{run, workflow, state, step?, stepState?}` (a struct, no variants) | Every persisted change of a run or one of its steps (start, intent, dispatch, outcome, end, acknowledgement, association decision; not the restart reconciliation, which runs before any listener); broadcast capacity 256; unchanged in M9 (no instance field: a view re-reads the run) |
| `workflow-trigger` | `WorkflowTriggerEvent{trigger, workflow, state, queued, listening, lastFiredMs, run?}` (a struct; `run` = the run it just started) | A trigger was enabled or disabled, its project closed, trust was revoked, its project became ready (active triggers), or a check suspended, first queued, first deferred by a limit, fired, or had its start refused; not on debounce scheduling; broadcast capacity 256 |
| `project-op` | `OpResult{plan, outcomes, notices}` (a struct) | After every `project_op_apply` and `project_op_restore`, an agent's `delete_paths`/`copy_paths` apply and review restore, and the reconciliation of an interrupted operation when its project's graph becomes ready (§8.1) |
| `file-import` | `FileImportProgress{import, root, folder, total, done, finished, canceled, items}` (a struct) | Progress and result of an OS drop import (§3.7); emitted directly by the import, not from a broadcast channel |

Listeners (`src/lib/ipc.ts` `events`; graph channels in `src/lib/graphIpc.ts` `graphEvents`; `research:setup` and `research:setup-resync` in `src/features/research/ipc.ts` `setupEvents` (→ research setup views); `lean:setup` and `lean:setup-resync` in `src/lib/ipc.ts` (→ the Mathlib setup dialog, status item, and Lean view); `workflow-run` and `workflow-trigger` in `src/features/workflows/ipc.ts` `workflowEvents`; `project-op` in `src/features/operations/ipc.ts` `opEvents`, fanned out by the operation client with duplicates dropped; `file-import` in `src/features/workspace/fileIpc.ts`): documents → editor, page, agents; workspace → workspace, editor, agents, research; graph → graph, research; workflow-run → workflows (run tab, run lists, end-of-run notifications); workflow-trigger → workflows (Triggers section, one notification per suspension); project-op → workspace (Files folders), research (listing), workflows (list, run views), agents (task lists), settings (Storage & History); jobs → jobs; trust → page, research; lean → lean; typesetting:build → page, typesetting; typesetting:status → typesetting; agents → agents; research → research. The window's close request (Tauri built-in) is handled by the editor's unsaved-changes guard. Backend-internal (no webview event): the agents service clears the task allowances of a folder whose trust is revoked; the trigger engine follows workspace `opened`/`closed`/`filesChanged` (a lagged receiver counts as a rescan), graph `reconciled`/`status` (indexing finished), trust revocations, and run ends; TeX and typesetting rebuild the watched entries a `changed` document can concern (§3.1; every watched entry when events were missed) and every watched entry on `filesChanged`; workspace `closed` forgets watched entries (typesetting also drops its retained builds; TeX releases the workers, builds, and page images of every folder but the open one on `opened` and `closed`).

## 12. Connections and processes

| Network endpoint (the only one in app code) | Request | Rules |
|---|---|---|
| `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` (agents) | `POST`, SSE; headers `x-api-key`, `anthropic-version: 2026-10-21`, `anthropic-beta: server-side-fallback-2026-07-01` (model ids starting `claude-opus-5`, `claude-fable-5`, `claude-mythos-5`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`), `content-type: application/json`, `accept: text/event-stream`, `User-Agent: Exposition/<version>` | base URL fixed; connect 30 s, idle read 300 s; redirects never followed (3xx → error); system/env proxy honoured; reqwest `default-tls` |

- **Network by external programs, not app code:** `elan toolchain install` (explicit, confirmed, trusted; also the first step of a Mathlib setup whose pinned toolchain is missing) downloads Lean toolchains; a Mathlib setup (explicit, planned, trusted) runs `lake update`, which clones Mathlib and its dependencies from GitHub, and `lake exe cache get`, which downloads Mathlib's prebuilt build outputs from `https://cache.mathlib.org` (several GB into the package's `.lake`; §5); pip during guided environment setup (explicit, planned, trusted) downloads binary wheels from `https://pypi.org/simple` only (§4); the system browser opens links the user chose (`app_open_external`); trusted native code (kernels, approved commands/Python, LaTeX, Lake) has the user's network access; a TeX distribution's on-the-fly package install is not addressed in code.
- **Never contacted (code-backed):** no telemetry, analytics, crash reporting or updater (only plugin `tauri-plugin-dialog`); CSP `connect-src ipc: http://ipc.localhost`; Monaco, workers, fonts bundled; Typst packages only from local `typst/packages` dirs (`typst-kit` without downloads); Markdown refuses URL images; editor links copied, never opened; agent responses load no images and open a link only in the system browser after the user's click; no runtime installs, and no package installs except guided setup's pip into a project environment; Docker images never pulled; containers `--network=none`.

**Docker** (local engine via the `docker` CLI; client runs in a job object with only `DOCKER_HOST`, `DOCKER_CONTEXT`, `DOCKER_CONFIG`, `DOCKER_CERT_PATH`, `DOCKER_TLS_VERIFY`, cwd `<data>/execution/probe`)

| When | Invocation | Timeout |
|---|---|---|
| Settings › Execution image list | `docker image ls --format {{json .}}` | 30 s |
| App start (background, only if an image is configured); retried before the first restricted job | `docker ps -aq --filter label=org.exposition.instance=<FNV-1a-64 of lowercased <data>/execution>`, then `docker rm -f <hex id>` each | 20 s each |
| Each restricted job | preflight `docker image inspect --format {{.Id}} <image>` (20 s), then `docker run --rm --pull=never --network=none --read-only --cap-drop=ALL --security-opt=no-new-privileges --pids-limit=<256> --memory=<2g> --memory-swap=<same> --cpus=<2> --user=<65534:65534> --init --hostname=sandbox --tmpfs=/tmp:rw,nosuid,nodev,size=256m --mount=type=bind,source=<root>,target=/workspace,readonly --mount=type=bind,source=<job>/out,target=/out [--mount=…<job>/in,target=/exposition-input,readonly] --workdir=/workspace/<rel> --env-file=<job>/container.env --name=exposition-<job> --label=org.exposition.{managed=1,job=<job>,instance=<instance>} --log-driver=none -i --entrypoint=/bin/sh <image> -c <supervisor script> exposition-job <program> <args…>` | job timeout |
| Cancel / timeout | `docker rm -f exposition-<job>` (tests/diagnostics also `docker ps -aq --filter name=^exposition-<job>$`) | 20 s |
| Restricted availability for workflows (operations, validation, save, import, get, list, plan, the Research page's restricted row, trigger reviews and fire-time checks, reproduction drift) and again at the start of each workflow batch | `docker image inspect --format {{.Id}} <image>` once for the image the step uses (the Python image, or the R / Julia image) (the one answer gives presence and the image id recorded with the run); never pulls; answers reused for 5 s per project | 20 s each |
| First restricted Python workflow batch per image identity (M9 environment probe, before staging; R and Julia images are not probed) | a restricted job (same `docker run` flags as above, started from the identity, an empty working folder at `/workspace`) running `python3 -c <probe>` that lists the image's installed distributions; label "Restricted image environment probe (…)" in Jobs; result cached in `<data>/research/image-environments/` | 30 s, log 4 MiB |

**External programs** (all via the shared execution service: job object, constructed env, argument array, no shell)

| Program and arguments | When | Policy, timeout |
|---|---|---|
| 18 catalog tools (pdflatex, lualatex, latexmk, biber, bibtex, synctex, python, python-jupyter, Rscript, R, irkernel, julia, ijulia, lean, lake, elan, docker, git): `--version` (latexmk `-v`, synctex `help`, python `-E -c …`, docker `version --format …`, IRkernel/IJulia package checks) | Toolchains view load/refresh, one per located tool | Probe (no trust), 90 s |
| `pdflatex`/`lualatex -synctex=1 -interaction=nonstopmode -file-line-error -no-shell-escape -recorder -output-directory=<out> <entry>`; `--version` (cached) | LaTeX build passes and reruns | TrustedNative, 180 s (version 20 s) |
| `bibtex <job>`; `biber --input-directory <mirror> --input-directory <project> <job>` | bibliography pass when needed | TrustedNative, 180 s |
| `py.exe -0p`; `python -c <probe>` (project `.venv`/`venv` only when trusted: `-I -c`); `Rscript --vanilla -e <probe>`; `julia --startup-file=no --history-file=no -e <probe>` | Research environment discovery | Probe / project job |
| `python -u -X utf8 <data>/research/kernel-host/0.1.0-<sha12>/exposition_kernel_host.py` | Research session start | TrustedNative, interactive |
| `<discovered python> -I -m venv <root>/.venv`; `<.venv or venv python> -I -m pip --isolated install --disable-pip-version-check --no-input --require-virtualenv --only-binary=:all: --index-url https://pypi.org/simple <validated packages>` | Guided environment setup (the user's Set up environment / Install packages, or an agent's `install_packages`), §4 | TrustedNative job bound to the project, run in an empty app-owned working folder, `PIP_CONFIG_FILE` = null device, research environment, 5 min / 30 min, log 1 MiB |
| helper children: Python kernel `<python> <helper.py> --ipykernel -f {connection_file} --HistoryManager.enabled=False`; IRkernel/IJulia argv from `kernel.json`; `taskkill.exe /PID <pid> /T /F` on restart | Session run / restart | inside the helper's kill-on-close job |
| `<toolchain>/bin/lake serve` (Lake project) or `lean --server` (standalone); `lean --version` (60 s) | Lean file opened in a trusted folder | TrustedNative, no timeout |
| `lean -R <dir> -o <olean> <src>` ×2 (Compile, Check programs, once per toolchain and session); `lake env` (Lake projects) | Lean audit preparation | TrustedNative, 5 min |
| `lean --run RunCompile.lean <src> <olean> <module>` (secret on stdin); `lean --run RunCheck.lean <tag> <lib> <module>` | Lean audit steps 1 and 2 | TrustedNative, 15 min |
| `elan toolchain install <qualified name>` | explicit "Install toolchain" | TrustedNative |
| `elan toolchain install <qualified name>` (only when the pin is not installed); `<toolchain>/bin/lake --keep-toolchain update` with `MATHLIB_NO_CACHE_ON_UPDATE=1` (only without a manifest); `<toolchain>/bin/lake exe cache get`; `<toolchain>/bin/lake build <Name>` (new package) or `lake build --no-build Mathlib` (existing package) | Mathlib setup the user started (§5), one step after the other in the package folder | TrustedNative; 60 min, 30 min, 60 min, 30 min |
| approved program + args; container `python -` (code from `/exposition-input/stdin`) | agent `run_command` / `run_python` | TrustedNative after approval (or covered by a task allowance or Auto's Code: native) or Restricted, 10 min |
| container `python3 <entry>` (working copy at `/workspace`, read-only); native `<discovered python> <data>/research/batches/<run>/work/<entry>` (`<entry>` = `__exposition_main__.py` or the declared script's path) | workflow Python batch step (or for-each instance / until iteration) of a started run (§7.7), several at once within the limits | Restricted (Docker), or TrustedNative in a trusted folder; the step's time limit (≤ 3600 s), job log 1 MiB per stream |
| container `Rscript --vanilla <entry>` / `julia --startup-file=no --history-file=no <entry>` (working copy at `/workspace`, read-only; the configured R / Julia image, started by its approved identity); native discovered `Rscript --vanilla <script>` / `julia --startup-file=no --history-file=no <script>` (plain absolute path in `<data>/research/batches/<run>/work`) | workflow R/Julia batch step (or instance) of a started run (§7.7) | Restricted (Docker), or TrustedNative in a trusted folder; the step's time limit (≤ 3600 s), job log 1 MiB per stream |

Not launched: latexmk, makeindex, kpsewhich, synctex CLI, Typst CLI, any rasterizer (Typst, Markdown and PDF rendering run in-process); `exposition-testproc` is a test fixture. Outside the execution service: `app_open_external` hands a validated http/https URL to the system's default browser (`ShellExecuteW` "open" on Windows). The project graph (reconciliation, extraction, backups) and project creation start no process and use no network; `project_capabilities` reuses the toolchain and research-environment discovery above and reads elan's toolchains folder. Workflow draft commands run nothing of the workflow, but their runnability check asks Docker about the configured image (above) and, in a trusted folder, reuses research-environment discovery for native Python. A started run's processes are its batch jobs and the image probe (above), its Lean audit (the Lean service's own processes), and its agent review (the provider endpoint, §6). Environment capture, manifests, reproduction plans, bundles, and triggers launch nothing else: native environments come from research discovery's cached report, Lean provenance from files, and a trigger's run is an ordinary run.

| IPC channel | Transport and rules |
|---|---|
| Webview ↔ core | Tauri IPC (`ipc:` / `http://ipc.localhost`) + events; ACL-gated (§10–11) |
| Research supervisor ↔ kernel host | stdio, one JSON object per line; protocol version 1 handshake; protocol stdout reserved by fd dup; malformed lines ignored |
| Kernel host ↔ Jupyter kernel | ZeroMQ, jupyter_client default (TCP 127.0.0.1), HMAC-signed; CurveZMQ only for Python kernels when pyzmq, jupyter_client and ipykernel support it, else `transportEncryption: "none"` |
| Lean server; Lean audit | LSP JSON-RPC over stdio, Content-Length framing, header ≤ 8 KiB, body ≤ 64 MiB, no widgets/RPC; audit results read from stdout lines tagged with a per-run secret/nonce |

| OS facility | Use |
|---|---|
| Windows Credential Manager | API keys, generic credentials `anthropic-api-key.org.exposition.desktop` and `openai-api-key.org.exposition.desktop` (debug default `….development`), `CRED_PERSIST_LOCAL_MACHINE` (roaming entries moved on first read: roaming copy deleted, local copy written); Keychain/keyutils via `keyring` elsewhere (unverified) |
| Job Objects | every child: `KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, `DIE_ON_UNHANDLED_EXCEPTION`, no breakaway; `CREATE_SUSPENDED \| CREATE_NO_WINDOW` → assign → resume; tree killed when the primary exits; app exit runs `tex.shutdown()` + `execution.shutdown()` (≤ 5 s); crash → handle close kills trees |
| File watching | `notify` (ReadDirectoryChangesW), recursive on the open root; notifications that could report nothing (access events, and paths only in `.git`, `.lake`, `node_modules`, `target`, save temps, or graph storage) are dropped before they are queued; at most 16,384 notifications wait, further ones are dropped and the batch that follows is a `filesChanged{rescan: true}` for that folder; batch handled 150 ms after its first event; journals throttled to 300 ms |
| Recycle Bin (P1) | `trash` crate 5.3 (Windows shell file operation with undo, no UI; freedesktop trash on Linux; the Trash on macOS) on a fresh thread per call; Windows bin listing and restore (`trash::os_limited`) to identify and restore an item (§8.3); never used for a permanent deletion |
| Clipboard (P1, Windows) | `CF_HDROP` file list read and written through `windows-sys` (`OpenClipboard` with 40 retries × 25 ms, `SetClipboardData`, "Preferred DropEffect" = copy); clipboard text is never read as paths |
| WebView2 / dialog | Tauri defaults, no extra browser args; window `main` 1440×900, min 720×480, drag-drop off; native folder picker via `dialog:allow-open`; save dialogs (exports, Save a Copy) opened by the backend |

## 13. Data and storage

`<data>` = `EXPOSITION_DATA_DIR` if non-empty, else `app_local_data_dir()` (Windows `%LOCALAPPDATA%\org.exposition.desktop`); never a project, never trustable.

```
<data>/settings.json            AppSettings; invalid → kept as settings.json.invalid-<ms> (original removed only after a byte-identical copy), defaults + one warning
<data>/recent-projects.json     {version:1, projects:[{path,name,openedAt}]}, ≤12, newest first (+ .invalid-<ms>)
<data>/agents.sqlite3 (-wal, -shm)   tables tasks, transcript, tool_calls, checkpoints, task_files, changes, settings; v2 task_graph, task_graph_contexts, task_targets, task_proposals; v3 tasks.deleted_at; v4 task_files.op_ref; v5 tool_calls.authorized_by, tasks.allowance_version, task_allowances (schema 5); agents.sqlite3.premigration-v<N>-<ms> (copy taken before an upgrade)
<data>/graph-operations/<24 hex>/   operation journal per project (SHA-256 of the lowercased root): <op-id>.json (promotion, projectCreation, crossService incl. project operations with their restore records; ≤200 per project, ≤1 MiB each)
<data>/project-creations/       <op-id>.json: full blueprint + per-file SHA-256 of a project creation (≤32 MiB), for Finish Setup / Keep as Is
<data>/workflow-runs.sqlite3 (-wal, -shm)   workflow run history: table runs (one JSON body per run, keyed by project; deleted_at since user_version 1; optional index runs_listing; §7.7); workflow-runs.sqlite3.premigration-v<N>-<ms> (copy taken before an upgrade); unusable → set aside as workflow-runs.sqlite3.bad-<ms>; written by a newer version → left unchanged, runs kept in memory
<data>/workflow-triggers.json   workflow triggers of every project (exposition.workflowTriggers/1; standing plans, baselines, fire times, history; §7.8); written via .tmp-<uuid> + rename; unparsable → set aside as workflow-triggers.json.bad-<ms>
<data>/research/batches/<run>/  work/ (staged declared inputs + code; an empty folder for an image probe), out/ (native batch output area); removed after each batch and at startup
<data>/research/image-environments/<64 hex>.json   a restricted image's installed Python distributions, one file per image identity (exposition.imageEnvironment/1, ≤ 2 MiB; §7.9)
<data>/recovery/<wskey>/        live-<pkey>.json (dirty buffer), rec-<pkey>-<ms>.json (earlier session), bad-<pkey>-<ms>.json + .reason (quarantined); keys = 16 hex of SHA-256
<data>/execution/               settings.json (+ .tmp, .invalid-<ms>; bad values reset with warning), trust.json (+ .tmp), project-permissions.json (Auto permissions per project, §9; + .invalid-<ms>), probe/, cache/par/ (PAR_GLOBAL_TEMP)
<data>/execution/jobs/<job>/    tmp/ (removed), out/ (/out, kept if non-empty), container.env (removed), in/ (restricted batch input, removed when the job ends)
<data>/tex/<sha16(root)>/       mirror-<sha16>/ (project copy), <key>/out/ (aux, log, pdf, synctex.gz), <key>/builds/<build-id>/ (frozen; 4 kept)
<data>/research/                kernel-host/0.1.0-<sha12>/exposition_kernel_host.py, runtime/ (connection files, HMAC keys), probe/, selections.json (remembered environment per project, §9), setup-records.json (unfinished setups, §9)
<data>/lean/                    setup-records.json (Mathlib setups that may have left a package incomplete, §9)
<data>/EBWebView/               WebView2 profile (Tauri default)
%TEMP%\exposition-lean\audit\   runs/<id>/ (removed after each audit), tools-<session>/ (removed when the service drops)
```

- **Project-side:** `.exposition/project.json` (+ `.invalid-<ms>`, `ProjectSettingsInvalid` event); `.exposition/runs/<run-id>.json` (workflow batches too: owner `workflow`, `execution` mode); `.exposition/artifacts/<run-id>/output-N.{png,jpg,gif,svg,html,csv}` (workflow batches: `<name>.{png,jpg,jpeg,gif,svg,pdf,csv,tsv,json,txt,html}`) + `transcript.json` (text artifacts redacted); workflow run records and triggers are not project-side (`<data>/workflow-runs.sqlite3`, `<data>/workflow-triggers.json`); exported workflow bundles and run manifests go only where the user chooses; `.exposition/runs/deleted/<run-id>.json` + `<run-id>.deleted-at` (a recoverably deleted research run record and its deletion time, P1); `.exposition/artifacts/deleted/<run-id>/` + `.deleted-at` (recoverably deleted run outputs, held until restored or purged, P1); `.exposition/graph.sqlite3` (+ `-journal` while writing; project graph schema 5, also holds saved views, workflow drafts of both schemas, and recoverable deletions of graph records and workflows, §7.1); `.exposition/graph-backups/graph-<ms>-r<revision>-<reason>.sqlite3` (newest 8); `.exposition/graph.damaged-<ms>.sqlite3` (a damaged graph set aside by recovery, never deleted); `.exposition/research/<rs-id>/{sources,data,notes}/` (research space, §4.1); `.venv/` created by guided setup (§4) with `.venv/.exposition-setup.json` (`exposition.environmentSetup/1`: token, `inProgress` / `complete`, creation time).
- **Project-side, transient or tool-owned:** `.<name>.exposition-save-<hex>.tmp` (atomic save, hidden); `.exposition-save-op…tmp` / `.exposition-save-rs…tmp` (a copy or restore being published, hidden; after an interruption a copy's leftovers are removed and a restore's item is completed, put back in the Recycle Bin, or kept and reported, never deleted); `.exposition/graph-backups/*.partial` and `restore-<ms>.sqlite3` (backup and restore in progress; leftovers older than 1 h removed); exported `<stem>.pdf` via `<stem>.pdf.<pid>.tmp`; `.lake/` (Lake); `exposition-dev-ran.txt` (debug dev job).
- **Browser storage:** localStorage `exposition.theme`, `exposition.workbench.layout`, `exposition.graph.mode.project` (Graph page Canvas / Table), `exposition.workflows.view` (workflow editor Form / Canvas), `exposition.workflows.layout.<workflow id>` (workflow canvas positions), `exposition.operations.undo:<project root>` (Undo list: restore tokens, targets, names); sessionStorage `exposition.startupReopenAttempted`; never document content or secrets. Credential: OS store (§12).

|---|---|---|

| `PATH`, `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `USERPROFILE`, `ProgramFiles`, `PROGRAMDATA`, `SystemRoot`, `ELAN_HOME`, `HOME` | tool, environment and elan discovery (host `PATH` never passed on) | all |
| `SystemDrive`, `PUBLIC`, `TEMP`, `TMP`, `OneDrive`, `windir`, `ProgramFiles(x86)`, `ProgramW6432`, `ProgramData`, `ALLUSERSPROFILE` (+ above; Unix `TMPDIR`) | too-broad folder rules; `TEMP`/`TMP` also Lean scratch | all |
| `DOCKER_HOST`, `DOCKER_CONTEXT`, `DOCKER_CONFIG`, `DOCKER_CERT_PATH`, `DOCKER_TLS_VERIFY` | docker client only | all |
| all host variables | credential-named values become redaction literals; 28-name base list copied to children (`SystemRoot`, `windir`, `SystemDrive`, `ComSpec`, `PATHEXT`, `OS`, `PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE`, `PROCESSOR_IDENTIFIER`, `PROCESSOR_LEVEL`, `PROCESSOR_REVISION`, `NUMBER_OF_PROCESSORS`, `USERPROFILE`, `HOMEDRIVE`, `HOMEPATH`, `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `ProgramData`, `ProgramFiles`, `ProgramFiles(x86)`, `ProgramW6432`, `CommonProgramFiles`, `CommonProgramFiles(x86)`, `CommonProgramW6432`, `PUBLIC`, `ALLUSERSPROFILE`, `USERNAME`, `USERDOMAIN`, `COMPUTERNAME`) | all |

Set for children: job `TEMP`/`TMP`, constructed `PATH`, `PAR_GLOBAL_TEMP`; research `PYTHONUTF8`, `PYTHONIOENCODING`, `PYTHONUNBUFFERED`, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE`, `PYDEVD_DISABLE_FILE_VALIDATION`, `JUPYTER_RUNTIME_DIR`, `R_LIBS_USER`/`JULIA_DEPOT_PATH`; TeX `TEXINPUTS`, `BIBINPUTS`, `BSTINPUTS`, `max_print_line`, `error_line`, `half_error_line`, `openout_any=p`, `openin_any=p`, `shell_escape=f`; Lean audit `LEAN_PATH`; containers `PATH`, `HOME=/tmp`, `TMPDIR=/tmp`, `LANG=C.UTF-8`; workflow batches `EXPOSITION_OUTPUT_DIR` (restricted `/out`, native `<data>/research/batches/<run>/out`), `EXPOSITION_SEED` (decimal, §7.9), `EXPOSITION_ITERATION` (repeated steps), `EXPOSITION_PARAM_<NAME>` (parameters set on the step), Python also `MPLBACKEND=Agg`, restricted Julia `PATH=/usr/local/julia/bin:…`, native R/Julia discovery's `R_LIBS_USER` / `JULIA_DEPOT_PATH`, restricted Python also `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE`, `PYTHONUNBUFFERED`, `PYTHONIOENCODING`, `MPLCONFIGDIR=/tmp/matplotlib`, native also `PYTHONUTF8`, `PYTHONIOENCODING`, `PYTHONUNBUFFERED`, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE`, `PYDEVD_DISABLE_FILE_VALIDATION`.

## 14. Security boundaries

- **CSP:** `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; font-src 'self' data:; worker-src 'self' blob:; connect-src ipc: http://ipc.localhost; object-src 'none'; frame-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'`.
- **ACL:** one capability `main-window` (window `main`): `core:event:default`, `core:window:allow-set-title`, `core:window:allow-destroy`, `dialog:allow-open` + 180 `allow-<command>`; no `core:default`, no `dialog:allow-save` (export and Save a Copy save dialogs are opened by the backend), no fs/shell/http/opener plugin; debug builds add `dev-execution-probe`, `dev-agents-scripted`, and `dev-research-faults` at runtime.
- **Check-permissions gate:** `npm run check` fails when any `src-tauri/permissions/autogenerated/*.toml` matches no `COMMANDS`/`DEV_COMMANDS` entry (now 184 files = 180 + 4); the same pipeline runs the private-path guard and bindings drift check.
- **IPC boundary:** no command runs an arbitrary program; ids, sizes, page indexes, coordinates validated; `WorkspacePath` rejects absolute, `..`, reserved names and invalid characters during deserialization.
- **Trust model:** trust lives in `<data>/execution/trust.json`, never in the project; opening never runs code or grants trust; native jobs (TeX, kernels, Lean, commands) need a trusted folder, re-checked at spawn; revoke cancels the folder's jobs (≤10 s each) and broadcasts `trust:changed` to every view.
- **Too-broad folders:** drive/share roots, personal folders and their ancestors, system folders and anything inside them cannot be opened or trusted; compared by canonical text and by file identity (volume serial + file index), so `\\localhost\C$\…`, `\\127.0.0.1\C$\…`, `\\?\UNC\localhost\C$\…`, `\\localhost\ADMIN$` aliases are refused.
- **Data folder:** never a project (is, inside, or contains `<data>` → `workspace.tooBroad`) and never trustable (nor anything above or inside it), by name and identity.
- **Tool-location re-check:** a configured tool path inside an open project or the execution data folder (name or identity) is refused when set and at every use (jobs, probes, TeX, Docker, sweep) → `RefusedOverride` / `programRefused`.
- **Program validation:** absolute path or catalog id; Windows runs `.exe` only (`.bat`, `.cmd`, `cmd.exe` refused); no `..`; argument arrays, no shell; NUL refused.
- **Environment scrubbing:** child env built from empty (28-name base list, constructed `PATH`, job allowlist); credential-like names or secret-looking values make the job fail (`environmentRefused`), never silently filtered; the refusal names the variable, never its value. Values of credential-named host variables count as secrets only when they can be: not when the name ends `_PROFILE`, `_REGION`, `_CONFIG_DIR`, `_CONFIG_FILE`, `_SESSION_ID`, `_HOME`, `_PATH`, `_DIR`, `_FILE`, or `_VERSION`, not when the value is a path, and only from 16 characters for names matched by a provider prefix alone (8 for names that say token, password, key, or secret).
- **Restricted backend:** Docker only; explicit, locally present image checked by one reference grammar in settings and at launch; flags in §12; settings refuse root `uid:gid`, invalid memory/cpus/pids; never a native fallback. Workflow restricted batches use the same backend (R and Julia only with their own configured images, each bound by identity in the plan, re-checked at dispatch, and started by identity) with a staged working copy of only their declared inputs and code as the read-only mount (never the project), refuse a batch whose Docker settings changed after the check, and record the image, image id, user, and limits in the run manifest (§7.7).
- **Container lifetime:** batch jobs hold stdin as a liveness pipe, so the container kills itself when the app exits or crashes; in-container `sleep <limit+5 s> && kill -9`; `--rm`; cancel/timeout `docker rm -f`; leftovers of this data folder swept at startup and before the first restricted job.
- **Redaction:** job output, events and summaries redact literal values of credential-named host variables (≥8 chars) and token shapes (`sk-ant-`, `sk-`, `gh[pousr]_`, `github_pat_`, `glpat-`, `npm_`, `hf_`, `xox?-`, `AKIA|ASIA`, `AIza`, `bearer`, PEM keys) to `[REDACTED]`; kernel stream/display/error output redacted; text artifacts (HTML, SVG, CSV, JSON) redacted and rewritten before registration; provider errors replace the key with `<redacted>`.
- **Credentials:** the key lives only in the OS store, kept machine-local on Windows; the webview learns only "configured"; it never enters child envs, the database or logs; validation errors never echo it; debug builds use a `development` entry unless told otherwise; output redaction matches it by its value (registered when read or saved) and by token shape; it is sent only to the fixed endpoint, redirects never followed; code trusted to run as the user can still read it.
- **Project path rules:** reads and writes canonicalize and must stay inside the root (`path.outsideWorkspace`, `path.brokenLink`); aliases (case, 8.3, links, hard links) map to one document; link-following listing stays inside the root; resolve-then-act is not atomic (TOCTOU).
- **Saves:** temp file + fsync + rename, original untouched on failure; refused if the disk hash changed (unless Overwrite), read-only, a folder, too large, or binary; rename breaks hard links, so a file outside the folder is never written.
- **Rendering:** Typst pages as SVG `<img>`; kernel HTML parsed inert with handlers neutralized; Lean hovers untrusted (no HTML, no command links), goals plain text; editor links copied, never opened; agent responses through the one shared Markdown renderer (§6.13): raw HTML shown as text, never elements; no `href`/`target` on any link; images never loaded; KaTeX without `trust`; no IPC access besides opening a link the user chose.
- **Navigation:** the main window never leaves the app's own content (`navigation-guard` plugin: `tauri:`, `http(s)://tauri.localhost`, `about:blank`, and in debug builds `localhost:1420`); external links open only in the system browser through `app_open_external` (absolute http/https with a host, no credentials, no whitespace, control, invisible, or direction-changing characters, ≤ 2048 characters), after the user's click.
- **TeX:** builds require trust; `-no-shell-escape`, `shell_escape=f`, `openout_any=p`; reads are not confined, hence the trust requirement; the engine writes only to the app-owned mirror/out dirs; rendered pages capped at 32 Mi pixels.
- **Builds and exports:** builds of a folder no longer open are never served or exported; export refuses an existing destination that is not a file or not a PDF, a source, an open document, or a build input.
- **Research:** sessions need trust and are bound to one root; only discovered environment ids launch; project venvs probed only when trusted (`-I`), and never one whose setup did not finish; the folder reaches R/Julia as bytes, never spliced into code; webview sessions and runs are owner `User`, and the webview cannot supply a requester or a run `authorization`; interrupt/restart/shutdown/cancel have no owner check (the user may stop agent sessions).
- **Environment setup:** plans are built in Rust from discovered environments (the webview names an environment id, never an executable) and validated package names; only `<root>/.venv` is created, from a Python outside the project; pip runs isolated, with no configuration file, from an empty app-owned working folder, inside the virtual environment only, binary wheels only, from PyPI only (names that look like local files are refused); planning never runs a project interpreter; no global install, no elevation, no repository setup script or requirements file; steps are trusted native jobs (a virtual environment is not a sandbox: installed packages run code with the user's privileges when imported); starting re-plans; a failed or canceled setup removes only a folder it created, proved by its marker and the app-local record, never a link; existing environments are never deleted.
- **Lean:** server, audit and install run TrustedNative; elan shims never run; no implicit downloads, also not through Lake: the server does not start on a Mathlib package whose dependencies or prebuilt cache are missing, nor on a package whose `lakefile.lean` has unresolved requirements and mentions Mathlib (§5; a `lakefile.lean` that requires other libraries without mentioning Mathlib is started, and Lake fetches them); a Mathlib setup is started only by the user from a plan built in Rust (the webview names a folder and a library name, never a program or revision), names where every dependency is fetched from, is re-planned at start and refused when the lakefile, the manifest, or any source changed since the review, prepares `mathlib` only from the git address the reviewed plan shows (the official repository, or a fork marked "Not official" under a warning; a local folder is refused; checked at planning and again after `lake update`, before the cache program of that repository is built and run), removes on failure or cancel only what it created and still recognizes as its own, needs trust for the package folder (for a folder that does not exist yet, the nearest existing folder above it), runs TrustedNative with no sandbox for Lake (its configuration and downloaded programs run with the account's privileges), never changes elan's default toolchain or an existing lakefile, `lean-toolchain`, or manifest, and no agent tool reaches it; audit step 2 runs none of the file's code; audit programs SHA-256-pinned and held open deny-write; outputs tagged with per-run secret/nonce; name tripwire against the server's index.
- **Agents:** one central gate checks each tool call's declared reads/changes/runs against scope, consent, profile, execution mode, approval mode (the project's permissions in Auto, the task's allowances) and budgets before anything happens, and the basis is re-read immediately before the call runs (§6.4). Only the webview's composer, approval card, task view, and Settings change permissions or allowances; no tool, schema field, file, prompt, or tool output can, and a model cannot approve its own requests. Project permissions are app-local, bound to the folder and the graph identity; storing them outside the project keeps them away from agent tools and project content, not from native code running with the user's privileges. Approval mode and execution containment are independent: Auto never turns a restricted launch into a native one. Workflow agent steps are always Ask.
- **Project graph:** the project's `graph.sqlite3` is untrusted input: opened only if ≤ 1 GiB, a regular file inside a real `.exposition` folder (no links), with SQLite defensive mode and triggers, views, and untrusted schema disabled, exactly the expected schema objects and columns, `quick_check`, foreign keys, and every row decoded and bounded; a refused database is left unchanged and never replaced automatically (§7.1). Statements are parameterized and reads bounded. No SQL, no service-owned records (evidence, observed relations, service batches: `graph.notService`), and no execution are reachable over IPC; the webview's changes are attributed to the user and agents' to their task by the backend, never by the payload; agents' relations stay suggestions; validated proposals can be applied only by the origin that made them. Ids from IPC or the database are validated and never interpreted. The graph's own storage is never an agent file-tool target (`agents.graphStorage`). Saved views are presentation only.
- **Project creation:** blueprints (presets, agent proposals, imports) are parsed strictly in Rust before anything is written; no trust, run, startup-hook, or grant fields exist; paths are relative and confined, with tool folders, startup-hook files, executable extensions, and `.exposition/` content (except the research space) refused; files are created with create-new semantics (never replaced) in a new or empty folder that the workspace would open; a trusted parent folder needs explicit acknowledgement. Creation grants no trust, runs no code, installs nothing, and sends nothing to a model; only built-in presets assert relations.
- **Workflow drafts:** imported definitions and bundles are untrusted data (strict schema, unknown fields refused anywhere, ≤ 1 MiB per definition, ≤ 8 MiB per bundle, fresh ids, every structural check before anything is saved); saving, importing, or an agent's `workflow_propose` never runs anything; a stored or imported `runnable` claim is never read: runnability is recomputed for the open project from validation and runner availability. A composite's child is validated and expanded from the project's own saved revisions, its ceiling must fit within the parent's, and its pin never moves without a parent edit. Agents can propose only schema `/1` definitions and cannot revise a workflow that uses schema /2 features (`agents.workflowUnsupported`). Script inputs must be in the step's language, also through composites; batch parameters reach code only as validated one-line environment variables.
- **Exports:** a bundle or run manifest is written by the backend to a `.json` file the user picked in the backend's save dialog (or a path an IPC caller gives); an existing file is replaced only when it is an earlier bundle or manifest (by its `schema`); both exports and the bundle import refuse locations in the project's `.exposition` folder and the app data folder, links, and folders; manifests are scrubbed of local paths and secrets and carry no logs, code, or report text; bundles carry definitions only.
- **Workflow triggers:** only the user's webview commands create or change a trigger (no agent tool; definitions, bundles, blueprints, and the graph cannot carry one); enabling requires the hash of a standing plan reviewed in the last 30 min and recomputed identical; a trigger's run starts through the normal start check, and any change beyond the watched inputs' content suspends it; checks preview the plan without keeping it; code and Lean inputs cannot be watched, and their full text is in every plan; native steps need explicit per-trigger permission; rate and model-request limits are enforced by the engine; nothing fires on project open or catches up on changes made while closed; revoking trust suspends the folder's triggers; records live only in the app data folder, and an unreadable store is never overwritten; every review starts with native permission off.
- **Workflow runs:** a run starts only through `workflow_run_start` with the id and approval hash of a plan made in the last 30 min (or from a trigger's standing plan, §7.8), after the plan is recomputed and found identical (inputs by SHA-256, payloads, runners, budgets, conditions, repeats, composite expansion, `maxParallel` and the concurrency limits, image and its identity, folder trust when native steps exist, project); one run per workflow and at most 3 at once, with application-wide step slots; budgets reserved before each dispatch; steps are re-checked before each dispatch (project, cancellation, budgets, input and upstream digests, trust for native steps, the approved restricted image identity, runner availability; a restricted step is never downgraded and its container starts from the approved identity); outputs are validated in Rust (declared port and type, recomputed SHA-256, provenance against the dispatched operation, service-owned types only from their owning operation) before any consumer; interrupted runs are never resumed or replayed; agent review steps are read-only, run no processes, approve nothing, and count against the run's model-request budget; promotions and evidence associations are only proposed until the user approves them. Run records are app-local and read back as untrusted data.
- **Project operations (P1):** every route (shortcut, context menu, palette, Storage & History, agent tool) goes through one Rust orchestrator; the webview names targets, never decides authority, and cannot send an agent actor; targets are canonicalized inside the project (links and junctions never followed out of it; a junction's target is never deleted or copied), Exposition's own data under `.exposition/` and the app data folder are refused, read-only entries are not deleted, a folder is never copied into itself; a confirmation never overrides a refusal; apply re-plans and refuses a changed plan (`ops.planStale`); files go to the Recycle Bin and a failure never becomes a permanent deletion; copies and restores are published create-new (nothing is overwritten or merged); clipboard file lists and external paths are explicit paste inputs only, read and never granted any access; manual deletion needs no folder trust and runs nothing; copies inherit no evidence, verification, trigger, approval, or run history; agents keep their scope, consent, and permission profile (no widened grant; decision-needing plans refused in Ask `autoApply` tasks and otherwise approved once, never for the rest of a task), cannot touch app records, external files, running work, or triggers, and never delete permanently.

## 15. Dev/test-only features

**Compiled out of release builds** (`cfg(debug_assertions)`; ACL entries only when `PROFILE=debug`)

| Feature | Where | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `dev_start_probe_job` | `src-tauri/src/execution.rs` | "Dev probe: process tree": `python -E -u -c` spawns a sleeping grandchild, 400 burst lines, ticks every 0.5 s; Application kind, 10 min |
| `dev_start_workspace_job(root, holdSeconds?)` | same | registers `root` directly, Batch python writes `exposition-dev-ran.txt`; `holdSeconds` ≤600 adds a two-process tree; trust still required |
| `agents_dev_load_script` (≤1 MiB) | `src-tauri/src/agents.rs` | loads a scripted provider turn queue |
| `research_dev_fail_next_graph_write` | `src-tauri/src/research.rs` | makes the next promotion's graph write fail (`graph.injectedFailure`) to exercise pending reconciliation |
| Runtime capabilities `dev-execution-probe`, `dev-agents-scripted`, `dev-research-faults` | `execution::add_dev_capability`, `agents::add_dev_capability`, `research::add_dev_capability` | added in `setup` |
| Simulated crash during project creation | `src-tauri/src/projects.rs` (`EXPOSITION_TEST_INTERRUPT_PROJECT_CREATION=<n>`) | aborts the process after n files; release builds ignore the variable |
| Scripted provider (`provider::scripted`, `allow_scripted`) | `crates/exposition-agents` | release stub → `agents.scriptedUnavailable`; UI shows "Scripted provider — development only" only when `scriptedAvailable` |
| Credential namespace | `src-tauri/src/state.rs` | debug default entry `org.exposition.desktop.development`; `EXPOSITION_CREDENTIAL_NAMESPACE`; `EXPOSITION_USE_REAL_CREDENTIAL=1`; release always uses the real entry |
| Console window | `src-tauri/src/main.rs` | release sets `windows_subsystem = "windows"` |
| "(debug)" / "(debug build)" labels | status bar, Settings › About | from `AppInfo.debugBuild` |
| Debug-gated test files | `crates/exposition-agents/tests/{approval_modes,central_gate,execution_tools,lean_integration,review_actions,review_content,review_m5,services_integration,stop_reasons,tool_call_states,task_loop,workspace_integration}.rs`, `exposition-markdown/tests/typst_print.rs`, `exposition-typesetting/tests/latency.rs` | use `cfg(debug_assertions)` |

**Present in release although developer-oriented:** `EXPOSITION_DATA_DIR`, `EXPOSITION_TOOL_CACHE_DIR`; palette command "Developer: Show UI Reference" and the activity-bar menu item "UI Reference" (`ui-reference:` component gallery); `data-testid`, `data-offset`, `data-anchor` attributes; test hooks `queueEventForTest`/`flushEvents` (research store), `PageSession.caretPosition()`.

**Test-only code:** `exposition-testproc` binary (never launched by the app); `LocalProcessRunner`, `TexToolchain::discover`, `Fonts::embedded_only`; `crates/exposition-tex/examples/dump_glyphs.rs`; ignored measurements (`typesetting/tests/{dump,latency}.rs`, `tex/tests/latency.rs`, `graph/tests/perf.rs`, `lean/tests/{lean_audit_probe,review_m4_lean}.rs`); WMI-escape limitation test `execution/tests/review_exec.rs`.

**Checks** (`npm run check`): private paths → permission files → typecheck → vitest → Vite build → rustfmt → clippy `-D warnings` → `cargo test --workspace` → bindings drift (restores the previous bindings if regeneration fails).

**e2e harness** (`npm run e2e [-- <names>]`, `scripts/e2e/run.mjs`): launches `EXPOSITION_E2E_EXECUTABLE` or `target/debug/exposition.exe` with isolated `EXPOSITION_DATA_DIR`, `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER`, `WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS=--remote-debugging-port=<free port>`; connects with Playwright `connectOverCDP`; raw IPC via `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke`; strips `EXPOSITION_USE_REAL_CREDENTIAL` except for `live-*`; artifacts and last-state screenshot/console in `test-results/e2e/<name>/`. Helpers: `agent-scope-check.mjs`, `app.mjs`, `app-env.mjs`, `p1-helpers.mjs` (P1: notifications, decisions, Recycle Bin and clipboard read-back through PowerShell, reference screens), `palette.mjs`, `processes.mjs`, `wait.mjs`, `workspace-helpers.mjs`, `repro/page-race-keystrokes.mjs`.

**105 scenarios** (`scripts/e2e/scenarios/*.mjs`, every file there is a scenario; 94 in the default run, incl. editor-find-outline, qol-media, qol-files, project-switch, qol-journey, graph-dependency-status, page-recovery, p1-research-runs, workflow-preset-template, workflow-state-refresh: `run.mjs` takes the scenarios named on the command line, else every file whose name does not start with `live-`): a1-environment, a1-permissions, a1-responses, agent-save, agentctx-narration, agents-attention, agents-composer, agents-composer-input, agents-graph, agents-harness, - Excluded from the default run: the eleven `live-*` scenarios `live-a1-loop`, `live-agentctx`, `live-agents`, `live-attachments`, `live-graph`, `live-m9-agents`, `live-restricted`, `live-review-m8-agent`, `live-tool-continuations`, `live-workflow`, and `live-workflow-audit` (runner skips `live-*`; real key, spends API credit). `live-tool-continuations`: a real model answers from bounded tool results and their continuations, checked on the stored provider conversation (read-only questions; execution disabled except for the Python part): two facts in a 686 KB text file over the whole-read limit are found with partial `read_file` calls whose ranges cover both lines, nothing is read whole or edited; a file name beyond the first 500 entries of a 620-file folder reaches the model only in a `list_files` call that passes the truncated result's `nextOffset` and `listing`; a follow-up on an unchanged project graph is stored with the short unchanged statement and answered from the earlier context block without a tool call; and a `run_python` call that shows 650 values returns the latest 500 with `resultsOmitted` and `outputTruncated` while the last printed line is reported (this part is skipped, with the reason recorded, when the host has no usable Python research environment). `live-attachments`: a generated PNG and one-page PDF attached as pasted bytes are read by the real model, and a follow-up with a text attachment still answers from the earlier image (read-only tasks, execution disabled). `live-agentctx`: Auto with Files: edit and Code: off; a script is written and run (the first native run asks and is approved for the task), changed and run again (no card may appear), then the model explains how the user runs, saves, and keeps things; the model's sentences are recorded verbatim and the assertions cover what the application did (cards, authorizations) and the clearest narration errors. `live-workflow-audit`: a real model drafts a formal audit workflow from a request that names no port, parameter, or operation id; asserted: saving ran nothing, every proposal of the task is one workflow (a correction is a revision), the draft opens by its own id; reported, not asserted: whether the first proposal was already well-formed and what the closing message claims. `live-m9-agents`: a user-saved schema /2 workflow (maxParallel 2, model requests only) whose two read-only review steps over different declared files run at the same time against the real model, each its own read-only task with execution disabled scoped to its file, and a third review step receives both reports on its multiple material port in binding order; checks the overlap, 25 requests reserved per model step, the run's counted requests equal the tasks' turns, no command or approval, and no file changed. `live-review-m8-agent`: a workflow's read-only review step over material containing instructions to run code, write a file, and save a workflow; checks that nothing is written or saved and the output is a report only. `live-workflow`: a whole-project task with restricted execution proposes a restricted Python workflow over declared data with `workflow_propose` (the approval shows the complete definition; saving runs nothing), then the scenario plans the saved draft (restricted containment, inputs pinned by hash), starts it as the user, and checks that it ran in the Docker container and produced its validated output; then (W3) a user-saved workflow with a read-only agent review step runs against the real model: the plan shows it sends material and runs nothing, the review is a read-only task with execution disabled scoped to the declared files, its requests count against the run budget, it returns a report, and no file changes; model-dependent steps are reported as `model:` checks. `live-graph`: a whole-project task gets the scoped graph context, uses `graph_query`, proposes a dependency with `graph_propose` (approval card; stored as a suggestion attributed to the task) and saves a workflow draft with `workflow_propose` (the scenario checks that saving it ran nothing: no run exists for it), changing no files; a metadata-only task then never sends or receives a content-derived label (checked on the stored provider transcript). `live-restricted` uses no development-only command, so it also runs against release/installed builds: an unattended task's `run_python` in the Docker container, then the same request refused with no image configured.
- Self-skip unless named: `review-m5-agents`, `review-m7-graph-fs`, `review-m7-ipc`, `review-sec-acl`, `review-sec-exec`, `review-sec-ipc`, `review-sec-render`, `review-sec-secrets`.
- Need a debug build (counted by the scenario files that name a debug-only command of §10): 42. 21 call one of the eleven probe commands and say so in their header ("Requires a debug build"): `consistency`, `graph-core`, `graph-dependency-status`, `graph-evidence-impact`, `lean-check`, `mathlib`, `mathlib-plan`, `mathlib-setup`, `mathlib-setup-guard`, `page-latex`, `project-create`, `research-attach-build`, `research-workspace`, `review-m7-graph-fs`, `review-m7-ipc`, `review-sec-exec`, `review-sec-ipc`, `review-sec-render`, `tex-build`, `trust-propagation`, `v2-tour-research`; `m6-paper-project` calls `lean_check` only in a debug build and records that part as not verified otherwise. The others use the scripted provider (`agents_dev_load_script`), the fixed jobs (`dev_start_probe_job`, `dev_start_workspace_job`: `execution-jobs`, `settings`, `trust-propagation`, `v2-tour-research`), or a fault injection (`research-workspace`, `qol-files`). The release qualification run uses `smoke`, `ui-shell`, and `pages-review`, which need none of them (`docs/testing.md`).
- Report SKIPPED without Docker and the local image `python:3.13-slim` (fail instead with `EXPOSITION_REQUIRE_DOCKER=1`): `workflow-run` (its agent-generated part (scripted provider) runs only in debug builds), `m9-parallel`, `m9-triggers`, `m9-workflow-ui`, `workflow-repro`; `p1-decisions` reports its two Docker parts SKIPPED and runs the rest. `m9-bundles` needs neither Docker nor a debug build. None of the M9 scenarios uses a development-only command or the scripted provider.

- Mathlib coverage: `mathlib-plan` (default run, no network: opening a file of the unprepared package `tests/fixtures/lean-mathlib` (Lean `v4.35.0`, Mathlib `v4.35.0`) in a trusted folder starts no Lean server, downloads nothing, and creates no `.lake` or manifest; the Lean view says why and offers Set Up Mathlib…; the dialog shows the whole plan; a new package is refused inside or around an existing one and planned beside it; the Sources table names `mathlib`, the registry entry, the tag, and "Resolved by Lake" with its note; a tampered plan and an untrusted folder are refused; nothing in the project changed and standalone Lean still checks) and `mathlib-setup-guard` (default run, real IPC and the installed toolchain, no model. A, nothing runs: a Mathlib requirement written with TOML literal strings is seen and its package starts no server; the plan of a package with a manifest lists both pinned dependencies with address and commit and carries the lakefile and manifest digests; an untrusted folder starts nothing (`lean.untrusted`); a dependency redirected in the manifest after the review, and then an edited lakefile, each make the reviewed plan stale (`lean.setup.planStale`); a manifest taking `mathlib` from another repository is planned with `mathlibSource.official` false and that address, in the summary and under the plan hash (the plan edited to say official, and the plan made for the official repository, are stale), the package still counts as using Mathlib, and the dialog shows the warning with the address, the row marked "Not official", and one primary action; the build-from-source choice does not start Lean on a package whose dependencies are not resolved, and no `.lake` or manifest appears. B, a real setup started and canceled as soon as its first command runs: a new package in a folder that does not exist yet starts; a file the scenario adds to the new library folder meanwhile is kept, listed in `kept`, and named in the message; everything the setup created is removed, nothing else in the project is removed, nothing writes afterwards, the folder is not a package, and the status shows no credential. It reaches the network only for the first seconds of `lake update`). Opt-in, with real downloads of several GB: `mathlib-setup` (`EXPOSITION_MATHLIB_SETUP=1`, optionally `EXPOSITION_MATHLIB_SETUP_DIR`; through the dialog unless noted. 1: a new package canceled while Lake resolves, everything it created removed, the project tree and file digests exactly as before, the folder not a package. 2 (over IPC): an existing package without a manifest that requires the Mathlib release of another Lean plans one `lake update` and keeps the pin, fails at that step naming both Lean versions before any cache is fetched, has its lakefile and `lean-toolchain` unchanged and the created `.lake` and manifest removed, and reads `dependenciesUnresolved` afterwards. 3: a new package created and prepared, ending `ready`; `lean-toolchain` is the planned toolchain, the manifest's `mathlib` has the tag of that Lean as `inputRev` and a 40-hex commit, Mathlib's checkout pins the same Lean, the package folder holds only the planned files plus `.lake` and the manifest, the rest of the project is unchanged, and the starter file is Checked. 4: a new app session on the same data folder finds the folder trusted, plans `lean.setup.alreadyPrepared`, checks the starter as Checked with the manifest digest unchanged, no setup status, and no setup, update, or cache job; the timings are recorded. The prepared project is left in place for `mathlib`) and `mathlib` (`EXPOSITION_MATHLIB_PACKAGE=<prepared package>`; the editor, the Lean check IPC, an agent's `check_lean`, the Formal audit workflow, and formal evidence resolve the same package root, toolchain, and dependencies; the audit reports transitive axioms for a file that imports Mathlib; a manifest or source change makes recorded evidence stale; it never runs `lake update` and downloads nothing). Without their variables both report SKIPPED.

product defects print `DEFECT` lines without failing it, held checks fail it): A, a Lean source cannot be watched (`trigger.codeInputWatched`), the plan shows its full text and `suspendsOn` names it, an external `#eval` edit suspends the trigger with no run and no marker file, and a re-enabled trigger fires over the reviewed text through a watched data input;
