Not exercised end to end: the native save/open dialogs (unit tests cover their actions), interval triggers, trigger rate limits, native steps in triggers, trust revocation of a trigger, the queued follow-up, native/Lean/agent environment capture, and R/Julia batches (cargo tests: native ones skip without R/Julia, restricted ones need `EXPOSITION_DOCKER_TESTS=1` and `-- --ignored` with the local `r-base:4.6.2` / `julia:1.13` images).

## 16. Known limitations

From `docs/capabilities.md` (verified where code allows) plus code findings (marked *code*).
- Windows 11 x64 is the only verified platform; macOS/Linux build but are unverified (Unix process-group containment unverified).
- Agent saving (`save_document`): one open document with unsaved changes per call, to its own path only (no save-as, no save-all); the whole buffer is written, the user's unsaved changes in it included (disclosed, never split off); never over a file that changed on disk, a disk conflict, or recovered text; a save is not a review record, so Revert changes the buffer and the file keeps the saved text until it is saved again; whether a buffer holds changes from outside the task is read from the document history (2048 revisions), beyond which it is reported as unknown; enabled triggers that watch the saved file are named on the card and in the result and still fire, but a trigger that the save suspends (the file is a non-watched input of its standing plan, such as its script) and watching builds are not named.
- The NSIS installer (per user, no admin) is not code-signed; SmartScreen warns.
- Page editing refuses generated text: numbering, references, citations, bibliography entries, list markers, footnote marks, line-break hyphens.
- Page editing refuses code/macro output: Typst code expressions, `#let` and function bodies, loops, `#set`, imported modules, `#show` replacements; LaTeX user macros; raw/verbatim text; package content ("not editable here — edit in source").
- Structure-changing page edits (e.g. deleting a `\left`/`\right` delimiter, typing a character that opens markup) are refused, not guessed.
- LaTeX character mapping is inferred and checked glyph by glyph; unmapped text is not page-editable; exotic catcodes and the output that text-rewriting packages generate (`siunitx` unit and number formatting, `cleveref` names, TikZ) stay source-only; a literal argument that is typeset unchanged and maps glyph by glyph (e.g. the digits of `\qty{12.5}{\metre}`) is edited on the page like other text.
- LaTeX navigation is line level (SyncTeX) outside mapped text.
- pdfLaTeX: typed `"` becomes ` `` `/`''`; non-Latin text and combining marks are LaTeX errors. LuaLaTeX: missing glyphs give one warning per font.
- Mixed-direction lines: caret approximate exactly at a direction boundary.
- Typst content included twice is not detected as duplicated and stays editable in both places.
- If concurrent edits (source editor, agent) make page keystrokes unplaceable, typing pauses with a message; nothing is applied at a guessed position.
- Edit-to-page time (debug, Ryzen 7 9800X3D): Typst ~2–15 ms, Markdown ~16–26 ms, pdfLaTeX ~0.3–0.45 s, LuaLaTeX ~0.8–1 s.
- Markdown math is a declared LaTeX-math subset; unsupported commands show a marker and a diagnostic.
- TeX can read any file the account can read (`openin_any=p` does not confine absolute reads), so LaTeX builds require a trusted folder.
- Typst packages are never downloaded; they must already be in the local package directory.
- R interrupt is unsupported on Windows (IRkernel ignores it); Cancel restarts the kernel.
- Exposition never installs runtimes (missing ones: the official download page and re-detection). Guided setup covers Python only: a project `.venv` and packages from PyPI as binary wheels (no conda, uv, or poetry; no private index or proxy configuration, and the setup's clean environment carries no `HTTP(S)_PROXY`; pip's download cache in the user profile is used); verified on Windows only; no setup of a restricted Python image (the container image must already hold every package). R and Julia kernel packages (IRkernel, IJulia) are installed by the user with the shown commands, user-wide.
- Packages installed by setup run code with the user's privileges whenever they are imported; a virtual environment is not a sandbox.
- R/Julia kernel messages are HMAC-signed but unencrypted over loopback TCP; Python uses CurveZMQ only when pyzmq, jupyter_client and ipykernel support it, else also unencrypted.
- Research sessions need a trusted folder; switching or closing the folder, or revoking trust, detaches them.
- Lean "Checked" certifies the listed declarations as kernel-checked in the audit's own elaboration, not how the text reads (a file can redefine its syntax). A file written to behave differently under the audit is caught only when it drops a declaration the language server indexes; otherwise the list shows what was checked.
- A trusted Lean file's code runs with the user's privileges while compiled; deliberate tampering with the toolchain or other processes is not contained.
- Lean audit programs target Lean 4.35 APIs; toolchains they do not compile on report Not verified; kernel re-checking large dependencies (Mathlib) is slow: the first check of a file that imports Mathlib takes a minute or more.
- Mathlib: a setup needs network access (GitHub, `cache.mathlib.org`) and several GB of disk in the package's `.lake`; it runs Lake, the package's Lake configuration, and downloaded programs natively with the user's privileges (no sandbox). One Lean/Mathlib pair is tested (Lean `v4.35.0`, Mathlib `v4.35.0`), and the downloading path is covered only by the opt-in scenarios `mathlib-setup` and `mathlib` (§15). A new package can only pin an installed Lean release (or the folder's own `lean-toolchain`) with the Mathlib tag of the same name; nightlies and custom toolchains are refused, and an existing package whose `lean-toolchain` names a channel (`stable`) is refused until it names one release. A Mathlib fork fetched from a git address is prepared under a "Not official" warning; no fork setup has been run against the network, its prebuilt cache may be incomplete (the setup then fails at its last step, and building from source is offered once the sources are downloaded), and its code runs natively. A local copy is refused by the plan and, like a mirror, is prepared by hand; the other dependencies' addresses are shown for review but not restricted. There is no upgrade flow (a package with a manifest is never updated), no editing of an existing lakefile (Mathlib is not added to a package that does not require it), no mirror or offline-cache setting, and no setup for dependencies other than through the package's own lakefile. A failed or canceled setup of an existing package whose `.lake` already existed keeps completed downloads and leaves the package marked unfinished until a setup succeeds (building from source stays possible only when the dependency sources were complete before that setup); cleanup keeps files added or changed in the folders the setup made, but removes a `.lake` or manifest the setup created whole, whatever was put inside meanwhile; a toolchain the setup installed stays installed. A package whose Mathlib is made for another Lean version is not checked and must be fixed by hand. Lean does not start on an unprepared Mathlib package at all (no partial checking); "Build from source instead…" exists only for a missing cache and lasts until the app closes. A lakefile is read, never run: a `lakefile.lean` that requires Mathlib in a way the reading does not recognize is held back before a manifest exists only if its text mentions Mathlib outside comments, and then without Exposition knowing whether Mathlib is required. Other packages that do not use Mathlib are not gated, so their missing dependencies are still fetched by Lake when the server starts. Setup is verified on Windows only.
- Lean goals are plain text; third-party infoview widgets are not executed.
- Agents: two providers (Anthropic Messages API, OpenAI Responses API with an API key; the ChatGPT-plan sign-in is not offered, its eligibility for Exposition is not established). Provider requests, catalogs, and attachments are verified against local mocks of both APIs; live behaviour is covered only by the opt-in `live-agents`, `live-attachments`, `live-restricted`, `live-graph`, `live-workflow`, `live-review-m8-agent`, and `live-m9-agents` scenarios (spend credit).
- Trusted native code (kernels, approved commands, Python, LaTeX, Lean) runs as the user and can read the stored API key. It can also change any file the account can write, including app settings such as `project-permissions.json` and `trust.json`; Exposition cannot confirm each action inside native code, whether it was approved once, for a task, or under Auto.
- Code an agent writes into ordinary project files (a script, a module) runs when the user later runs or imports it; it is a reviewable change like imported code, not an execution the gate sees.
- Auto and allowances (A1): any change of a project's permissions (also a widening) or of a task's allowances while a call waits between its decision or approval and its run refuses that call (`agents.permissionsChanged`); the result tells the model to call the tool again. Narrowing or revoking waits at most 10 s in all for the affected Auto tasks to stop, and the Settings button stays busy meanwhile. A project replaced at the same path by a copy with the same graph identity keeps the record (no file identity is persisted). `read_file` of a `.lean` file in a trusted folder opens it, so Lean elaborates the file's existing text, as when the user opens it. Text the user typed into a Lean buffer is elaborated once the folder is trusted (agent text is gated in any folder, rule 6b).
- Rendered responses: relative links (e.g. to project files) stay inert text, with no open-in-editor; KaTeX `\color` is allowed and can lower contrast; Lean hovers use Monaco's own, separate Markdown renderer.
- Restricted execution needs Docker with a locally present image providing `/bin/sh` (`sleep` for the in-container time limit); no pull, no native fallback.
- the startup container sweep runs only when an image is configured; a sweep failure is only printed to stderr (the first restricted job retries).
- the liveness pipe covers batch containers only; interactive restricted containers would rely on the time limit alone (the app starts none).
- Processes created through system services (WMI, Task Scheduler, COM) can outlive a job.
- Path resolution and the following file operation are separate steps (TOCTOU against hostile local processes).
- Files with mixed line endings open read-only until normalized.
- Binary, legacy-encoded (not UTF-8 / UTF-16 with BOM) and >10 MiB files are not opened as text.
- Editor links are never opened; they are copied to the clipboard. Links in agent responses open only in the system browser, after a click.
- Project graph extraction never expands macros or evaluates code: LaTeX theorem-like environments count only with standard names or a literal `\newtheorem`/`\declaretheorem` earlier in the same file (not one in another file or a package; a theorem-like environment used without such a definition makes the file's coverage partial, naming the environment and the input whose definitions are not read; a macro defined in another file that hides a theorem environment is reported only in its defining file); Typst recognises the default theorem function names and ctheorems (`thmbox`/`thmplain`/`thmproof`) definitions in the same file only, and links no proofs; Markdown only Pandoc fenced divs with the default class names; remarks and examples are not entities. What is not interpreted is reported as partial coverage.
- Lean declarations generated by macros, elaborators, or notation are not recognised, and Lean use-site references are not extracted. The Lean audit reports no declaration dependencies, so formal dependency edges are unavailable and formal evidence is never current against imported modules (freshness unknown, then stale after the file, or for a Lake package its `lean-toolchain` or `lake-manifest.json`, changes). The recorded identity is the Lean environment (toolchain, `lean --version`, `lean-toolchain` digest), the manifest's digest, and the Mathlib revision it pins, not fingerprints of the declarations a proof uses: a change inside a dependency checkout that leaves the manifest unchanged is not detected. Formal evidence exists only where the user recorded it (Record evidence, Associate with…, an accepted workflow association) and only on the formal declaration; an informal statement shows it by association, never as its own. Impact of a file lists the items located in it without following their dependents.
- Scripts contribute only literal imports of project files; data files a script reads at run time are not recorded (research pins them only when declared as experiment inputs). Notebooks (`.ipynb`), datasets, assets, and configuration files are known only as resources (coverage unsupported).
- *code*: `.ltx` files are not indexed (the LaTeX extractor accepts them, the admission policy does not).
- A reconciliation scan covers at most 32 folder levels, 200,000 folder entries, and 20,000 files; files beyond a bound stay as they were (unknown, not removed). *code*: the bound reached is reported in `graph_reconcile`'s result only, not in the UI.
- A rename or move keeps its identity only when the old and new paths are seen in the same reconciliation pass and match uniquely; otherwise the new path gets a new identity and the old one is tombstoned. Tombstones nothing refers to are removed only after a permanent file deletion (under those paths) or when the user purges "Disposable records" in Storage & History; reconciliation itself never removes them.
- Evidence freshness is computed against the last reconciled content of each file; while files are pending, the Graph page says answers may not include the latest edits.
- A folder that cannot hold `.exposition/graph.sqlite3` (read-only) gets a temporary in-memory graph: nothing curated persists, and the Graph page offers no changes or saved layouts.
- Graph canvas: edges are straight lines (parallel ones bowed) and can pass behind other nodes; there is no edge routing; one saved layout per section, with no UI to create, rename, or delete views (saved views can be deleted and duplicated only as `graphView` targets over IPC).
- *code*: "Reindex project" (`graph_reconcile`), manual backups and the backup list (`graph_backup`, `graph_backups`), and the operation list (`graph_operations`) are debug-only commands with no UI. A release build therefore has no way to force a full re-read of the project or to take a backup on request: reconciliation runs by itself (file events, open, full pass on a rescan), backups are the automatic and pre-migration ones (§7.1), and backups are restored from the unavailable-graph recovery choices.
- A rejection of a relationship is forgotten once the source has stated that relationship: the rejected record becomes the observed one, and when the source later stops stating it the relation is removed like any observed one, so an agent can suggest it again.
- Impact's cycle report follows each item once, breadth-first: a cycle that is reachable only through an item already reached by another path, or from another root, may not be listed.
- Graph views: a layout save that is still in flight when the same project's views are loaded again can be answered with "changed elsewhere" on the next edit, which then drops that edit; a first save of a new view that is in flight during a project switch is not bound to the project it was made in.
- PDF tab (§3.7): pictures of pages only — no text selection, search, copying, links, or forms; files up to 64 MiB, the first 2,000 pages, at most 16 MP per page (lower resolution beyond); a page being drawn cannot be interrupted and has no time limit. Parser and rasterizer panics are caught; whether the renderer bounds the memory an embedded image of very large declared size needs was not established, and running out of memory is not a panic. Not exercised by the real-app scenario (`pdf-viewer`): a PDF with text and fonts, and a PDF exported by Exposition.
- Agents: a Continue sent in the moment after a task's final status is announced can be refused with `agents.taskFinishing` ("The task is finishing up; try again in a moment.") while the finished run still releases its services; nothing announces the end of that moment. While a Continue is being admitted, a delete or review action on the same task can be refused as still running.
- Workflow presets exist for Python and Lean only (an R or Julia computational study gets no draft); R and Julia steps are added to a workflow by hand (or by an agent). Restricted R and Julia batches need their own configured local image (no default; the Python image is never used), which must provide `Rscript` / `julia` and every package the code uses; they record no interpreter version or package list, only the image identity.
- At most 3 workflow runs are active at once and one per workflow; within a run at most 4 steps run at the same time (the workflow's `maxParallel`, default 1), under fixed application-wide limits (4 in flight: 2 restricted, 2 native, 2 agent, 1 Lean). There are no retries and no open-ended loops: conditions are fixed predicates, for-each takes at most 20 values, until at most 10 iterations. A run's budgets are derived from its plan and cannot be edited; the time budget is wall-clock time, not reserved per step.
- *code*: a step waiting for a slot or for its budget reservation stays `pending` (there is no "waiting" state) and holds back the later steps of its run; slot waiting counts against the run's time budget, and there is no ordering between runs waiting for the same slot.
- JSON conditions compare numbers exactly as decimals, but a number in the JSON document that has a fraction or lies outside the 64-bit integer range is first read as a double-precision value; a JSON-compare condition can be set on any dataset output, and a dataset that is not JSON fails the step at run time.
- Batch parameter values reach the code as environment variables (`EXPOSITION_PARAM_<NAME>`, one line, ≤ 4096 bytes; `value` ≤ 1000 bytes); a value that looks like a secret is refused only when the batch is about to run (validation accepts it, and it is stored in the saved definition). `EXPOSITION_ITERATION` is set only for repeated steps. Seeds are distinct per instance by hashing, not guaranteed unique.
- A step skipped because a condition did not hold does not fail the run, even when it would have produced a required outcome (the outcome reads Not produced with the reason).
- A native Python step's recorded packages are only the nine that research discovery checks (R: the runtime and IRkernel; Julia: the runtime); a restricted Python image's package list is captured once per image identity by an extra short container run before the first batch in that image (limited to 30 s), and a failed probe is retried by every later batch in that image. Reproduction drift compares the configured model; the served model is recorded beside it.
- Workflow triggers: one per workflow (enabling another replaces it, as the dialog says); a runner briefly unavailable when a trigger checks (for example Docker not running) suspends it until re-approved; revoking trust suspends every trigger of the folder, including those without native steps; triggers listen only while their project is open and indexed and never catch up; triggered runs that are rate limited wait rather than being dropped; the quiet period is shown and checked for interval triggers too, although it only paces their suspension checks; a change of the model provider or model of a workflow's agent review steps suspends its trigger like any other change of the standing plan (§7.8).
- Workflow bundle import is not one transaction: a storage failure part-way leaves the children already saved as imported drafts. A single definition imported with composite references keeps them as written.
- An interrupted run is never resumed or repeated; a step that was dispatched when Exposition stopped has an unknown outcome and may have partly run.
- Restricted Python steps need Docker with a locally present image that provides `python3` and every package the code imports (no pull, no network in the container). Only regular files directly in the output folder with one of 11 exported types (PNG, JPEG, GIF, SVG, PDF, CSV, TSV, JSON, TXT, HTML) are kept: at most 32, 64 MiB each, 512 MiB per batch.
- *code*: a batch's output area is on the host disk and is measured about every 0.5 s; the batch is stopped once it holds more than min(output budget, 512 MiB) + 64 MiB.
- Trusted native Python steps and Lean audit steps run with the user's account privileges and are not contained. Each native batch is a new Python process, not a kernel session.
- The Lean audit step allows at most 600 s (the Lean service's maximum for one check); it audits the file's open document, so unsaved changes that differ from the plan's hash fail the step (`workflow.inputChanged`).
- The approval of a run binds, for a Lean audit step, the audited file only. The Lean toolchain, the Lake manifest and lakefile, and the project's other Lean files that the audited file imports are not shown in the plan and are not in the approval hash; they are recorded with the attempt. Whether they should be bound is undecided.
- A final run record the history store never accepts in this session is kept in memory only (§7.7 Final record); if Exposition closes first, the run reads Interrupted afterwards although it ended.
- A workflow promotion is always prepared at the end of the manuscript. Prepared reviews are held in memory for 30 minutes; after a restart a pending one reads Expired and the run must be repeated to prepare it again.
- Interactive research sessions run trusted native; restricted research execution exists only as the workflow Python, R, and Julia batch steps.
- *code*: workflow canvas positions are kept in browser storage per workflow, not in the project or the definition.
- An agent review answer is kept up to 65,536 characters; a longer one is cut to that length, ending in "…".
- Project creation declares required tools but never installs them; a project is created only in a new or empty folder.
- Project operations (P1): no cut/move, no drag-and-drop inside the application, and no whole-project cloning (a file or folder is renamed with the explorer's Rename, and files dropped from the operating system are imported as copies, §3.7); Copy and Paste of files between applications and Undo of a deletion work on Windows only (macOS: Delete moves to the Trash with no Undo, and restore is unsupported; Linux: implemented, unverified; the file clipboard is Windows-only, elsewhere copies paste within Exposition). Undo needs a reliable match in the Recycle Bin (one new item with that original path and deletion time) and expires with the journal's limits (≤ 500 restore records and ≤ 200 journal records per project); otherwise the result says to restore from the Recycle Bin. A drive without a Recycle Bin is covered only by a simulated bin.
- The text of a deleted file kept in the editor (`preserve`) is no longer journaled for crash recovery; Save a Copy… is how it reaches disk. Copies are made from saved bytes only.
- Graph items extracted from a source (theorems, proofs, Lean declarations, references) are never deleted from the graph alone and never duplicated: Delete from Source (next item) removes their text as one unsaved edit, Delete Permanently is refused, and Copy Reference / Copy Text are offered instead of a duplicate. A deleted relationship stays deleted across reindexing because reindexing never re-creates curated relationships (no suppression record is kept).
- Delete from Source (§8.6): verified in the real app for LaTeX theorem/lemma/proof environments, a BibTeX entry, Lean declarations and a labelled Typst theorem (`p1-source-removal`); Markdown declarations follow the same rules but are covered by unit tests of the shared locator only. A Typst markup call is removed together with the label that follows it (across whitespace, blank lines or a comment, which stays), in the same undoable edit, so references to it become undefined; LaTeX and Markdown labels sit inside the construct, and a LaTeX `\label` after `\end{…}` is not the theorem's and stays. Lean dependents are literal name mentions (no `open`/namespace resolution: a list to review, which may include another declaration with the same short name or miss a use through notation); LaTeX dependents are the graph's relations as of the last save (a `\ref` added in an unsaved buffer is not listed). An unnamed construct (a proof) edited inside since the last save, or two identical ones near unsaved edits, is not located (`ops.sourceChanged`). The graph keeps showing the item until the file is saved. Sections are not graph entities, so they are not removed this way.
- Disk space: the project graph database is not compacted after purges (its file does not shrink; the freed space is reused, and graph backups may still hold purged records until they rotate out); deleted run outputs and research records stay on disk until purged; the run history and task databases are compacted after permanent deletions; deletion is not secure erasure.
- Stopping work for a deletion (`activeWork: stop`) happens before the files change and cannot be undone if the deletion then fails (the option says so); triggers turned off for it are turned back on. TeX/Typst builds are not detected as work that uses a file.
- *code*: the triggers turned off for a deletion are remembered in memory only; if Exposition stops before the deletion's outcome is known, they stay off. A run history written by a newer version is left untouched and the session's runs are kept in memory only; this is logged, not shown in the interface.
- External pastes record no copied-from provenance; the backend cannot tell whether an absolute paste source came from the clipboard or a dialog (both are explicit user input).

## Verification

The rows marked "(770b78f)" were recounted at `770b78f` (`qc/integration`); the other rows keep the commit they were last read at and were not recounted then. Counts were read from the sources at `b2bef49` (`m7/integration`), re-read at `25ae702` (`main`, M8) for the rows marked "(25ae702)", and re-read at `f29ce7a` (`main`, M9 final: lanes A–F, the docs-audit and R-m9 fix rounds) for the rows marked "(f29ce7a)", and re-read at `ebe1130` (`main`, P1: lanes L1 files, L2 data incl. the `runOutputs` target and the docs-audit fix rounds, L3 ui; interface fixes of `64ab7c1` read on `p1/ui`) for the rows marked "(ebe1130)", and re-read at `bc4800c` (`main`, A1: lanes R responses, P permissions, E execution, and the integration fixes) for the rows marked "(bc4800c)"; unmarked rows are in files M8, M9, P1, and A1 did not change. The A1 facts were read from the code (`crates/exposition-agents/src/{permissions,model,store,service,prompt}.rs`, `tools/{exec,mod,execution,services}.rs`, `crates/exposition-research/src/{setup,selection,discovery,types,service,launcher}.rs`, `src-tauri/src/{app,agents,setup,research}.rs`, `src-tauri/build.rs`, `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json`, `src/ui/components/Markdown*`, `src/features/agents/`, `src/features/research/`, `src/features/settings/{AgentsSection,ResearchSection}.tsx`, `src/features/workflows/{RunView.tsx,runAgain.ts,PlanReview.tsx}`, the `a1-*` e2e scenarios); nothing was built or run for them. Gate rules 6b (Lean/Lake configuration) and 6c (protected folders) were added after `bc4800c` by the R-a1 F1 fix (`18dd709`). Nothing was built or run for this inventory: the P1 facts were read from the code (`crates/exposition-core/src/ops.rs`, `crates/exposition-workspace/src/ops/`, `crates/exposition-workspace/src/service.rs`, `src-tauri/src/{project_ops,project_data,clipboard,workspace}.rs`, `crates/exposition-workflow/src/{project_data,run_records,store}.rs`, `crates/exposition-graph/src/{project_ops,store}.rs`, `crates/exposition-research/src/records.rs`, `crates/exposition-agents/src/{tools,review.rs,store.rs,service.rs}`, `src/features/{operations,workspace,research,graph,workflows,agents,settings,editor}/`, `src/app/{registry,keybindings}.ts`, the `p1-*` e2e scenarios), and the M9 facts likewise (`crates/exposition-graph/src/workflow{,/v2,/interchange,/registry}.rs`, `crates/exposition-workflow/src/{plan,runner,slots,service,condition,store,environment,manifest,reproduce}.rs`, `adapters/{batch,rj}.rs`, and `triggers/`, `crates/exposition-research/src/{batch.rs,sandbox/{mod,language,stage,image_env}.rs}`, `crates/exposition-execution/src/settings.rs`, `src-tauri/src/{workflows,workflow_repro,workflow_triggers,settings}.rs`, `src/features/{workflows,settings}/`, the M9 e2e scenarios). The acceptance runs below are the M7 and M6 runs and predate M8.

**P1 final verification:** Integrated on main `ac7798c` (scenario fix `ac6bcda`), Windows 11, 2026-11-14: `npm run check` 9/9 PASS (Rust 1788 passed / 0 failed / 39 ignored; vitest 488; clippy `-D warnings`; rustfmt; bindings; private paths; permissions); `npm run build:app`; default e2e with Docker required: 56/57 PASS on the first run — the one failure, `p1-storage`, was the scenario's own ambiguous locator (the task appears in both the Agents side panel and the page), fixed in `ac6bcda`, then 2/2 PASS on the same build — so 57/57. The seven `p1-*` scenarios ran against the real app with no product defects: real Recycle Bin (read back through Shell.Application), the real `CF_HDROP` clipboard both ways, a junction, a locked file (partial failure), a real Docker batch stopped before deleting its input, an enabled trigger turned off, a pinned revision purged, kill-after-delete reconciliation and Undo after a restart. Live provider scenarios were not rerun for P1 (P1 adds no model-facing behavior beyond the agent delete/copy tools, covered by central-gate and task-loop tests). Independent review: scheduled as the Codex review after the UI rework (spec sequence). Lane evidence recorded during P1 (not a substitute for the final run): the file engine's real-OS tests on Windows (Recycle Bin delete and restore of files and folders, restore into an occupied path, a locked file, access denied, a junction trashed and never followed, read-only entries, eight concurrent pastes; the `CF_HDROP` round trip is an ignored test run on request) and restore tokens surviving a restart with the real Recycle Bin.

**M9 final verification (main `706410a` product code; scenario fixes 3773b2f, 3ae1ccf):** `npm run check` 9/9 PASS (Rust 1646 passed / 0 failed / 38 ignored; vitest 409, 411 with the e2e helper tests); `npm run build:app`; default e2e with Docker required 49/50 PASS, the one failure an e2e-harness false positive in `m6-paper-project` (a process whose dead parent's id Windows had reused for the app; fixed in the harness, then 2/2 PASS on the same build); `review-m9` 24 ok / 0 DEFECT; Docker-backed crate tests run by the lanes (restricted Python, R and Julia batches, two-branch overlap, image probe) and real Lean/native R/Julia tests; live against the real provider (claude-opus-5-5, debug build): `live-workflow`, `live-graph`, `live-agents`, `live-restricted`, `live-review-m8-agent`, and `live-m9-agents` all PASS. Not exercised in the running app: interval triggers (minimum 15 minutes; covered by unit tests with a simulated clock).

**M7 acceptance run (main `3eb64a8`, 2026-11-12, Windows 11 x64).** Debug app at `bef5674`/`68cb2ff`: `npm run check` gates all pass (1261 Rust tests, 11 ignored; 323 frontend tests); default e2e 40/40 including `m6-paper-project` 16/16 with Docker; reviewer scenarios by name 8/8 (`review-m7-ipc`, `review-m7-graph-fs`, `review-sec-*`, `review-m5-agents`; 0 defects); real-Lean `review_m7_lean_identity`. Live provider (claude-opus-5-5): `live-graph` 16/16, `live-agents` 20/20, `live-restricted` 19/19 — the first live run found every task rejected by the provider ("The compiled grammar is too large") with the graph tools strict, fixed in `3eb64a8`. Release: NSIS installer 29.01 MiB (sha256 `d95b1cefc095fb15dc0adaa139d6d30e84bc9ed8cb4a1b9db9c86359856dd6b5`); silent per-user install in 3.2 s without elevation (HKCU uninstall entry, Start Menu shortcut). Against the installed app: 30 of 36 scenarios pass; the 6 that fail need development-only commands or the scripted provider, refused by the release by design (`agents-graph`, `execution-jobs`, `project-create`, `research-workspace` after its 28 checks, `review-sec-render`, `settings`); key-touching scenarios were not run against the release. Installed `m6-paper-project`: every step passes except the two that need an agent (draft; unattended Docker run), covered by the live scenarios against the installed app: `live-graph` 16/16, `live-restricted` 19/19, `live-agents` 20/20. Silent uninstall (1.2 s) removes the program folder, the uninstall entry and the shortcut; Tauri's `HKCU\Software\exposition\Exposition` (install path) is left, as before, and the saved key remains a single local entry.

**Final acceptance run (main `c8fdca6`, 2026-11-12, Windows 11 x64).** `npm run check` 9/9 (1033 Rust tests, 9 ignored; 231 frontend tests). Debug app: default e2e 24/24 including `m6-paper-project` 16/16; reviewer scenarios by name all pass with no defect (`review-m5-agents`, `review-sec-*`, the M2/M3 reviewer's seven scenarios at `f6404c2`). Release: NSIS installer 26.63 MiB; silent per-user install (`/S /D=…`) in 3.2 s without elevation. Against the installed app: 23 of 26 scenarios pass; the 3 that fail need development-only commands or the scripted provider, which the release refuses by design (`execution-jobs`, `settings`, `review-sec-render`). Scenarios that save or remove a key are never run against a release build (it always uses the real credential entry). Installed `m6-paper-project`: every step passes except the two that need an agent (draft; unattended Docker run), which the live scenarios cover against the installed app with the real provider: `live-restricted` 19/19 (at `c8fdca6`) and `live-agents` 20/20 (at `f6404c2`). Silent uninstall removes the program folder, the uninstall entry and the Start Menu shortcut; Tauri's uninstaller keeps `HKCU\Software\exposition\Exposition` (the install path) and the app data unless its "delete app data" option is chosen.
